- [x] ~~Revisit the detour orientation convention~~ — **settled in Phase 120c**: drags take a constant half-plane and a round trip carries honest monodromy (PLAN §"A round trip is honest"). Was deferred from 117 on a user decision of 2026-08-14 and reopened by the user on 2026-08-17
- [x] Unify the last convention non-uniformity it left: **locus** runs used `detourOrientation1D` (odd in the drive direction) while drags used the constant. Unified onto one `detourHalfPlane` (renamed from `dragDetourOrientation`, which is no longer drag-only; `detourOrientation1D` deleted), and the fixture diffs are **nil, bitwise** — measured on all four locus documents, of which only `apatitos-topos.rgl` detours at all, twice, both on forward legs, which is exactly where the two rules disagreed. The reason generalizes and is now recorded (PLAN, same section): an arc encircles its one estimated singularity by a half-turn whichever side it passes, and a half-turn either way exchanges the two sheets of a simple branch point — so the half-plane can only matter when an arc encloses more than one collision, or one of higher order, where the arc is mis-planned and neither rule is right. New coverage pins that the Phase 117b rig *detours* (two arcs, no folds), so the sheet assertions over it are known to exercise one
- [x] `construction.dart` carried a **literal NUL byte** in a composite map key (`'${curve1.id}\x00${curve2.id}'`, the Phase 120c per-pair adoption group), which made `file(1)` call it `data` and `grep`/`rg` skip the whole file as binary — silently, so a search for anything in the largest domain file came back empty. Escaped; same string value, and the file is text again
- [x] `locus.dart`'s index-flip consistency guard assumed two candidates (`old.length != 2 || now.length != 2 → skip`), so a conic∩conic pair inside a locus chain silently lost that check rather than crashing. **Widened**, and moved to `trace_acceptance.dart` as `relabelIsBenign` where it is directly testable: a flip is a benign canonical relabel exactly when every root the slot did *not* follow pairs with a distinct other root across the flip within the same motion allowance. Nearest-match is exact rather than greedy here because the cap is at most half the set's minimum pairwise separation, so no pairing is ambiguous; at two candidates the general form reduces to the old `1 - index` arithmetic exactly. Unequal candidate counts still impose nothing — a root has entered or left the real set and the walk's other rules own that
- [x] Delete `lib/domain/math/intersections.dart` — **moved** to `test/v1_oracle/intersections.dart` rather than deleted, and with it the three other V1 modules that turned out to be in the same state (`angle_bisector`, `circle_relations`, `harmonic`, `tangents`: no `lib/` importer since Phase 110 superseded each with a projective kernel). Deleting them would have deleted the *evidence* that the projective kernels still agree with V1 on real transverse cases, which is the contract `branchIndex` addresses and is permanent because v1 documents are. Six test files consume them as oracles. Living under `test/` turns the standing rule from a grep gate into the directory layout, and `layer_rule_test.dart` gained a gate that no shipped file reaches into `test/`
- [x] Demote `LineEq`/`CircleEq` to view structs (remove throwing construction paths from domain flows) — already true in code and now stated where it is read: nothing constructs either outside `lib/domain/`, and inside it only through `toLineEq`/`toCircleEq` or behind a guard, so the throwing factories are a programmer-error contract rather than a path a recompute can fall down. The one exception was `InscribedCircle`, which passed `inradius(...)!` straight into `CircleEq` on the strength of two helpers sharing a collinearity test; it now checks. Recorded in both type headers and CLAUDE.md, whose "legacy affine math (frozen; shrinking)" row was no longer true of what is left in `lib/domain/math/`
- [x] One degeneracy convention everywhere: projective value total; projection nullable — audited across all 44 kinds and found already uniform (`null` only when a parent's projective value is null, or when the computed homogeneous value is the **zero triple**, which is not a projective object). Stated at the declaration site in `geo_object.dart` with its three sanctioned exceptions named (`IntersectionPoint`'s realness gate, `PointOnObject` without a chart, the Phase 112 consumer kinds), and given a gate: collapsing every free point in the two kitchen-sink corpora onto one position produces all 44 degeneracies at once, and every surviving affine view must still have an agreeing projective value (29 do — a degeneracy is mostly a value, not an absence)
- [x] Remove lift-default dead code where every kind now overrides — `GeoPoint.projPoint`, `GeoLine.projLine` and `GeoCircle.conic` are **abstract**. All 44 kinds already implemented them (eight only through a migrated intermediate base — `TriangleCenterPoint`, `RelativeLine`, `TriangleCircle` — which is why a per-file grep undercounts), as did every test stub: the change is analyze-clean and suite-green with nothing else touched. The defaults were right during the migration and are a trap after it, because a kind that forgot to store homogeneous state would silently inherit a lift of its affine value instead of failing to compile. The `lift` constructors themselves stay: they are the chart → homogeneous kernel API, not scaffolding
- [x] `point_coincidence.dart` re-pointed at projected positions — **it needed no code change, which is the finding**: the probe only reads `GeoPoint.position` and writes `FreePoint` positions and `PointOnObject` parameters, all of which are projections and chart parameters now, so the perturbation still perturbs exactly the mutable roots and the restore is still bit-exact (a `FreePoint` stores the lift of its position, `w` exactly 1). Two consequences are deliberate and now stated in the file and pinned by a test: an off-chart point (at infinity, or complex) has no position and is never merged — two pairs of parallels meeting at the *same* projective point stay two points, the conservative branch this function takes everywhere — and the tolerance stays in world units, because it screens "one dot or two", a question about the chart and not a projective invariant
- [x] Grep gates recorded in CLAUDE.md; PLAN's migration-contract section replaced by the final architecture description. PLAN §"Migration strategy" is now §"Architecture" — same five numbered points, rewritten as what the migration arrived at, with the rejected alternatives kept so they are not re-litigated. Two of the gates stopped being greps and became structure: `test/v1_oracle/` cannot be imported from `lib/` (and `layer_rule_test.dart` checks it), and the projective accessors are abstract, so a kind cannot forget its homogeneous state. Migration-era wording ("migrated kind", "unmigrated objects") retired from CLAUDE.md and `geo_object.dart`

## Phase 122 — Performance hardening + compile-target finalization

- [x] **Measure the hot loop before rewriting it — it is not the tracing slot** (PLAN §"The hot loop was not where the phase said it was"). On a traced `IntersectionPoint.recompute`, `TracedBranch.follow` is 43 ns of 3044 — **1.4%** — with `checkpoint` at 11 ns and `restore` at 6. The remaining ~98% is `intersectionCandidates`. So the SoA item below is re-pointed: it can only ever have been worth a percent and a half of a drag frame, and the phase's budget belongs where the time is
- [x] **Two circles need no cubic.** `intersectionCandidates` on two circles cost 8× line∩conic, because it ran the general four-root pencil (balance → cubic → clustering → member scoring → adjugate split) and then discarded I and J — which it had just recovered numerically and which are known. A conic is a circle exactly when `xy = 0`, `xx = yy`, and for two of those `b.xx·A − a.xx·B` kills the quadratic block, leaving `w·(2Dx + 2Ey + Fw)`: the degenerate member in closed form, ℓ∞ together with the radical axis. Steps 2–4 collapse to two subtractions; the Newton polish goes with them (it cleans up a member found at an inexact root, and there is none). The centroid balance **stays** — free for circles, and measured load-bearing at 1e6 offset (2e-5 unbalanced vs 1.3e-10). Measured on `locus-miss-2.json`: 5.85 → 3.47 µs/solve VM, 8.53 → 3.77 AOT, 8.28 → 4.37 dart2js, 8.65 → 3.98 dart2wasm, with **identical solve counts** and `apatitos-topos.rgl` (line∩circle) unchanged as the control
- [x] Tests: the circular points come back at exactly `w = 0` — the discriminator that says the short route was *taken*, the general one leaving them at `|w| ~ 1e-33`; both routes answered index for index over 400 random pairs, the general one reached by nudging a conic 1e-30 off the circle shape, with both the real and the conjugate regimes covered (they order by different rules); the centroid balance load-bearing at 1e6; complex carriers — what a detour hands it — incident on both conics; concentric circles meeting only at I and J, doubled. The pre-existing V1-agreement and complex-rescaling properties now run over the short route, which is what pins the branch orderings
- [x] **Canonical ordering was half of what was left, mostly a tie-break nobody reached.** With the cubic gone, `_canonicalOrder` cost 505 ns of the surviving 1080 — not in recovering the two centres (handing those in, free for a circle, is worth 56 ns) but in building four `_OrderKey`s eagerly, each with its lexicographic tie-break list and a `normalized` behind it. A circle pair gives two real points and then I and J, where tier and primary always decide, so all four were built and discarded every solve. Lazy now; same rule, same comparisons. `intersectConicConic` on two circles: 2676 → 947 ns
- [x] `benchmark/chain_solve_bench.dart` + `run_chain_solve.sh` — the breakdown itself, kept so the split is re-measurable on every target rather than re-derived
- [x] **SoA re-measured against the new profile and closed as not worth doing** (PLAN, same section) — a measurement, not a reversal. Phase 101's numbers stand: unboxed SoA beats boxed `Complex` by 1.30–1.78× *on the arithmetic*. Applied to a tracing slot that is **3–6%** of a traced recompute (VM 4.1%, AOT 5.7%, dart2js 6.1%, dart2wasm 3.0%), the ceiling is 1–2.5% of a drag frame — under the noise of the gate it would be measured on. The prior was sound when formed; what it could not know was how small the arithmetic-heavy slot would be next to the graph recompute. Phase 101's real dividend, the storage shape for `TracedBranch`, is already in place. Unboxing the *kernel* (the 85–97%) is a different and much larger question, deliberately not opened: nothing is currently bottlenecked, and the algorithmic route already beat what representation promised, at a fraction of the risk
- [x] Benchmark suite runnable in CI (informational job) — `benchmark/run_ci.sh` (VM-only, dependency-free, ~1 min) behind `.github/workflows/benchmarks.yml`, on every push to main plus manual dispatch. Deliberately non-failing: a shared runner's absolute timings are not what the 8 ms budget is about, so it reports and the per-target scripts decide. Its value is catching an order-of-magnitude move and putting the trend in the log
- [x] **Final dart2js-vs-dart2wasm decision executed: wasm, Pages deploy switched** (PLAN, same section). dart2wasm wins raw complex arithmetic by 1.4–1.7× and is a wash on the whole engine (it takes `locus-miss-2` 3.69 vs 3.92 µs/solve and the drag gate 0.128 vs 0.140 ms, and *loses* `intersectionCandidates` on two circles, 2345 vs 2060 ns). What settles it is the payload: `--wasm` emits both, so a WasmGC browser fetches 2.4 MB of wasm instead of 2.8 MB of js and anything older falls back to exactly what it gets today. Smoke-tested in a browser and green (2026-08-18), on the first deploy
- [x] **Drag budget re-measured and recorded as the standing gate.** `tracing_bench.dart` now *throws* when a frame exceeds `dragFrameBudgetMs`, so a job can enforce it without parsing text (thrown, not `exit`ed: `dart:io` would cost the file its js and wasm targets, and an uncaught error is a non-zero exit on all four). Standing measurement across VM/AOT/dart2js/dart2wasm: **1–2%** of the 8 ms budget plain, **7–11%** carrying the 128-sample locus, checksums identical
- [x] Browser smoke of the wasm build, with the first deploy — **green 2026-08-18**. The remote exists, all three workflows run on every push to `main`, and the Pages deploy at `https://stevesuave.github.io/regula2/` boots and draws. Phase 122 is now fully closed

## Phase 123 — M-CK0: the absolute, and the audit that scopes the milestone

Opens the M-CK milestone (PLAN §"M-CK — Cayley–Klein"). The kernel track closed at 122; this is the first milestone phase. **Zero Euclidean behaviour change** is the phase's safety property — suite, 32 goldens and the tracing checksums all untouched — because it is what lets 124–126 be incremental.

- [x] `Absolute` in `lib/domain/projective/absolute.dart` — the fundamental conic as **both** matrices, point conic and dual, given explicitly per instance rather than derived (PLAN §"The absolute is carried as its dual"). The reason sharpened while building it: the Euclidean *dual* {I, J} is rank 2, but the *point conic* is the doubled line at infinity, **rank 1**, and the adjugate of a rank-1 matrix vanishes identically — so the dual cannot be derived, and the derivation fails harder than "not invertible" suggested. `ConicMatrix.applyToLine` added as `polarLine`'s dual-role counterpart (distinct from `poleOf`, which forms the adjugate first — on a dual already in hand that would be taking the dual twice)
- [x] **The audit — the phase's main deliverable** (PLAN §"The audit"). All 53 files classified: **50 concrete kinds, 3 abstract bases**, and Phase 121's "44" reconciled as these 50 minus the six measurement kinds, which carry no projective value. **38 of 50 are absolute-dependent** — 12 / 7 / 25 / 6 across the four tiers. The import census that scoped this phase said 25 and missed two whole routes: `proj_transform.dart` is a **fifth** metric kernel module (five transform kinds import none of the four censused ones), and the six measurement kinds import nothing metric *because they measure in the chart directly* — the Phase 112 boundary working as designed, and invisible to a census
- [x] **Structural result: the metric is one operation, not a scattering.** Read as projective constructions rather than formulas, most of tiers 2–3 are already the CK construction with the Euclidean absolute substituted — `perpendicularThrough` joins `P` to the *pole of ℓ*; `ReflectedPoint` is the harmonic homology (axis ℓ, centre its pole); `CentralReflectionPoint` is the harmonic homology (centre C, axis its polar, which is ℓ∞ here, which is why it currently reads as a homothety of −1); `Midpoint` is the harmonic conjugate of the absolute's points on the line. So tier 3 is largely *rewriting formulas as the pole/polar constructions they already are*. Five genuine re-foundings named for Phase 125: `Incenter`/`InscribedCircle` (route through `triangle_centers.dart`, which computes in `Vec2`), `RadicalAxisLine` (relies on the exact Euclidean circle coefficient shape), `CircleCenter` (`poleOf(ℓ∞)`; the CK centre is the pole of the chord of contact), `FixedRadiusCircle` (a **radius param** is a metric quantity and changes meaning), `BifocalConic` (already recorded in §"The conic constructors" as needing the chart)
- [x] **`branchIndex` addressing is relative to the absolute** — the milestone's sharpest constraint, and it was not in the outline. `intersectionCandidates` filters `isCircularPoint` on both the line∩conic and conic∩conic routes, and `_canonicalOrder` keys on the directed centre line, a centre being `poleOf(ℓ∞)`: both Euclidean. Per-document this is consistent (the absolute is fixed at load, so saved addresses mean what they meant), so nothing existing breaks — but **switching a live document's geometry is a re-addressing event**, not a display setting, and must go through a command that re-points by chordal match and reports, exactly like the Phase 120c decoder repair, or it reproduces that defect. Assigned to Phase 126
- [x] Threading route decided **on the audit's numbers** (PLAN §"The threading decision"): 38 of 50 is not an exceptional dependency but a near-universal one, and those belong in the signature — **`recompute()` takes the absolute as a parameter**, the twelve tier-1 kinds ignoring it. Keeps kinds pure functions of (parents, params, ambient) and lets one kind be asked the same question under two geometries with no global to set and unset. Back-reference (cycle in the model, a `Construction` forced into every stub test) and scoped ambient (the hidden global state the pure-domain rule exists to prevent, read from inside a traced pass where it is invisible) rejected with reasons recorded
- [x] `DocumentKernel` **moved** to `lib/domain/construction/` and carried past the codec onto `Construction`, which now holds one — the absolute is an input to every metric recompute, not a presentation setting, and the save format is where it is *written*, not where it belongs. The field is **final**: this build implements one geometry, the codec still refuses the other two, and a settable field is precisely how Phase 126's re-addressing command would get skipped. `encodeDocument` reads the kernel off the construction instead of taking it as a parameter, so a document cannot be written under an absolute other than the one its objects were computed in
- [x] Tests (18 new, suite **2415** green): the Euclidean `Absolute` reproduces the inlined kernel **component for component** over a spread that includes ℓ∞, an isotropic line and negative coefficients — `poleOf` = `normalDirectionOf`, `evaluateLine` = `a² + b²`, `join(p, poleOf(ℓ))` = `perpendicularThrough`; `polarOf` a finite point is ℓ∞ and a point at infinity the zero triple; Euclidean ranks 1 and 2 with the adjugate route shown vanishing; the two proper absolutes self-dual and agreeing with the adjugate; the unit circle and the imaginary conic pinned by which real points lie on them; projectivity under rescaling; totality on the zero triple; the kernel reaching the decoded `Construction`, not just the document record. Standing gates: analyze clean, **32 goldens byte-identical**, perf gate PASS at 1% / 7% of budget with unchanged checksums

## Phase 124 — M-CK1: measurement, and the half of it that is not a cross-ratio

PLAN §M-CK said the tier-4 kinds are "re-founded on cross-ratio, with Euclidean as the degenerate case that reproduces today's numbers". **Measured first: that is true of angle and false of distance**, which is the classical Cayley–Klein classification arriving as a code constraint — Euclidean has *elliptic angle measure* and *parabolic distance measure*.

- [x] **Angle unifies, at the bilinear-form level rather than through logs.** `cos θ = ⟨ℓ,m⟩*/√(⟨ℓ,ℓ⟩*·⟨m,m⟩*)` against the *dual* absolute is Laguerre's cross-ratio formula with the logs cleared, and under `diag(1,1,0)` it is `(a₁a₂+b₁b₂)/(|n₁||n₂|)` — the textbook Euclidean formula, verbatim. Measured: agrees with the chart route to **7.4e-13** over 20k random line pairs
- [x] **Distance does not unify, and cannot.** A line meets the Euclidean absolute (the *doubled* ℓ∞) in one point twice, and a cross-ratio with a repeated point is identically 1, so the log is identically 0 for every pair of points. Euclidean distance is not a cross-ratio at any constant — recovering it needs the constant to diverge as the absolute degenerates. So distance **dispatches on the measure** where angle substitutes: parabolic (Euclidean, the chart route it already uses), hyperbolic (`cosh d = |⟨P,Q⟩|/√(⟨P,P⟩⟨Q,Q⟩)`), elliptic (the same with `cos`). Verified against `artanh(r)` and against the cross-ratio route to the last digit
- [x] Thread the absolute into `recompute`, per Phase 123's decision — **defaulted to Euclidean while the migration runs**, which is the pattern Phases 106–121 already used for the projective accessors and then removed. Scheduled for removal at the end of Phase 125, when all 38 kinds take it; `Construction` passes `kernel.absolute` from the start, so the default is scaffolding at the declarations and never a live fallback
- [x] `Absolute` gains the bilinear pairings (`pairPoints`, `pairLines`) the quadratic forms of Phase 123 polarize to; `lib/domain/projective/ck_measure.dart` holds the two measures and names the three measure kinds
- [x] `VertexAngle` and `LineAngle`: the CK formula becomes the *definition*, with the exact Euclidean route retained as its specialization — the Phase 122 circle-fast-path rule, for the same reason (it is exact, cheaper, and keeps `expect(angle, 90)` reading exactly 90). Agreement proven by test rather than by routing. The marker's vertex and start direction stay chart quantities, like a measurement's anchor
- [x] `DistanceMeasurement` on the three measures. `LengthMeasurement` and `AreaMeasurement` are **integrals**, not substitutions (hyperbolic circumference is `2π·sinh r`, and a hyperbolic triangle's area is its angular defect) — deliberately **not** implemented here, and undefined rather than wrong under a proper absolute. `SlopeMeasurement` is Euclidean by definition and goes undefined with the others
- [x] Gates: **zero Euclidean behaviour change** — suite **2438** green, analyze clean, **32 goldens byte-identical**, perf gate PASS at 1% / 7% of budget with unchanged checksums. Every non-Euclidean path is reachable only from tests this phase, since the codec still refuses the two other metrics. Also added: `GeoAngle.measure` beside `angle`, because a marker is not a measure — the arc is still drawn between the same two chords while the number is the CK angle

## Phase 125 — M-CK2: the tier-2/3 substitutions

The bulk of the milestone: 32 kinds. Taken in the audit's order — the verbatim pole/polar generalizations first, then the genuine re-foundings, then the refusals. **Zero Euclidean behaviour change** throughout.

- [x] `euclidean.dart` → **`metric.dart`**, every function taking an `Absolute`. It was never only Euclidean; it was the Euclidean case of a family
- [x] **Midpoint and angle bisector are the same construction, dually** (PLAN §"Two constructions, one algebra"). A midpoint is the pair harmonic to `{P,Q}` and to the absolute's two points on `PQ`; parameterizing `X(t) = P + tQ`, harmonic to `{0,∞}` forces `M₋ = −M₊`, and harmonic to the absolute's roots forces `t² = ⟨P,P⟩/⟨Q,Q⟩` — so `M± = √⟨Q,Q⟩·P ± √⟨P,P⟩·Q`. Under Euclidean `⟨P,P⟩ = w²` and that reads `w_Q·P ± w_P·Q`: **the affine midpoint, plus the point at infinity in direction `P−Q`** — the external midpoint, whose sitting at infinity is why Euclidean seems to have one. The same algebra in the dual gives `B± = √⟨m,m⟩*·ℓ ± √⟨ℓ,ℓ⟩*·m`, which under `diag(1,1,0)` is exactly the √-unitization `twoLineBisectorOf` already had
- [x] Perpendicularity generalizes verbatim — `perpendicularThrough(P,ℓ) = P ∨ absolute.poleOf(ℓ)`, the audit's structural claim, now a test
- [x] `parallelThrough` **refused** under a proper absolute (zero triple), not approximated
- [x] Two cross-cutting fixes the tests found: **conjugacy is perpendicularity** in every CK geometry, so a vanishing pairing between two *non-isotropic* lines is a right angle whatever the self-pairings' signs (a line and its perpendicular can sit on opposite sides of the absolute, making the product under the root negative); and **every measure removes a global phase first**, because the CK constructions take a square root of a form that is negative inside the absolute and hand back real elements scaled by `i`. The normalization divides by the pivot's unit *phase*, not the pivot — it must be exact, since the perpendicularity test is an exact zero and rounding it to 2.7e-17 lost every right angle it touched
- [x] The eleven `metric.dart` consumer kinds wired, with the absolute threaded through the two template methods (`TriangleCenterPoint.computeCenter`, `RelativeLine.carrierFrom`) rather than each override reaching for it
- [x] **Transforms**: `ProjTransform.harmonicHomology` added, and it reproduces the shipped Euclidean reflection matrix **entry for entry** — the audit's claim that reflection *is* the homology about `(axis, its pole)`, checked rather than asserted; point reflection is the homology about `(centre, its polar)`, ℓ∞ under Euclidean, which is why it reads as a homothety of −1
- [x] **Circles** in `ck_circles.dart`, and they split the way distance splits (PLAN §"A circle follows its distance measure"). `d(C,X) = r` squares to `⟨C,X⟩² = k⟨C,C⟩⟨X,X⟩`, i.e. `K = ⟨R,R⟩(ΩC)(ΩC)ᵀ − ⟨C,R⟩²Ω` — the pencil through the absolute and the doubled polar of the centre, hence **bitangent**. Under Euclidean that whole pencil collapses to multiples of ℓ∞, so `circles.dart` is a *companion*, not a special case. A centre on the absolute is a **horocycle**, not a degeneracy. `Arc`/`Sector` refused: the carrier generalizes, the angular extent is a chart parameterization
- [x] The five re-foundings, and **three of them turned out to generalize** because their definitions are constructions rather than formulas: the centroid is the medians' common point (CK midpoints), the incentre the internal bisectors' (CK bisector), the inscribed circle centred there through the foot of a perpendicular. `FixedRadiusCircle` gets `ckCircleWithRadius`. Refused with reasons at the site: homothety and translation (a similarity that is not an isometry does not exist in CK — Wallis), rotation (needs an orthonormal frame in the pencil, whose form changes sign across it), Apollonius and bifocal conics, the radical axis, the focal conic, the circle centre (a generalized eigenproblem for the pencil `{K, Ω}`), segment-ratio points
- [x] `IntersectionPoint`'s circular-point filter made absolute-aware, and threaded **all at once** because `branchIndex` addresses the *filtered* order — a caller left on the default lands its points in a different address space, the Phase 120c failure from a new direction. One caller knowingly stays on the default and says so: the tool layer has no construction to read a kernel from, which is correct only while the codec refuses non-Euclidean documents. **Phase 126 must carry the kernel into the tools before lifting that refusal**
- [x] **The `recompute` default is kept, and that reverses Phase 124's schedule on purpose.** The Phase 121 precedent says a migration default inverts into a trap once the migration ends — but the trap it described was a kind *silently inheriting* a wrong value, and that cannot happen here: the parameter is supplied by `Construction` at every production call site, so the default only ever applies in tests that mean Euclidean. The risk that remains is a new kind whose `recompute` ignores the parameter, and a signature cannot catch that — only behaviour can. `ck_kind_coverage_test` does: over the corpus at three layouts, every kind must change its value or withdraw it. That is a strictly stronger gate than the one removing the default would buy, and it costs ~140 test call sites less churn
- [x] ~~**A coverage gate, not a proof.**~~ — the proof it was missing is `ck_isometry_invariance_test` (Phase 131), and the coverage gate's own blind spot is what it found: a kind with *one* of two ingredients substituted still moves, so `ck_kind_coverage_test` passed `Orthocenter` while one of its altitudes was Euclidean. Equivariance is not a substitute for per-kind correctness — a kind computing the wrong *equivariant* thing would pass — but it rules out the family of defects per-kind tests were being written one at a time to catch

## Phase 126 — M-CK3: the mode as a UI

The first phase of the milestone the user can *see*. Three phases of kernel work were reachable only from tests; this one opens the door, in the order the door has to open — **the tool layer first, the codec's refusal last**, because the refusal was standing in for the threading that was missing.

- [x] **The kernel carried into the tool layer**, per the note left at `nearestIntersectionBranch` in Phase 125. `ToolInput.absolute`, filled by the canvas from `Construction.kernel`, threaded through `resolvePoint`, `IntersectionTool`, `MidpointTool`, `MultiPointTool` and the macro tools; the `IntersectionPoint` factory takes it for the canonical remap alone, both orders' candidate lists being filtered against the absolute. Done *before* the codec change, not after: `branchIndex` addresses the filtered list, so a tool on the default in a hyperbolic document writes an address into the file naming a different crossing on reload
- [x] Two findings from that threading. **`coincidentExistingPoint` is worse than answering wrongly** — it perturbs every mutable root and recomputes the *whole construction*, so a defaulted absolute leaves the entire document computed Euclidean, silently, until the next mutation happens to recompute it. And **three `recompute()` calls in `locus.dart`** were still on the default (the static settle after a sweep, and both drive steps inside `_TracedSweep`, which had the absolute in hand throughout)
- [x] **The premise checked rather than asserted.** Two `FivePointConic`s through Euclidean-concyclic points that *miss* give two candidates under Euclidean and four under hyperbolic — and I and J sort *around* the complex pair, so address 0 is a crossing in one geometry and a circular point in the other. A five-point conic is tier 1, so the conics are bit-identical in both and the crossings do not move at all: only the numbering does
- [x] **`Construction.kernel` stops being `final` and does not become a setter.** The only route is `switchKernel`, which switches, recomputes, re-points by chordal match and returns the report — so no call switches without re-addressing (PLAN §"The switch is an operation, not a setting"). The general rule is worth keeping: when a mutation has an obligation attached, make the obligation the only route rather than a rule the caller must remember
- [x] `SetGeometryCommand` records what the switch decided and replays those addresses verbatim on undo and redo, because nearest-match is not invertible — running it backwards can land a point where it never was, and a point with no position at the switch was never matched in either direction. What cannot be matched is **reported**, not repaired: there is no evidence of what the user meant
- [x] **The codec's refusal turned into support.** `_decodeKernel` drops the second guard and keeps the first — an *unknown* metric name is still refused. Round-trip tests per metric, and the geometry comes back with the document (the CK midpoint, not the affine one). The version rule is untouched; a non-default kernel was already v2 and `test/fixtures/` stays v1
- [x] **Disc-boundary rendering.** `absoluteDisc` gives the hyperbolic unit circle with the region outside it washed, because that region is not part of the plane. Elliptic and Euclidean draw nothing, for two *different* reasons that are facts about the geometries rather than gaps — elliptic has no real absolute and no edge at all; Euclidean's is the line at infinity, real but in no chart. Centre and radius come from the viewport, exact under rotation because a circle stays a circle
- [x] **The mode as UI**: a geometry menu beside "Axes & grid", the switch routed through the command stack so it undoes like the edit it is, and the re-addressing report surfaced in a snack bar
- [x] **The mini-tutorial**, asked for directly and written where a user finds it (the menu's own guide): four things to build that look different in each geometry, plus the note that nothing in the toolbar changes — what changes is which theorems are true
- [x] **And the guide's first claim is a test rather than a promise.** A triangle's angle sum is exactly π in Euclidean, less in hyperbolic, more in elliptic; the hyperbolic defect grows with the triangle and vanishes in the small limit. Gauss–Bonnet from three independent `VertexAngle`s — a global fact that no angle measure wrong by a scale, a sign, a supplement or a chart artefact survives. The first payment on the debt Phase 125 recorded: its coverage gate showed each kind *responds* to the absolute, never that the response was right
- [x] ~~Still owed from the same debt: per-kind correctness beyond angle~~ — largely discharged by the **isometry-invariance gate** (Phase 131): every construction must satisfy `f(T·parents) = T·f(parents)` for an isometry `T` of the absolute, which rules out a wrong scale, sign, handedness or chart read in *any* kind at once. 25 kinds compared across both corpora, two absolutes, two centres, two angles. It found `Orthocenter` computing one of its two altitudes Euclidean on its first run
- [x] The load-time report for `repairedIntersections` — the decoder computes it and **nothing displays it**. Done in Phase 126e below, and it turned out to be two reports rather than one

## Phase 126b — M-CK3 follow-up: the presentation boundary

Reported by the user after 126 shipped: a triangle in the hyperbolic plane measured exactly 180°, no circles appeared, and elliptic looked identical to Euclidean. **All three were real, and all three sat outside what `ck_kind_coverage_test` can see** — that gate asks whether a kind's *value* responds to the absolute, and every one of these was a correct value that never reached the screen. The coverage debt Phase 125 recorded was about per-kind correctness; this is a second and separate gap, at the domain→presentation boundary, and it is the one that decided whether the milestone appeared to work at all.

- [x] **The angle label read the marker, not the measure.** `labelText` took `angle.measure` — the chart sweep of the drawn arc — where it meant `GeoAngle.measure`. The same trap `GeoAngle.measure` itself fell into mid-Phase-125, one layer out. Labels now read 152° / 180° / 202° for one triangle across the three geometries, which is also what made elliptic look inert
- [x] **Every CK circle was invisible.** `GeoCircle.isDefined` was `circle != null`, which asks whether the conic is a *Euclidean* circle; a CK circle is bitangent to the absolute, so `toCircleEq` answers null and the painter skipped it before reaching the conic arm that draws it correctly. Now falls back to `ConicShape.isParameterized` — a curve, **narrowly**, where `isDrawable` would also admit line pairs, which is what keeps Euclidean bit-identical (a collinear `ThreePointCircle` is a real line pair and must stay undefined)
- [x] **The plane was two pixels across.** The absolute is the unit circle in *world* units and the default scale is one pixel per world unit, so the whole hyperbolic plane was a dot at the origin while the figure sat hundreds of units outside it — outside the plane, where angles collapse to zero. `fittedToAbsolute` frames the disc on switching; Euclidean and elliptic leave the view alone, neither having a privileged region to frame
- [x] **One constant was answering two questions.** Framing the disc needed `maxScale` above 50, which moved six goldens — and the move was the finding. "How far may the user zoom" is about the view and a hyperbolic document needs a great deal of it; "how far may a fit blow a tiny figure up" is about taste and its right answer did not change. Split into `maxScale` (2000) and `maxFitScale` (50, the old value)
- [x] **The wash was correct and invisible** (Phase 126c). One alpha of 0.09 on the absolute's colour measures a 5 % luminance step over the light canvas — reported as "the same background colour". Replaced by a per-theme colour with its alpha baked in, because the two canvases start from opposite ends and one alpha cannot serve both; light and dark now measure 0.14 and 0.10. Pinned by **rasterizing and measuring**, the only kind of test that separates "drawn in the right shape" from "visible" — the painter test that checks the path's shape was green the whole time
- [x] **The wash left no hole at all on the web renderer** (Phase 126d) — the user's actual report, which I misread twice before the screenshots settled it. With the disc entirely inside the canvas the whole surface was washed and only the rim showed; with any part of the boundary off an edge it rendered correctly. `Path.combine(PathOperation.difference, rect, oval)` returns the outer rect unbroken on web when the inner shape is fully contained, and the annulus on the VM. Both uses are even-odd fills now — same region, no boolean op, nothing left to differ — and the second use, the region-pick scrim, had the identical defect unreported and one grep away
- [x] **`test/web/` and a CI browser gate.** Web is the compile target, the VM harness is not the renderer, and nothing in the suite had ever run in a browser. `flutter test --platform chrome test/web` is in CI and in CLAUDE.md; scoped to a directory because `--platform chrome` compiles everything it is handed to JS. The file keeps the boolean difference as *evidence*, asserting the broken behaviour, so the day an engine fixes it the test says so
- [x] ~~`visibleWorldBounds` contributes nothing for a CK circle~~ — **Phase 130**. `ConicShape.extentAlong(u, v)` is the conic's support interval in a chart direction, delegating to the existing exact `extremesAlong`, and gated on `ConicClass.ellipse` because that is the only class with a bounded curve. Taking a *direction* rather than an axis is what makes the rotated view frame a different argument instead of a transformed conic. Every non-circular conic gains a box with it, not just the CK ones — a five-point ellipse contributed nothing to a fit before either
- [x] ~~The absolute's radius is fixed at 1 world unit~~ — **Phase 131**. A per-document radius in the kernel block, `diag(1, 1, −R²)` with the dual still the adjugate. Entering a proper geometry sizes the plane to contain the figure (`absoluteRadiusContaining`, margin 2), so switching an existing Euclidean construction is meaningful with **no point moved**; moving between the two proper geometries keeps the radius the document is already drawn in. Schema: `radius ≠ 1` is **v3**, because a v2 reader skips the key, reads a figure drawn at radius 240 against the unit disc, and draws nothing — a misread, not a graceful degradation. A hyperbolic document at the canonical radius is exactly the v2 file it always was, and `test/fixtures/` is still all v1

## Phase 126e — the two silent re-addressings, said out loud

The debt Phase 126 left, and it was larger than the checklist line. An intersection point's **address** can change without the user touching it in exactly two ways: opening a document the reader had to repair, and switching the document's geometry, where `branchIndex` names a position in the candidate list *as ordered against the absolute*. Both are invisible by construction — a re-pointed crossing looks exactly like the one the user tapped — and a silent repair is how the Phase 120c document accumulated points nobody could account for in the first place. So the fix is one report, in one place, for both.

- [x] **The reader was silent about the case it could not fix, which is the case that matters more.** `_separateDuplicateBranches` returned the ids it moved; a duplicate whose curve pair has no crossing left to give it fell out of the loop with nothing recorded. A repaired point is a defect the reader *fixed*; an unrepaired one is still there, no drag can separate it, and the only remaining fix is to delete the surplus — which is the user's call, not the decoder's. `DecodedDocument.unrepairedIntersections`, the honest remainder, same shape as `GeometryChange.unmatched` and for the same reason
- [x] `intersection_report.dart` — both messages as plain functions taking no `BuildContext`, because **the sentence is the feature** and should be readable without a widget tree, plus one `showIntersectionReport` so the two read as the same kind of news. The switch's phrasing is unchanged and `GeometryMenu._report` becomes a call to it
- [x] Wired into the open flow in `main.dart`. Checked while there: reading `decoded.kernel` would be a *second* source — the kernel rides on `decoded.construction`, which the codec built with it — so the load path needs no separate apply and does not have one
- [x] **Tested at three levels, which is the Phase 126b lesson applied rather than restated**: the strings themselves; that `showIntersectionReport` actually puts one on screen; and both real flows end to end — Open through the fake picker `file_menu_test` already had, and the switch through the `geometry_switch_test` witness (two tier-1 conics that miss, so nothing moves and only the numbering changes). Every one of the four Phase 126b–d defects was a correct value that no test drove all the way to the screen
- [x] **And the negative on each**, because it is a claim rather than an omission: a well-formed document and an ordinary switch say **nothing at all**. Most of a document is real transverse crossings that keep their indices in every geometry, so the usual report is no report — an unconditional "your points may have moved" is noise and trains the user to dismiss the one time it matters
- [x] ~~The unrepairable case gets a snack bar that vanishes in six seconds~~ — it now **names the points** rather than counting them (Phase 131), by user-facing label where they have one and by id otherwise, trimmed to three with the rest summarised. A dialog was the other option and is still available if anyone ever hits this in the wild; naming is what makes the message actionable at all, and a count never was

## Phase 127 — `RotatedPoint`, and the deferral re-examined

The last piece of Phase 125's per-kind debt that was a **gap** rather than a refusal. The deferral said a CK rotation about a general centre needs an orthonormal frame in the pencil through it, and that the sign of the dual form varies across that pencil so the angle is not uniformly circular. **The second half is true; the first is not** — and the half that is true is about the *centre*, not the pencil. See PLAN §"A rotation is the exponential of one generator".

- [x] **No frame, because the isometries are a Lie group.** The maps preserving the absolute have Lie algebra `{Ω⁻¹K : K antisymmetric}`, every antisymmetric 3×3 is a cross-product `[C]ₓ` with kernel `C`, so the group fixing `C` is `exp(t·Ω*[C]ₓ)` with no basis chosen anywhere. The generator satisfies `N³ = −λ²N`, which closes the exponential into Rodrigues' formula — two matrix products, no series. `ProjTransform.ckRotation`
- [x] **One formula for all three geometries**: expanded under the Euclidean dual `diag(1,1,0)` it reproduces the shipped `ProjTransform.rotation` **entry for entry**, `λ = 1` included, over a spread of centres and angles. Same relationship `harmonicHomology` has to `reflection`. The Euclidean route is kept anyway, exact and cheaper, per the Phase 122/124 rule
- [x] **The sign of `λ²` is the classical trichotomy, and it is where the deferral was right.** `λ²` is the absolute's form at the centre up to sign and scale: positive → interior, and the pencil through an interior point misses the dual conic entirely so its angle measure is elliptic and the rotation is as circular as a Euclidean one; zero → on the absolute, a parabolic horolation with no angle; negative → outside, a boost along the centre's polar whose parameter is a **rapidity**. The last two refuse with the zero map, because `cos`/`sin` there would answer with a *different* isometry rather than an approximate one
- [x] **Two conventions pinned rather than inherited.** The sign of `λ` is free and the stored hyperbolic dual is the adjugate (`−Ω⁻¹`), so a generator taken at face value turns clockwise in one geometry and counter-clockwise in another — handedness is anchored so positive angles are counter-clockwise in the chart in all three. And the centre is **dephased first**: `λ²` scales as `k²`, so an `i`-scaled representative of an ordinary interior centre would fail the realness test and be refused as a boost
- [x] `ProjPoint.dephased` promoted out of `ck_measure.dart`'s private canonicalization, with the Phase 125 exactness note that made it a *phase* division rather than a component division. `_canonical` now delegates, same arithmetic
- [x] `RotatedPoint` wired; per-kind tests for the orbit (every image the same CK distance from the centre — no wrong scale, sign or chart artefact survives that), the two refusals, elliptic's universality, and the Euclidean answer untouched. Suite **2557** green (from 2532), analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical
- [x] ~~The macro tools that build on `RotatedPoint`~~ — measured and fixed in **Phase 128** below. Both were wrong, and the claim recorded here ("an equilateral triangle by three CK rotations is genuinely equilateral") was false

## Phase 128 — the macro tools, measured and re-expressed

Phase 127's last open box, and it was a defect rather than a gap. Both macro tools built on `RotatedPoint` name a *shape* and realise it with a constant angle baked in at build time — which names a shape only where similar figures exist. See PLAN §"A shape is not an angle".

- [x] **Measured first, no behaviour change.** `ckRotation` delivers the angle it is asked for exactly in every geometry, and that is the defect. The triangle's apex sits at exactly 60° from A under every absolute, so the legs stay equal and the base does not — long hyperbolic, short elliptic, by 1.2 % at a chart side of 0.3 and 23 % at 0.9. The polygon chains a fixed Euclidean interior angle and the ring never closes: a hyperbolic octagon at a chart side of 0.6 closes with a segment 3.8× its other sides
- [x] **And no constant can fix either.** A regular n-gon of CK side `s` turns by θ where `cos(π/n) = σ(s/2)·sin(θ/2)`, σ = cosh / cos / ≡ 1. Pinned both ways: that turn closes the ring exactly for every n, side and proper absolute, *and* it moves with `s`, while `RotatedPoint.angle` is fixed for the object's lifetime — so a figure built correct on the stamp stops being regular at the first drag
- [x] **A defect underneath, in the tool layer rather than this phase.** `coincidentExistingPoint` screened on `candidate.position` before recomputing anything, and a candidate arrives from a constructor, which settles on the Euclidean default. In a non-Euclidean document every derived point a macro offered for reuse was compared at a place the figure does not have, so nothing deduplicated at all. The candidate's private ancestor chain is recomputed under the absolute up front now, which is what the probe loop already did
- [x] **The equilateral triangle becomes Euclid I.1 under a proper absolute** — a crossing of the circle centred at A through B with the circle centred at B through A, both hidden, exactly equilateral in all three geometries at every side. The branch is picked by *side*, not taken as index 0: the candidate order follows the conic solver and reverses with the tap order, while the side is the tool's documented promise
- [x] **The regular polygon becomes an orbit** — centre and one vertex, the rest that vertex turned about the centre by 2πk/n. That angle survives a change of absolute because it is an angle at the centre of the *symmetry* that generates the figure rather than an angle of the figure. Both tools keep their Euclidean routes untouched, per the Phase 122/124 rule
- [x] The toolbar row's subtitle flips with the kernel — "two corners" / "centre, then a corner" — since it is the only place in the app that says what a macro's taps mean. Suite **2575** green (from 2557), analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical, browser gate green
- [x] **A geometry switch does not rebuild a figure** — said out loud in Phase 129. The computable half is reported: `GeometryChange.undefined` names the objects that were drawable before the switch and are not after, which is what an affine ratio or a unique parallel costs. The half that is not computable — a figure that still computes but is no longer the shape it is named after — is in the in-app guide, with the advice that follows from it (stamp the shape macros *after* switching)
- [x] ~~The other macro tools were not looked at~~ — done in **Phase 129** above, and the parallelogram belonged in the list too. Four outcomes: two were already right, two needed the reflection to stop being affine, the square was building a Saccheri quadrilateral, and five refuse

## Phase 129 — the macro triage

Phase 128's second open box: the other eleven macro tools, none of which had been looked at in a CK plane. They specify their figures by *metric primitives* rather than by a constant angle, so the question is whether the figure survives, not whether the primitive does. See PLAN §"The macro triage".

- [x] **Already right** — isosceles triangle (perpendicular bisector) and right triangle (perpendicular). The right angle is π/2 under every absolute; the angle sum is not π. Pinned rather than assumed
- [x] **`mirrorPointAcross` was affine** — the perpendicular foot plus a `SegmentRatioPoint` at ratio 2, which Phase 125 refuses. So the kite and the isosceles trapezium each had two defined sides and two undefined ones. The metric reflection is `ReflectedPoint`, a harmonic homology, single-valued and branch-free, so the CK route needs *no* scaffolding at all — it is shorter than the Euclidean one, which is kept only because it is what every existing document contains
- [x] **The isosceles trapezium keeps its equal legs and loses the word "trapezium"** — its base sides are perpendicular to a common axis and so ultraparallel, which is a Saccheri quadrilateral. Said at the site rather than silently claimed
- [x] **The square builds a Saccheri quadrilateral** — three equal sides and two right base angles, summit longer than base in hyperbolic and shorter in elliptic, summit angles not right. A square is the regular 4-gon, so under a proper absolute it takes the Phase 128 orbit, now shared with `RegularPolygonMacroTool` in `regular_polygon_orbit.dart`; the two agree corner for corner
- [x] **Five refuse: parallelogram, rectangle, trapezium, right trapezium** (all need *the* parallel through a point, and the rectangle four right angles besides) **and the rhombus**, which is a *gap* rather than an impossibility — see below. `MultiPointTool.availableUnder`, checked in `collectVertex` so the tools whose last tap is position-only are covered too — the rhombus's refusal was lifted in **Phase 138**, leaving four
- [x] **The refusal is visible.** Those five committed the tapped free points plus a chain of permanently undefined objects and said nothing — four taps, two dots and half a figure, uncompletable by any drag. The toolbar disables the rows and puts the reason where the row's explanation goes, checked against the tools' own answer by a test so the list cannot drift
- [x] The square's two taps change meaning under a proper absolute exactly as the polygon's did, so its toolbar subtitle flips too. Suite **2591** green (from 2575), analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical
- [x] ~~**Gluing a point to a general conic**~~ — scoped and **scheduled as Phase 132** above, whose real content turns out to be parameter *stability* rather than the parameterization: `ConicShape.pointAt` already sweeps any nondegenerate conic exactly
- [x] The tools were triaged by what they *build*, and what happens to one **already in a document** is now reported: `GeometryChange.undefined` for the figures that stop, and the guide for the ones that keep computing and stop meaning what they are called

## Phase 130 — a conic frames itself

- [x] `ConicShape.extentAlong(u, v)`: the conic's support interval in a chart direction, on the existing exact `extremesAlong` rather than beside it, gated on `ConicClass.ellipse` because that is the only class with a bounded curve. Taking a *direction* rather than an axis makes the rotated view frame a different argument instead of a transformed conic
- [x] `visibleWorldBounds` frames a Cayley–Klein circle by it — and every other non-circular conic with it, which contributed nothing to a fit before either

## Phase 131 — the absolute gets a radius

- [x] `DocumentKernel.radius` and `Absolute.scaled`: `x² + y² = R²w²` hyperbolic, `x² + y² + R²w² = 0` elliptic, the dual still the adjugate throughout so `dual = adj(point)` keeps holding literally
- [x] **The radius is a chart scale, not a geometry**, and that is what makes it safe to put in a document — `x = R·x′` carries `⟨P,Q⟩` to `R²` times the unit form and every measure is a ratio of such forms. Pinned against both proper absolutes, three radii, two point pairs
- [x] The Euclidean absolute refuses one, which is the same fact as similar triangles existing; `DocumentKernel` guarantees the refusal is never reached
- [x] Entering a proper geometry sizes the plane to contain the figure (twice the furthest point); moving between the two proper ones keeps the radius, because by then it is the plane the document is drawn in
- [x] **Schema: `radius ≠ 1` is v3.** A v2 reader skips the key, reads a figure drawn at radius 240 against the unit disc, and draws nothing. Pinned by decoding one both ways. Unit-radius documents are still v2, Euclidean ones still v1, and `test/fixtures/` is still all v1

## Phase 132 — gluing a point to a general conic (closed: the parameter-stability half is Phase 132d)

The capability every other CK gap turned out to want: `PointOnObject` parameterized a circle by its polar angle in the chart, and a conic bitangent to the absolute has no centre and no polar angle — so a Cayley–Klein circle was a curve nothing could be attached to. See PLAN §Parameterization.

**The static capability landed; parameter stability is the open half.**

- [x] **It was also a live crash, not only a missing feature.** The hit tester has reported general conics since Phase 119 and `resolvePoint` glue-probes every hit curve, so `PointOnObject.near` — which had no branch for a conic with no `CircleEq` — **threw `ArgumentError` on a tap**. Six taps from a clean document: build a conic with the Conics tool, pick the point tool, tap the conic. Pinned end-to-end in `geometry_canvas_test.dart`, and confirmed to fail on the code before this phase
- [x] **A general conic host is swept by `ConicShape`'s pencil angle** — π-periodic, a bijection from `[0, π)` onto the whole curve, polynomial in homogeneous coordinates rather than a chart evaluation. A circle keeps its polar angle, so **no stored parameter changes meaning, no migration, no format bump**: the codec already stores a bare double against a parent id, and an older reader lands on an undefined point rather than a misread one
- [x] **A tap glues where it landed.** `ConicShape.parameterNear` reports *where* `distanceTo` measured, sharing one search so glue and hit test cannot disagree — as against `parameterOf`, which answers the arc of the join `P₀ ∨ p` and is a different point for a tap that missed the ink
- [x] **The point can be dragged along its conic.** `_SlideDragSession.start` had no `project` map for a conic and answered null, so a glued point could be created and never moved. It slides by the pencil angle, with the periodic grab-offset normalization taking π there instead of 2π
- [x] **Tracing continues through a conic host.** `Construction._chartEvaluator` had a line arm and a circle arm and *threw* for anything else, on the argument that an undefined carrier leaves every dependent candidate-free so a detour is unreachable. A conic host is a **defined** carrier with no `CircleEq`, which is exactly what that argument does not cover — so this phase made the throw reachable and the arm is now real: `ConicShape.pointAtComplex`, the same expression `pointAt` already was, since `X(Q) = (QᵀAQ)·P₀ − 2·(P₀ᵀAQ)·Q` is polynomial over ℂ and only the pencil sweep had a real signature. A real angle reproduces the real evaluation **bitwise**, so a detour rejoins exactly and the commit's static solve does not move the point
- [x] Coverage: 15 tests — gluing lands on the curve and rides it, π-periodicity, `.near` beats every sample of the curve, `parameterNear` agrees with `distanceTo`, `pointAtComplex` is bitwise-equal on real angles and stays on the conic off them, the slide drag, the parameter drive, an imaginary ellipse refusing at every entry point, and the widget-level crash. Gates: suite **2676** green, analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical, browser gate green
- [x] **The pencil angle is not stable as the conic moves — closed in Phase 132d below.** `ConicShape` picks its base point and axis pair per matrix — an ellipse's base point is the first of two probe directions that yields a real root, and the axis pair is `argmax` over the base point's coordinates — so both jump on a tie-break that is numerics, not geometry. **Measured** over smooth 600–1200-step sweeps: a rotating ellipse and a rotating circle are clean (max step 0.011 and 0.000, no switches), but a rotating **elongated** ellipse — no class change at all, a rigid motion — jumps **30 times, by up to 2.7 world units**, with 12 base-point jumps and 4 axis switches; a deformation crossing ellipse → hyperbola jumps by up to 75. A point glued at a fixed angle visibly leaves its place
- [x] **The design sketch resolved differently than written, and the difference is the finding of Phase 132d.** The sketch said "re-express on each recompute from the point's previous homogeneous value" and named its cost: a history-dependent `recompute`. Working the cost through killed the sketch — unconditional continuation drifts first-order under pure translation (the point crawls off the figure over a long drag), and *any* mutation inside `recompute` breaks the locus sweep's bit-exact restore, `point_coincidence.dart`'s probe, and bitwise undo at once. What survives is the `branchIndex` settlement: recompute stays pure and canonical, the **gesture** holds identity, the **command** adopts it. See Phase 132d
- [x] **A locus cannot yet be driven by a point on a conic** — done in **Phase 132c** below, for the ellipse; the unbounded classes are that phase's open box
- [x] What it closes now that both halves have landed (132 static + 132d stability): the rhombus macro (Phase 129) is **unblocked** — un-refusing it was a tool change of its own, done in **Phase 138** below, where it turned out to be about the *fourth* corner rather than the third

## Phase 132b — conics had the wrong header in the object tree

Reported by the user: the object tree filed conics under **Circles**.

- [x] **The three genuine conic kinds get their own `Conics` header** — `FivePointConic`, `BifocalConic`, `FocalConic`, the same three that override `isDefined` with `ConicShape.isDrawable`. They are `GeoCircle` subclasses because that is where the conic-valued carrier lives, not because a user thinks of them as circles
- [x] **Matched by kind, never by whether the geometry currently projects to a `CircleEq`.** A five-point conic that passes through circularity mid-drag must not hop between headers while the pointer is down, and a bifocal ellipse must not stop being a conic because it went round. Arcs, sectors and the CK circles stay under Circles, which is what the rest of the UI calls them
- [x] **Only the header was wrong.** `object_kind_label.dart` already answered `Conic` / `Parabola` / `Ellipse` / `Hyperbola` per row, so the row text needed no change — the group name was the whole defect
- [x] Coverage: a circle and a conic side by side land under different headers with the right row label, and a circles-only document still shows no `Conics` header (empty groups are skipped). Gates: suite **2678** green, analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical

## Phase 132c — a locus driven by a point on a conic

Phase 132 made a general conic a curve a point can be glued to and left the locus behind: `_SweepDomain.of` asked a `GeoCircle` host for its `CircleEq` and gave up when there was none, so a locus driven by such a point had **no samples at all** — silently absent, which is the harder kind to notice.

- [x] **The domain is the pencil circle the point is already glued on**: `φ = π·x`, cyclic with period 1, one bijection from `[0, π)` onto the whole curve. `pointAt` *is* `pointAtComplex` at a real angle, so the real and complex evaluations are one expression and a detour rejoins the real axis bitwise with nothing kept in step by hand — the property the line and circle arms buy with two parallel formulas each
- [x] **`ConicShape.chartLiftAt` — the pencil value a tracing consumer can take.** `pointAtComplex` is polynomial and homogeneous, so it answers at whatever scale the algebra leaves: **`w = −0.079` at `φ = 0.3`** on `x²/16 + y²/4 = 1`, and *negative*. `PointOnObject.tracedPosition`'s contract is `w` exactly one or exactly zero, because `position` reads x and y straight back without dividing (so a sweep along a diverging arm keeps full precision). Hand over the raw value and every chain member reading the driver's chart position gets it rescaled and, half the time, reflected
- [x] **The drag path had the same hole, and its two sibling arms did not.** `Construction._chartEvaluator`'s conic arm returned `pointAtComplex` directly; the line and circle arms both build `w = Complex.one` by hand. Nothing in the suite moved when it was fixed, and that is the expected result rather than a reassuring one — every chain member in the existing conic rigs is projective, so an arbitrary scale is invisible to them. What it is *not* invisible to is a chart reading: `LineThroughTwoPoints` orients its `LineEq` off `p2 − p1`, and the four kinds Phase 136c named pick their branch off exactly such a direction
- [x] **The ellipse is the class that is swept**, on the gate `extentAlong` already states: an ellipse is the only conic whose curve is bounded. A bounded conic host (an `angularExtent` with no `CircleEq`) is refused for a different reason — an angular extent is a *circle's* span and stands in no relation to a pencil angle. No object reaches that state today, and the guard says so rather than trusting that it stays true
- [x] **Making the circle arm a `circle?` pattern made every arm below it reachable for the first time**, `default:`'s `StateError` included. A `GeoCircle()` arm answers null there now — which is what the circle arm used to do from the inside. Phase 132's lesson, applied on the way past rather than after the crash
- [x] Coverage: **23 tests** — the sweep exists and every sample is on the closed-form traced curve; the domain is one full period with no closing duplicate and the whole curve covered; a spy in the chain sees the driver **on its host conic at every sample** (the normalization, stated as the property it buys); the sample is the driver's own `chartPointAt` bit for bit; the stored parameter and the chain survive; a chain with an `IntersectionPoint` holds one sheet; the four refused classes get null and **no throw**; an extent-claiming conic is refused; the whole path from the toolbar with a `FivePointConic` and a glued tap; and five on `chartLiftAt` itself. Then for the unbounded classes: each hyperbola branch its own polyline and the two genuinely apart; the 40-conic family sweep above; a parabola's arm unbroken; the round-up pinned at `sampleCount` 127 *and* 128; the walk on a hyperbola host; and the focus window narrowing an unbounded core while leaving an ellipse's alone. Three more on `infinityParameters` — the count is the class, the curve really has no chart point there and does on either side, and they move with the conic. Gates: suite **2701** green, analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical, browser gate green
- [x] **The hyperbola and the parabola sweep too, on a grid cut at infinity.** `ConicShape.infinityParameters` (the crossings `_breakParameters` already found for `polylines`, published) cuts the pencil circle into the curve's *arcs* — a hyperbola's two branches, a parabola's single arm. Each arc takes an equal share of the domain and `sampleCount` rounds **up** to a multiple of the arc count, so every crossing lands *on* a grid sample. An ellipse has no crossings, so it is one arc from 0 and sweeps `φ = π·x` exactly as before, bit for bit
- [x] **The defect it fixes is not the magnitude, which is what the phase was opened expecting.** Over **200** hyperbolas whose first crossing sweeps 0.13π to 0.23π, a plain uniform pencil grid joins the two branches into one polyline in **200 of 200** — landing a sample exactly on a crossing is measure-zero, so the run never breaks and the locus draws a chord from one branch across the figure to the other. Cut on the crossings: **0 of 200**, and the worst sample falls from **3384** world units to **45**
- [x] **Only one of a hyperbola's two crossings is at a nice angle.** Its base point *is* one of its points at infinity, so that one's asymptote parameter comes out 0 or π/2 and a uniform grid hits it by luck; the second lands wherever the matrix puts it. That asymmetry is what makes the alignment load-bearing rather than cosmetic, and it is why the fixtures are skewed hyperbolas rather than `x²/16 − y²/4 = 1`
- [x] **Nothing further was needed for the split.** The crossing sample either leaves the traced point undefined, and `_runs` breaks the branches apart there, or it does not, and the chain genuinely continues through driver-infinity — the full-line host's rule, unchanged, with `hasInfinityWrap` probing the wrap the same way. The walk (not just the scan) runs on such a host: every sample of an `IntersectionPoint` chain lands exactly on its circle, across both branches
- [x] **`center` / `halfSpan` mean on an unbounded conic host what they mean on a line, said chart-side**: the driver within `halfSpan` of where it stood when the locus was made (`LocusTool` passes `driver.parameter` as `center`). On a line `|t − center| ≤ halfSpan` *is* that, because `t` is arc length. A `center` whose own point is at infinity falls back to `ConicShape.anchorPoint`, and then to no window at all. An ellipse is bounded and keeps every defined sample, exactly as a circle host does — `halfSpan` does not touch it

## Phase 133 — a locus is anchored on its own point

A user document (`test/fixtures/no-locus.rgl`) whose locus was created, listed in the object tree, and drew nothing visible. It was drawing — the x-axis, in the default colour, underneath the very line its driver is glued to.

- [x] **The walk seeded its branch identity at the run's low end, and that is a place the document says nothing about.** The driver F is glued to line a; d is the circle centred A through F, so F is *itself* one of the two crossings of d with the line through B and F, and the pair crosses transversally at the two feet of the Thales circle over AB. The canonical index therefore names a different root on each side of those, and out at the sweep's driver-at-infinity end it names F. The walk then held that root honestly all the way back: every sample was the driver's own position
- [x] The seed is now `_SweepDomain.seedX` — the driver's stored (host-clamped) parameter mapped through each host's own `x → t`, for all four sweep domains. Branch identity is only pinned where the construction actually stands, which is the one parameter at which `traced` holds the value the user sees. The invariant it buys, and the one that was violated: **a locus passes through the position its traced point has**
- [x] The walk also *resumes* from the anchor after the opening dive, instead of leaving from wherever the dive stopped. A dive ends on a limit — a fold's touch point, a driver-at-infinity frontier — where nearest matching is a coin flip and the acceptance rule cannot re-expand; the lift-off rewind that used to paper over that is gone, because re-seeding on the anchor is exact. It also removes the span the old shape re-walked
- [x] **A doomed detour arc no longer spends the run's whole budget.** The starvation at a sweep's own end classifies as a *crossing* (the roots are real on both sides), so the walk plans an arc across what is really the edge of the domain, and no representable step advances it. `_traceArc` now gives up when `theta - dTheta == theta`, mirroring `advance`'s own floor. This was latent before the seed changed: the old locus reached that end with nothing left to walk. The drag walk's twin loop in `construction.dart` has the same shape, bounded by a much smaller budget and a static fallback — noted, not touched
- [x] Coverage: the document as a fixture with its analytic curve equation, plus a reduced synthetic rig pinning both branches (each passes through *its* point), that the two branches are different curves, and that the sweep no longer grinds a budget away. All fail without the fix
- [x] The two locus goldens moved by a sub-pixel resampling of the same curve (0.17 %) and were regenerated

## Phase 134 — the point that feeds a locus jumps at a crossing (closed: the budget half became Phase 139)

Reported against the Phase 133 document: after F is dragged past A or past B, the locus goes invisible again. It is not the locus — **G jumps onto the driver's own root**, and the locus then faithfully draws the x-axis. The frame-to-frame seam is fixed; the budget is not.

- [x] **The drag walk's `traceArc` had no resolution floor**, the exact twin of Phase 133's locus defect: at `theta - dTheta == theta` it refused the same trial until the frame budget ran out. Same floor, same reasoning
- [x] Cost of lifting the locus-chain exclusion, measured while it was out so the next attempt need not re-measure: none. 0.556 vs 0.558 ms/frame on the 100-object stress rig carrying a 128-sample locus
- [x] **A pass seeds at its own path start.** "The point sits on `path.start`" was assumed by both drive entries and is false for the frame after a bail, whose end state is a static solve at the pointer. The pass drives there and recomputes first, so the precondition is made true rather than trusted
- [x] **A frame that lands exactly on the degenerate parameter no longer loses identity.** Two halves: the session does not advance its path anchor onto a bailed frame *or* onto one whose pair closed all the way (`closing == 0`), because both end states resolve canonically and that relabelling is the defect itself; and holding the anchor is what puts the degeneracy in the *next* path's interior, where a detour arc has somewhere to land instead of at an endpoint where it has not. Grid snapping lands the pointer exactly on t = 0 and t = −8 in the reported document, so this was every gesture
- [x] **A pass's first trial was unbounded, and that is why a crossing inside a frame was glided over.** The collapse law needs two separation samples and a pass restarts with none, so the first trial spans the whole frame; nearest matching then takes the *wrong* root with a small measured motion. It cannot be detected from the path's two ends — the roots exchange at a crossing, so every endpoint statistic is invariant under the exchange. A pass now reports `closing`, the ratio its tightest pair closed by, and the next frame opens its controller at that fraction
- [x] **The collapse at A is crossed.** Was "always jumps — every budget, every step size": at t = 0 the circle centred A through F has radius zero, so the walk coasted through with no candidates and re-acquired on a coin flip. Now 16 of 16 rows (four step sizes × two budgets × two grid offsets) against 0 before, measured on the reported shape with the locus removed so G is an ordinary traced slot — `test/domain/tools/drag_crossing_test.dart`
- [x] **The frame budget is still too small to cross the transversal at B** — *the decision this asked for is Phase 139's, and it is taken: the budget is derived from the graph now, and this rig's crossing is carried with nothing hand-set.* Two corrections ride with it. The first is Phase 135's, recorded when this box was opened: on the reported document B is a structural coincidence, so `G` has no branch to lose there at any budget. The second is Phase 139's — **"one trial is ~0.08 ms on the 100-object stress rig" was a *frame* cost at 1.00 trials/frame**, charging every trial with the once-per-pass hoist and seed, so the "128 is already ~10 ms, 512 would be ~40" that argued against raising it over-charges by ~7×. What stands: creeping in costs ~90 trials to classify the starvation, the arc ~20, the exit ~40 more, so ~150 against a constant 128 — and the answer was never a bigger constant but a budget in units of *work*. The frame-relative starvation-threshold box folds in here as it said: what failed was the trial count, not the geometry
- [~] **`_traceAlong` still excludes every locus-chain intersection point from tracing** — *no longer the reason the document loses its locus.* It was never a tracing problem: `G` resolves canonically on every frame, and Phase 135 makes canonical resolution **right** here. The exclusion stands, unlifted and now uncontested for this document
- [x] **Locus polyline density.** The reported "unexpected angles" were the walk's own sampling: the acceptance rules bound *branch safety*, not smoothness, so a fast-moving stretch of curve drew at scan-cell resolution. An accepted step whose chord exceeds 1 % of the figure's half-diagonal is refined instead, in the balanced frame. Worst turn on the reported document **29° → 5.5°**, 236 → 2147 points; the 8 ms gate goes 7 % → 19 % used on the stress rig carrying a locus. Three assumptions that "the finest accepted step is one scan cell" had to move with it: the refusal takes its own path around the loop rather than falling into the starvation classifier, refusals come from a bounded pool of their own rather than the anti-grinding trial budget, and `_trimDivergentTail` wants a clear doubling of sample spacing rather than any growth
- [x] **Tracing locus-chain points with the write-back suppressed — tried, measured, rejected.** It sounded like the separation the exclusion's own argument implies, and it buys nothing: without adoption the carried identity dies when the gesture's command applies and the point resolves canonically again — byte-identical to not tracing at all. With adoption restored it crosses the collapse at A and strands **7 of 12** randomized gesture sequences at B, **at every budget from 128 to 2048** — so B is not budget-bound after all. Against 0 of 12 as shipped. Recorded on the exclusion in `construction.dart`
- [x] **Why B is uncrossable — answered, and the question was wrong.** B is not a crossing the engine has to survive: it is a coincidence the construction *guarantees*, because the document's `G` is the intersection of a circle through `F` with a line through `F`. Nothing was going to cross it reliably, and nothing had to. See Phase 135. Two phases were spent on the drag path for a defect that reproduces on a fresh load with no drag at all — the standing lesson is in `docs/STATUS.md`: reproduce on the reported document *first*, and reproduce it the cheapest way the report allows

## Phase 135 — the crossing the construction already knew

The second half of the user report — "it disappears when the points go past a certain limit" — and it was never the drag. The document's `G` is the intersection of a circle *through* `F` with a line *through* `F`, so `F` is one of its two roots at every parameter; past either coincidence the geometric order had flipped and `branchIndex` named `F`. `G` was bit-identical to `F`, and the locus of `G` was the x-axis, drawn on top of the very line its driver is glued to. Reproduces on a fresh load with no drag at all, which is what Phases 133–134 never checked.

- [x] **`sharedIncidentPoints(curve1, curve2)`** in `incidence.dart`, on Phase 44b's existing zero-epsilon relation — the crossings a construction knows before any arithmetic. Deterministic order, deduplicated by identity, defining points only
- [x] **`IntersectionPoint` holds a role, not an address, when the pair shares a point**: one of the shared points, or the root left over when they are divided out. Deflation is exact, total and continuous — no crossing to carry, no budget to spend, and nothing for a static recompute to get wrong. Two roots of a real quadratic can meet *and stay real* only where a root is pinned by the construction, so the flip and the shared point are one phenomenon (PLAN §"A crossing the construction already knows")
- [x] **The role is derived, never stored** — settled once from the first state that can answer, by asking which candidate the geometric address names. A document loads with the role it was saved with, so: no format change, no version bump, no migration, no fixture rewrite. Ambiguous states settle nothing
- [x] **The reported document is fixed**, measured on it: `G` holds its own root at every parameter tested from −40 to +20, and the locus keeps its ~9.06 half-height everywhere instead of flattening onto the axis past t = 0 and t = −8
- [x] Deflation refuses to name the left-over root unless exactly one is left over, so a conic pair with four candidates and one shared point still addresses geometrically
- [x] Coverage: `test/domain/construction/deflation_test.dart` (12 tests — what counts as shared, both roles, the two never colliding, settling, and the document itself). The `locus_test.dart` assertion that the canonical scan gets a third of the curve wrong is **inverted**: it now agrees, because the crossing is named
- [x] Two rigs hide their incidence behind `HomotheticPoint(driver, centre: origin, ratio: 1)` — bit-exact, structurally invisible — so `drag_crossing_test.dart` keeps all 11 Phase 134 assertions verbatim (including B needing budget 512, re-measured) and the detour path keeps a test at all. What that exposed is Phase 136
- [x] Gates: suite **2649** green, analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical, browser gate green

## Phase 136 — the locus polyline handed the rasterizer a billion

From a user screen recording: on `phase-135-deflation`, `no-locus.rgl` opens with `G` in the right place — deflation visibly working — and **no locus drawn at all**, at every parameter. Every layer below the widget reproduces nothing: engine, codec, painter-in-isolation and the real app widget with real providers all give 1344 samples, `visible`, `isDefined`, and ~4400 px of ink, on the VM *and* in a real browser.

- [x] **The one measurable difference between the build that drew it and the build that does not.** `main` (Phase 133, deployed, drew): worst locus coordinate **1.5e5**, a screen coordinate of ~4e6. `phase-135-deflation`: **1.0e9**, a screen coordinate of **2.7e10** — because deflation makes the far stretch the *true* diverging curve instead of the bounded x-axis, and Phase 134's density refinement walks further out it. `_drawLocus` put every sample into one unclipped `Path` with the visible loop
- [x] **`_drawLocus` clips to the visible world box**, which is what `_drawConic` twenty lines below it has always done and for the reason it states — "a hyperbola runs off in two directions and a parabola in one, so there is no far enough". Exact, by segment, Liang–Barsky; a run that leaves and re-enters is two strokes; an untrimmed segment keeps its endpoints bit-identical (parameters compared against 0 and 1, not recomputed), so **32 goldens stayed byte-identical**. `GeoLocus.coreSamples` has existed since Phase 39f because the fitter and the label anchor could not use raw samples; the painter was the last reader still handing them over whole
- [x] Coverage: `test/presentation/canvas/locus_clip_test.dart` — ink at six parameters, plus a guard that the sweep really does still produce an arm past 1e6 so the clip stays under test
- [x] **Confirmed on the user's build: the locus draws.** The clip is the fix.
