It was never reproduced headlessly — a `Path` with a 2.7e10 vertex draws fine in `flutter test --platform chrome`, which is evidently not the renderer `flutter run -d chrome` uses — so the confirmation had to come from the app, and it is the only evidence there was ever going to be. What generalizes: **an unbounded world coordinate must not reach a `Path`**, whatever the rasterizer does with it, and `_drawConic` had said so since it was written

## Phase 136b — a shared point no theorem proves

**The headline defect is fixed** — the detour path holds its sheet on a hidden incidence now. The two items below were always "as real documents call for it"; Session 151 took each as far as that policy allows and closed them.

(Renumbered from 136 when the rendering defect took that slot.) Deflation reaches a crossing only when `incidence.dart` can *prove* the point is on both curves. A construction can put it there by a theorem the detector does not model, and then the walk is back to navigating. That path now has no coverage from the fixture corpus at all — both documents' detours were structural and went 4 → 0 — so its rigs hide an incidence behind a bit-exact copy of the driver on purpose.

- [x] **The locus walk landed on the wrong sheet on a hidden incidence — and the two walks differed in the *carrier kind*, not in the walk.** The rig planned both detour arcs and still put **406 of 723** samples on the driver's own circle. `Segment.recompute` nulled its projective carrier whenever an endpoint had no affine position (Phase 107, deliberate: "a segment IS its drawn extent — carrier included"), so the instant a detour drove the driver off the real axis the chord had **no carrier at all**: the slot coasted the whole arc with no candidates on a stale root, and the exit was decided by a nearest match *at the crossing* — the coin flip the arc exists to remove. The drag rig's chord is a `LineThroughTwoPoints`, whose carrier has been total since Phase 107, which is why that walk was fine on identical geometry
- [x] **The carrier is total; only the drawn extent is a chart reading.** `Segment` and `Ray` compute `carrierThrough` unconditionally and gate only the projection: `line`, `start`, `end`, `parameterExtent` and therefore `isDefined` read exactly as before, so the painter, hit tester and every extent-clamped constrained point see no change. Nothing static moves for a *complex* endpoint — `intersectionCandidates` refuses complex carriers on its own (Phase 110's realness gate). An endpoint at *infinity* now yields the real line through the finite one in that direction, which is what `LineThroughTwoPoints` has answered since Phase 107 and what `IntersectionPoint` already assumed when it said segments intersect via their infinite carrier
- [x] **The general rule, recorded in PLAN §5**: a chart-gated projective accessor is an object that *cannot be continued through*, and no static test can tell it from a correct one. This was a fourth, undocumented exception to the one degeneracy convention; the symptom surfaces only inside a walk, three phases downstream, as a wrong branch
- [x] Coverage: the reproducer in `locus_test.dart` is inverted — **406 of 723 wrong → 0 of 602**, every sample on the Thales circle with the incidence hidden, and the walk closes. `segment_test.dart` / `ray_test.dart` pin both halves of the split (at-infinity endpoint: carrier real and through both parents, extent undefined; complex endpoint: carrier complex and invisible to static intersection). Gates: suite **2653** green, analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical, browser gate green
- [x] Widen `onCarrierDefiningPoints` / `_derivedIncident` as real documents call for it — each addition converts a navigated crossing into a named one. Cheap and exact; the list is deliberately conservative. **Worked as far as the policy allows** (Session 151): `DiameterCircle` was the one *curve kind* whose defining points lie on its own carrier and were not listed, while every sibling with that property already was — a gap in the switch's own stated remit rather than speculative widening, now added and pinned on the Thales configuration. The audit found nothing else of that class: compass / fixed-radius / Apollonius / triangle circles, the polar and radical-axis lines and the focal conic are all correctly absent, their defining geometry being elsewhere. What remains is genuinely *new theorems* for `_derivedIncident` — a `Midpoint` or `SegmentRatioPoint` on the line through its own two parents, a `ProjectionPoint` on its reference line — and those are what the box means by "as real documents call for it"; none is added on spec
- [x] Deflation is refused when more than one root is left over (a conic pair with four candidates and one shared point). Whether the shared roles alone are worth naming there is untested either way. **Answered by measurement** (Session 151): naming the shared role is worth it and already worked — the code path is independent of how many roots remain, it simply had no test. Two ellipses crossing four ways with one crossing known by construction: the named crossing is P at **0 of 720** samples of a wiggle, while the same geometry with the incidence hidden behind a ratio-1 homothety loses it at **359 of 720** and gets there by a **3.4**-unit jump. Mutation-checked — refusing the shared role when more than one root is left over degrades the named point to exactly 359 of 720. The refusal itself is right (with three left over there is nothing unique to divide out) and is *not* free: each of the three left-over roots exchanges by the same ~3.4 over the same wiggle, which is now the baseline any widening has to beat

## Phase 136c — the carrier audit

Session 144's carried-forward question, asked of every kind: **does a projective accessor reach a `null` by way of a chart value?** `Segment` and `Ray` were found by following one walk; the other 50 kinds in `lib/domain/construction/objects/` had never been asked. The sweep is worth doing precisely because the suite cannot do it — a chart-gated projective accessor is statically indistinguishable from a correct one, so only reading the code finds these.

- [x] **The sweep.** Every `get projPoint` / `get projLine` / `get conic` in `objects/` is a plain field read, so the audit reduces to the 52 `recompute` bodies. Result recorded below, kind by kind, so the next occurrence is cheap to find
- [x] **`Sector` nulled its carrier off the chart — the same defect as `Segment`.** `circleThrough(center, start)` needs only projective values, but the guard bundled `center.position`, `start.position`, `end.position` *and* `end.closeTo(center)` into the same early return that nulled `_conic`. Its own sibling `Arc` has had the right split since Phase 109, which is what makes this an oversight rather than a decision. Nothing about it was visible statically: the carrier only matters to a pass that asked for it
- [x] **`Sector`'s carrier is total; only the wedge is a chart reading.** `circle`, `startAngle`, `sweep`, `startRim`, `endRim`, `angularExtent`, `containsAngle` and `isDefined` read exactly as before — painter, hit tester and every angle-clamped constrained point see no change. `end.closeTo(center)` moves to the extent gate with them: the end point fixes only the end angle and was never in the carrier
- [x] **`isDefined` had to be overridden, and `Arc` shows why it did not.** `GeoCircle.isDefined` falls through to *`conic` is parameterized* when `circle` is null, so the moment the carrier stopped being chart-gated a sector with a parent off the chart read **defined with no extent at all** — and the painter dereferences `circle!`, `startAngle!` and `sweep!` unconditionally on a defined sector. `Sector.isDefined` is now `sweep != null`, which is the old behaviour restated (the four fields were nulled together, so the two conditions named the same states). `Arc` needs no such override because its carrier reads all three parents: a circumcircle through a point off the chart is never a real circle, so `circle` is null and the conic is not parameterized either — measured on four off-chart configurations, all `isDefined == false`
- [x] **`TangentLine`'s realness gate is the same shape and is *kept*** — a tangent from a complex pole would fabricate real touch points, and `_v1Ordered(touches)[branch]` has no meaning on complex touches. That is `IntersectionPoint`'s Phase 110 argument verbatim, so it belongs *inside* sanctioned exception 1 rather than beside it; the exception on `GeoObject` now names both kinds
- [x] **`TwoLineBisectorLine`: the branch rests on a chart reading — no null, and the first guess about it was wrong.** `_anchored` fixes each parent's representative *sign*, and therefore which of the two bisectors `branch` names, off the parent's affine `LineEq.direction`, leaving it alone when there is no chart. The sign is load-bearing (negating a parent names the other bisector, 800 of 800). The **guess** was that Phase 136b made this reachable through `Segment`/`Ray`; measured, that is false — 0 of 1200 configurations move, because `carrierThrough`'s representative already agrees with the p1→p2 direction `orientedAlong` uses. What is true is narrower and worse: **four line kinds orient their chart *against* their own representative** — `TwoLineBisectorLine`, `PolarLine`, `RadicalAxisLine`, `TangentLine`, each via a `_v1Direction()` unrelated to the raw rep, at roughly half of configurations — and the other seven never do. A `PolarLine` whose pole sits on the circle's centre has a carrier (ℓ∞) and no chart, which Phase 134 showed grid snapping lands on exactly, and the bisector below it then names the other branch. Demonstrated, not argued
- [x] **Left open deliberately, then done as Phase 137:** making that branch chart-free means re-founding line *orientation* projectively, which is a branch-ordering change — load-bearing, per CLAUDE.md — and it would move `PolarLine`, `RadicalAxisLine` and `TangentLine` too. Phase 137 is that phase (`orientedAlong` and every `_v1Direction` deleted, the four kinds re-founded on the representative's sign); the box stood unticked until session 185 noticed Phase 186 still listing it as open work
- [x] **Audited and legitimate, with the reason** — `PointOnObject` (sanctioned exception 2: the parameter *is* a chart parameterization), `BifocalConic` (PLAN §"The conic constructors": the semi-axis is a sum of square roots of distances, with no homogeneous form), the Phase 112 consumer kinds and every measurement (exception 3), and the `absolute.isEuclidean` gates, which are a *geometry* question and not a chart reading. `Arc`, `LineThroughTwoPoints`, `PerpendicularBisectorLine`, `PolarLine`, `RadicalAxisLine`, `RelativeLine`, `AngleBisectorLine` (`_chart` renormalizes a representative and is total) and all sixteen point and circle kinds compute their projective value from projective parents only
- [x] **Noted, not changed:** several kinds still null a projective value inside a `closeTo` *band* rather than on the exact zero triple (`Arc`, `ApolloniusCircle`, `ThreePointCircle`, `AngleBisectorLine`, `HarmonicConjugatePoint`, and `Sector`'s surviving `start.closeTo(center)`). That contradicts PLAN §5's "only exact degeneracy degenerates", but it is a uniform decision taken across the migrated kinds in Phases 107–110 and guarding a genuine collapse of the carrier itself — a different argument from this phase's, and a sweep of its own
- [x] Gates: suite **2661** green (2653 + 8), analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical, browser gate green

## Phase 138 — the rhombus un-refused, and the corner that jumps

Phase 132's last box, and Phase 129's. Gluing a point to a general conic was what the rhombus was refused for; Phases 132/132d built it, so the refusal goes. The content turned out to be elsewhere — the *fourth* corner's route, which PLAN §"The macro triage" had recorded as Euclid I.1 and which measurement rejected.

- [x] **`RhombusMacroTool.availableUnder` deleted** and the toolbar row's "needs a point on a curved circle — not yet" with it, so the row carries its ordinary label again. The two are one fact written twice and `toolbar_test` still checks them against each other; the triage test's refusing set drops from five to four
- [x] **Corner C works because Phase 132 exists**: under a proper absolute the compass circle about B is a conic bitangent to the absolute with no centre and no polar angle, so C glues by `ConicShape`'s pencil angle. The tool recomputes each hidden circle under the document's absolute before reading it — a constructor has no document to ask and settles on the Euclidean default, the `EquilateralTriangleMacroTool` pattern
- [x] **Corner D is B reflected across the diagonal AC**, not the Euclid I.1 crossing PLAN recorded. B lies on both of I.1's circles by construction, so its two crossings are B and D; they swap as B crosses the diagonal, which sliding C once around its circle does twice. **Measured**: D jumps **0.80 world units** onto B at the crossing and stays pinned there for the rest of the sweep, the figure folded flat onto triangle ABC with no drag that recovers it. The reflection is the harmonic homology already shipping as `ReflectedPoint` — single-valued, branch-free, no scaffolding at all, the same substitution the kite and the isosceles trapezium took in Phase 129
- [x] **An equilateral check cannot see this defect, and that is the lesson worth keeping.** D ≡ B satisfies every side equality — a folded rhombus is still a rhombus by measurement — so the whole CK macro suite passes with the jumping route in place. Mutation-checked: restoring I.1 fails exactly the three continuity/kind pins and *nothing else*, the four-equal-sides sweep included
- [x] **The Euclidean route is untouched.** The parallelogram trick is what every stored rhombus contains, and it is continuous through the crossing — undefined only where C ≡ A exactly, where the diagonal is a point and every route is undefined. Measured before being left alone
- [x] Session-level coverage per the standing rule: three taps in a hyperbolic document build four corners through the *command* path (`availableUnder` is checked in `collectVertex`, and the third tap is position-only), the glued corner drags, and the gesture is one undo unit. Stated at the site that this does **not** pin D's continuity — a screen-length drag does not reach the flat configuration — which is the engine test's 600-sample sweep
- [x] PLAN §"The macro triage" rewritten where it recorded the I.1 route. Suite **2740** green (2731 + 9), analyze clean, browser gate green

## Phase 140 — M-P0: the threading decision

The milestone gate PLAN puts before any prover code. Scoped as a **spike**, like Phase 101: the deliverable is a measurement and a pinned decision, not an engine — there is no prover to drive yet, and an abstraction with no consumer is what this project keeps deciding not to build on spec. Decision recorded in PLAN §"The prover yields with a MessageChannel, not a timer".

- [x] **The premise, checked rather than assumed.** `Isolate.run` **compiles cleanly** for both web targets and fails at *runtime* — `UnsupportedError: new RawReceivePort` (dart2js), a trap (dart2wasm). The compiler will not catch an isolate smuggled into shared code; only a browser run will, so the native path must be reached by conditional import rather than by a capability check
- [x] **A representative synthetic fixpoint** (`benchmark/threading_bench.dart`): a fact database plus rules deriving new facts in rounds to quiescence — DD forward chaining's *cost profile* (a growing set scanned against itself, hash-set membership on the inner loop, a queue that drains and refills), not its geometry
- [x] **The resumable shape is cheap and its cost is budget-independent**: 10% VM, 2% AOT, 11% dart2js, 4% dart2wasm against the straight-line loop, flat from 1 000 to 500 000 rule applications per step. So it is what the prover should be *everywhere*, not a web compromise — and the budget is counted in rule applications rather than milliseconds, the same argument as Phase 139's step budget
- [x] **The decisive measurement, and it had to be taken in a browser.** `await Future.delayed(Duration.zero)` is `setTimeout`, which the HTML spec clamps to 4 ms once five timeouts have nested: **5.07 ms/yield** measured in Chrome, against **0.048 ms** for a `MessageChannel` macrotask — 105×. On the fixpoint that is 16.8 s vs 109 ms on dart2wasm, and dart2js does not finish. Through the channel, sixty 4 ms chunks showed no measurable scheduling overhead. `test/web/yield_cost_web_test.dart`, pinned as properties (the timer shows a clamp; the channel is not clamped like one) rather than as machine-specific numbers
- [x] **The microtask is rejected for the opposite reason** — cheapest of the three (0.036 ms) and worth nothing, because the microtask queue drains *before* the browser renders. Cost is not the property being bought
- [x] **`Isolate.run` on native, measured**: round trip 0.05–0.09 ms, ~4 ns/int to bring a fact database home, 1.00× on a 75–91 ms job (0.16–0.22 ms of boundary). The rule the numbers give: export a job only when it is longer than the round trip; below that the chunked engine already there wins
- [x] **The Web Worker question, answered honestly and only as far as it was measured.** Not needed: chunking cleared the bar a Worker would have been introduced to clear. **Not verified** to work under dart2wasm — that was not measured, and the write-up says so rather than deferring it as folklore. The resumable shape is what keeps the option open
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite green, browser gate green (now carrying the yield test). No `lib/` code — a spike
- [x] **Carried into M-P1**: the `MessageChannel` yield needs a real home (`dart:js_interop`, behind a conditional import beside an `Isolate.run` native path) the first time an engine actually chunks. It is pinned here as a measurement, not shipped as an API — **landed in Phase 143** (`lib/domain/prover/event_loop_yield.dart`), when M-P2b's engine became the first consumer

## Phase 141 — M-P1: predicates + the numeric filter

The prover milestone's first code phase (PLAN §M-P1). The vocabulary DD forward chaining will range over, and the model filter that keeps it tractable: never attempt a deduction that is not numerically true in the diagram. The filter generalizes `point_coincidence.dart`'s perturbation probe from "same position" to "predicate survives perturbation" — V1 already built Cinderella's randomized theorem test; this is that test given the prover's vocabulary.

- [x] **The predicate vocabulary** in `lib/domain/prover/`: `coll / para / perp / cong / cyclic / eqangle / eqratio / midp / simtri / contri` — a kind with a fixed arity plus `GeoPoint` arguments, arity enforced as a programmer-error contract. Canonical forms (the eqangle/eqratio keying) are deliberately **not** here — they are M-P2's fact-database concern, and `Predicate` says so: it compares by identity
- [x] **Numeric evaluators as pure functions over chart positions** (`numeric_checks.dart`), dimensionless residuals against one shared tolerance (1e-6, deliberately the coincidence probe's magnitude), sitting between a true predicate's float noise and the smallest violation a probe leaves. Degenerate arguments answer conservatively (a zero direction has no parallel, a degenerate triangle is similar to nothing, a coincident pair determines no circle); the one deliberate exception is `coll` on coincident points (true — they impose no constraint a line could fail); an undefined point makes every predicate false. `simtri`/`contri` are **orientation-free** (side ratios / SSS); if M-P2's rules need Newclid's direct/reflected split it goes beside the ratio check, said at the site
- [x] **The filter samples configurations once, not once per predicate.** `DiagramFilter.probe` perturbs every mutable root, recomputes, snapshots every point's position, restores bit-exactly — `probeCount` times, up front. `holds(predicate)` then answers from the stored configurations, so M-P2 can screen thousands of candidate deductions against one sampling at no recompute cost. A point undefined in any configuration answers false; a predicate over a point the diagram never sampled throws
- [x] **The root machinery extracted, not duplicated**: `lib/domain/construction/mutable_roots.dart` (collect `FreePoint`/`PointOnObject` roots, perturb from a saved base — never cumulatively — restore bit-exactly, plus `recomputeCarriers`), re-pointing `point_coincidence.dart` at it with behaviour unchanged — same constants, same RNG draw order
- [x] **Euclidean-only, refused rather than approximated**: the vocabulary measures in the Euclidean chart (parallelism, congruence, angle equality), so a proper absolute is refused at `probe` — a CK prover re-founds the predicates at this boundary, the same place M-CK re-founded measurement
- [x] Tests mirroring under `test/domain/prover/` (43 new): per-predicate exact-true / perturbed-false / degenerate / scale cases with glados properties; the filter keeping structural truth (Varignon parallels *and* congruences, a glued point's collinearity, two glued radii of one circle) and killing accidental truth (a free point placed exactly on a midpoint — `holdsNow` true, `holds` false); bit-exact restore, with and without an undefined dependent in the graph; both refusals
- [x] Gates: suite **2799** green (2756 + 43), analyze clean, browser gate green, wasm build compiles

## Phase 142 — M-P2a: canonical forms and the fact database

M-P2 (PLAN §M-P2) split into three, because keying, rules and proof surfacing are three sessions in one box and the first is what the other two are built on: a rule engine written against identity-compared predicates would have to be rewritten. **142 is the keying and the store**; M-P2b is the ~20-rule DD core and the resumable fixpoint; M-P2c is the proof DAG surfaced as a readable derivation. PLAN calls eqangle/eqratio canonicalization "the fiddly part" and it is the whole of this phase's difficulty.

- [x] **`Fact` — a predicate reduced to its orbit under its own symmetries** (`lib/domain/prover/fact.dart`): same (kind, points) shape as `Predicate`, same arity contract, but with value equality, so a `Set<Fact>` *is* the database. The canonical form is the lexicographically least argument order in the symmetry group of the kind, ordered by point `id`
- [x] **The symmetry group per kind, named rather than assumed**: `coll`/`cyclic` fully symmetric; `para`/`perp`/`cong` sort within each pair then between the pairs; `midp` fixes the midpoint and sorts the ends; `simtri`/`contri` admit any permutation applied to *both* triples, plus the triangle swap (12 forms) — orientation-free, so nothing else is needed; `eqangle`/`eqratio` sort within each of the four segments and then take the least of the **eight** arrangements of the 2×2 matrix `[[s1,s2],[s3,s4]]`, which is D4 generated by the row swap (the two sides commute), the column swap (negate/invert both) and the **transpose** (`∠(l1,l2)=∠(l3,l4) ⟺ ∠(l1,l3)=∠(l2,l4)`, and `s1/s2=s3/s4 ⟺ s1/s3=s2/s4`) — the one that is easy to miss and doubles the collapse
- [x] **The symmetry table is pinned against the evaluators, not against itself.** Every form the canonicalizer collapses must be *numerically* the same statement: the test asserts both that the forms key alike and that `holdsNow` agrees across them on random configurations. A canonical form that merged two different statements would silently make the prover unsound, and only the evaluator can catch it
- [x] **`FactDatabase` with provenance from day one** (`fact_database.dart`): insert-or-ignore keyed on the canonical fact, insertion order preserved, and every entry carries a `Derivation` — a hypothesis, or a rule name with its premises. **First insert wins**, which is not a tie-break but the acyclicity argument: a fact's derivation is recorded before anything derived *from* it exists, so the proof DAG M-P2c reads can never contain a cycle. `add` throws on a premise the database does not already hold, making that closure structural rather than hoped-for
- [x] **Deliberately deferred, with the consumer named**: no index by kind (M-P2b's rule matcher is what would justify one), no rule objects (a name is a `String` the database does not interpret), no hypothesis extraction from a construction, no filter coupling — the database stores what a rule derived; screening candidates stays `DiagramFilter`'s job
- [x] Tests mirroring under `test/domain/prover/` (43 new): per-kind orbit collapse against a table written out independently of the implementation's; the numeric pin over 24 random similarities of each true rig plus a broken rig; idempotence; eight plausible non-symmetries that must key apart *and* evaluate false; id-ordering independent of construction order; the database's keying, first-insert-wins, premise closure and iteration order
- [x] **Mutation-checked** against a committed baseline: dropping the transpose family fails exactly the two eight-point collapse tests, dropping the triangle swap exactly `simtri`/`contri`, dropping the pair swap exactly the three four-point ones plus the `Set` test, sorting `midp`'s three arguments exactly its non-symmetry test; adding a non-symmetry to the *test's* own table fails the numeric pin, which is the evidence that pin has teeth; last-insert-wins and an unchecked premise each fail exactly their own test
- [x] Gates: suite **2842** green (2799 + 43), analyze clean, browser gate green

## Phase 143 — M-P2b: the DD rule core and the resumable fixpoint

The second third of M-P2 (PLAN §M-P2): the rules and the engine that runs them, built on Phase 142's fact database and Phase 141's filter. Hypotheses are read off the construction, the ~20-rule DD core comes from the open DDAR/Newclid sources (rules, not code), every candidate deduction is screened through `DiagramFilter` before insertion, and the whole thing runs to quiescence in Phase 140's resumable shape with a rule-application budget. This is also the phase where the `MessageChannel` yield stops being a measurement and becomes an API, because an engine that actually chunks is its first consumer.

- [x] **Hypothesis extraction** (`lib/domain/prover/hypotheses.dart`): read the predicate vocabulary off a construction's parent ties, never its positions — incidence-shaped facts (`coll`/`cyclic`/radius-`cong`) through `incidence.dart`'s zero-epsilon relation, per-kind statements for every kind with a pointwise theorem (midpoints, reflections, projections, triangle centers, the bisector lines, Thales on a diameter circle, the compass transfer, the harmonic eqratio). Point-tuple vocabulary means a line's facts need witness pairs — a carrier with one known point contributes nothing, honestly. Euclidean only, refused rather than approximated. The test's spine: every emitted hypothesis must survive the filter on the rig that emitted it
- [x] **The rule core** (`rule.dart`): 23 DD rules from the DDAR/Newclid sources as data — premises and conclusion over pattern variables in the sources' own notation, a conclusion only naming variables its premises bound. `simtri → eqangle` deliberately absent: orientation-free `simtri` admits the reflected pair that negates every mod-π angle. The sources' `ncoll` side conditions are carried by the filter screen, and the screen test pins that (a structurally-true collinear eqangle fires `isosceles_converse` and is refused)
- [x] **The engine** (`rule_engine.dart`): semi-naive chaining — each fact pivots once, remaining premises joined over the facts stored when its turn began, binding against every spelling of a stored fact (`orbitArguments`, the full orbit with within-segment swaps the canonicalizer sorts away). Every candidate conclusion screened through `DiagramFilter` before insertion; structural degeneracy refused at binding time without spending budget. The per-kind index lives in the engine — the consumer M-P2a named. `step(budget)` counted in rule applications, `step(1)` chunking bit-identical to a straight run (pinned). **`Isolate.run` wrapping is deferred with the consumer named**: facts key by point identity, so crossing an isolate needs an id-based transfer, and that design belongs to the first UI consumer (M-P2c/M-P4) rather than on spec
- [x] **The `MessageChannel` yield's real home** (`event_loop_yield.dart`): `dart:js_interop` arm behind a conditional import beside the native zero-timer arm — never a runtime capability check. `ProverEngine.runChunked` drives `step` through it. The browser gate holds the seam: `test/web/prover_yield_web_test.dart` pins that the *shipped* yield shows no setTimeout clamp (0.033 ms against the timer's 4.582 — the wrong-arm failure is silent everywhere else) and that a chunked browser fixpoint derives the straight run's database
- [x] Tests mirroring under `test/domain/prover/`: per-kind extraction with the filter pin; each of the 23 rules fired on a rig where premises *and* conclusion are structural theorems; the side-condition screen; quiescence, budget adherence, chunked determinism; a depth-three chain (`midp` → `coll` + midline → intercept eqratio) and the Varignon parallelogram via `para_transitive`; every premise strictly older than its conclusion (the M-P2c walk's contract); `orbitArguments` sizes, sameness, and least-element agreement with the canonicalizer
- [x] Gates: suite **2893** green (2842 + 51), analyze clean, browser gate green (now carrying `prover_yield_web_test`'s two), wasm build compiles. Mutation-checked: dropping the orbit's transpose family fails exactly the two orbit pins plus the three matcher tests that need transpose spellings (`eqangle`/`eqratio` transitivity, the similarity criteria); removing the filter screen fails exactly the side-condition test. The layer rule fired on the web arm and was extended deliberately: `dart:async` allowlisted, `dart:js_interop` allowed only in `*_web.dart` conditional-import arms

## Phase 144 — M-P2c: the proof DAG, read out and checked

The last third of M-P2 (PLAN §M-P2). Phase 142 stored a `Derivation` with every fact precisely so this walk would be free, and Phase 143 pinned premises-strictly-older-than-conclusion as the walk's contract. So the reading-out is small; the phase's real content is the second half — **a derivation checked against its own rule**, which is the soundness argument the `DiagramFilter` structurally cannot make. The screen says a conclusion is true in the diagram, never that its premises entail it, and Phase 143's STATUS named that hole as the one M-P2c's printed proofs would make visible.

- [x] **The proof walk** (`lib/domain/prover/proof.dart`): a `Proof` extracted for a goal fact — the backward closure over derivations, emitted in post-order so every premise stands above the step that uses it, each fact appearing exactly once so a shared sub-proof is stated once and referenced by number. Iterative, with a cycle guard that throws rather than trusts: acyclicity is structural, and this is the same insurance `FactDatabase.add`'s premise check is. Readable rendering — a numbered step list naming each point by `attributes.name` where it has one and its id otherwise
- [x] **The instantiation check** (`derivation_check.dart`): a derivation re-matched against the rule it names — the recorded premises against the rule's premise patterns *in slot order*, under one binding whose conclusion canonicalizes to the recorded fact. Premise spellings come from `orbitArguments`, the same full orbit the matcher binds against. The engine's slot-order convention is pinned by being relied on. `Proof.verify()` checks every deduction in a proof
- [x] Tests under `test/domain/prover/`: the walk on the Varignon and midline fixpoints (post-order, dedup, the given/deduction split, rendering); a goal that is itself a hypothesis; the cycle guard on a hand-built database; **the pin Phase 143's gotcha named** — a hand-built derivation whose conclusion is true and whose rule name is right but whose premises do not entail it is *rejected*, and every derivation a real fixpoint produces is accepted
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **2913** green (2893 + 20), browser gate green. Mutation-checked: binding premises to the canonical spelling instead of the full orbit fails exactly three tests (the orbit pin, the fixpoint-acceptance sweep, and the proof's certificate test); dropping the conclusion-canonicalizes-to-the-record check fails exactly the two rejection tests that turn on it, plus `verify`'s

## Phase 145 — M-P4a: the prover at the application boundary

M-P4 (PLAN §M-P4) is the milestone's *product* and is worth splitting, per Session 157: the boundary, the step list, the on-figure highlighting. This is the first third — the provider that runs the prover off the live construction — and it is where the things deferred with "the first UI consumer decides" come due.

- [x] **The provider** (`lib/application/providers/prover_provider.dart`): a `@riverpod` notifier holding a sealed `ProverState` (`ProverIdle` / `ProverRunning` / `ProverReady` / `ProverRefused`), driving `hypotheses` → `DiagramFilter.probe` → `seedHypotheses` → `ProverEngine.runChunked` off the live construction. The domain stays Flutter-free; the provider *is* the boundary. **On demand, never on every edit** — PLAN §M-P4's own division of labour: the numeric probe is the always-on cheap path, DD certifies when asked. A non-Euclidean document is a `ProverRefused` state, not an exception thrown at a panel: a CK document is a perfectly good document about which the Euclidean vocabulary has nothing to say
- [x] **Staleness is the consumer's comparison, not a rebuild.** The notifier does not watch the construction — a drag notifies per frame, and a panel emptying every frame is worse than one marked stale. A run records the `revision` it read; a consumer that already watches the construction compares. Named refinement, deliberately not built: a proof is about the *graph*, and only the filter's screen reads positions, so a drag that moves no parent tie invalidates strictly less than a revision bump says — which needs a structural revision counter that does not exist
- [x] **A run is capped, and the cap is not theoretical** (`ProverEngine.runChunked`'s new `maxApplications`, per-call, resumable). **Measured**: `test/fixtures/provoleas2.json` — 27 objects, 23 hypotheses — is **still deriving after 200 000 applications** (13.0 s on the VM), which is the `eqangle` join blowup Session 156 named as structural and had not yet seen materialize. So exhaustion is a *state*: `reachedFixpoint` false, every derived fact still readable, and `proveMore()` spends another budget on the same engine rather than restarting it
- [x] **The budget is 30 000 applications, ~2 s in the regime where it binds** (~65 µs/application on the blowup rig). Counted in applications, not milliseconds, for the Phase 139/140 reason — a run's outcome must be a function of the document, not of the machine
- [x] **The `Isolate.run` question, settled by measurement — and the answer is "yes, and it is its own slice".** PLAN §"The prover yields with a MessageChannel" states the rule: export a job only when it is longer than the round trip (0.05–0.09 ms). A real document's fixpoint measures **10 ms to well past 13 s** — clearing that bar by three to five orders of magnitude — and chunking, which keeps frames alive, does not make a 13-second answer arrive sooner. What stands between here and there is the id-based fact transfer that keying facts by point *identity* forces, which is a slice of its own
- [x] **Named, not fixed: an application's cost is not flat on the real rule set** (65 µs to 2.3 ms across the fixtures), because the join enumeration that yields no candidate is advanced but never charged. Phase 140's flat-cost premise came from a synthetic whose joins mostly hit. So the cap bounds work in the engine's own unit and the wall-clock it buys is document-dependent — true of Phase 139's drag budget too, and honest rather than fixed here
- [x] Tests (`test/application/providers/prover_provider_test.dart`, 15): Varignon through the provider yields a `Proof` whose `verify()` is empty; the provider's database equals a straight domain-side `ProverEngine.run`; the recorded revision, and an edit that moves it without touching the held run; a non-Euclidean refusal; an exhausted run that reports it, keeps its facts, and resumes to the same fixpoint; a run cleared in flight that never publishes. Plus four `runChunked` cap tests in `rule_engine_test.dart`
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **2932** green (2913 + 19), browser gate green (6). Mutation-checked: dropping the generation guard fails exactly the cleared-in-flight pin; dropping the Euclidean guard and dropping the `ProverRunning` publish each fail exactly their own test; ignoring `maxApplications` fails exactly the three cap tests

## Phase 146 — M-P4b: the proof panel

M-P4's second third: the surface that makes Phase 145's provider a product. A step list rendered from `Proof.steps` — never from `render()`, per Session 157 — with a goal list in front of it, because a proof needs a goal before it can be a proof.

- [x] **`lib/presentation/panels/proof_panel.dart`**, two levels. The list offers what the run *derived* and never a given: a hypothesis is what the figure was built to be, and thirty of them would bury the three statements the prover actually found. They are still in the proofs, above the steps that use them. Picking one shows the numbered steps, statement and reason, citations pointing upwards
- [x] **The selection narrows the list — to statements mentioning *at least one* selected object**, not all of them: a user who selects two points is asking what relates them, and the interesting answer usually names a third
- [x] **Every one of the four `ProverState`s has a rendering**, including the two that are easy to leave as blanks: a non-Euclidean document says *why* rather than showing an empty list, and an exhausted run says it stopped and offers **Keep going** (`proveMore`) — "nothing more was found" would be a different claim, and Phase 145 measured a real document that outruns the budget
- [x] **Staleness is this panel's comparison**, since the provider deliberately does not watch the construction. An edit leaves the findings on screen — they were true of a figure the user recognizes — and turns the run button into *the figure changed, prove again*
- [x] **The pure parts are pure**, the `intersection_report.dart` precedent: `provableGoals`, `stepReason`, `isStale` take no `BuildContext`, because what the panel *says* should not need a widget tree to read. `stepReason` keeps the rule's own name with underscores spaced out — a display-name table for 23 rules is a second spelling that drifts and then lies about which rule ran
- [x] **Docked where there is room, a bottom sheet where there is not** — the object tree's gate. Both drawers are already spoken for, and the panel is read *alongside* the figure, so the compact path takes half the height. The two lists carry distinct `PageStorageKey`s so opening a proof starts at step [1] and coming back lands where the reader left off
- [x] **The wasm smoke now reaches the prover** — `lib/main.dart`'s import graph gets there through this panel, `event_loop_yield.dart`'s conditional import included. That closes the gotcha Sessions 156 and 157 both carried
- [x] Tests (`test/presentation/panels/proof_panel_test.dart`, 12): the three pure functions directly; the app-bar toggle; prove → list → tap → step list checked against `Proof.steps`; an edit that marks stale without clearing; a live selection narrowing; the refusal; the exhausted run's **Keep going** reaching quiescence
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **2944** green (2932 + 12), browser gate green (6), `flutter build web --wasm --release` compiles

## Phase 147 — M-P4c: the step, pointed at on the figure

M-P4's last third, and the milestone's actual payoff: reading a step and seeing what it is about. PLAN §M-P4 asks for on-figure animated highlighting per step, reusing the painter's selection styling.

- [x] **`proofHighlightProvider`** (`lib/application/providers/`): the ids one step is about. A provider because it crosses two widgets with no useful common ancestor — the panel says which step is being read, the canvas draws it — which is `selectionProvider`'s shape and reason. **Deliberately not the selection**: one is what a command would act on, the other is what a sentence is about, and folding them together would let a proof step silently retarget the next tool
- [x] **The painter draws it as the selection halo's geometry at twice the width** (`_highlightExtra` 11 against the halo's 5), under the selection pass, so an object that is both shows both rings. Reusing the styling is not the same as being indistinguishable from a selection
- [x] **The pulse is an `Animation` handed to `CustomPainter`'s `repaint`**, so a tick repaints without rebuilding the canvas — an animation frame changes nothing the build method reads. It runs *exactly* while something is highlighted, driven from the watched state rather than a listener so the controller and the painter cannot disagree; an always-ticking controller would repaint the app's most expensive paint forever. Alpha floors at 45% of peak: an emphasis that blinks out reads as a redraw glitch, not as attention
- [x] **Tapping a step points at it; tapping it again stops** — a highlight the user cannot dismiss is one they have to close the panel to escape. Leaving the proof, picking another goal, re-proving and closing the panel all drop it, the last post-frame because a provider may not be modified inside `dispose`
- [x] Tests: the painter's emphasis at three pulse values and with the null listenable, both rings when highlighted *and* selected, `shouldRepaint` keyed on the set and the listenable but **not** on a tick; the panel's tap/untap, the three ways it drops, and the pulse read straight off the painter — the value moving *is* the animation, and `hasRunningAnimations` is not a usable probe here (the editor always has a ticker somewhere)
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **2950** green (2944 + 6), 32 goldens byte-identical, browser gate green (6), `flutter build web --wasm --release` compiles

## Phase 148 — M-P4d: asking, and the three answers

The panel derives everything and lists what it found — "what follows from this figure". What it cannot do is take a question. JGEX's model is the other way round: state a conclusion, get a proof. This phase adds the question, and it is cheap because DD is *already* not goal-directed — DDAR runs forward to quiescence and then checks membership, so `proofOf` is the second half and only the asking is missing.

**The answer is three-way, and that is the phase's point.** The numeric filter sits beside the prover, so "false" and "unprovable" are separable, and they are very different news:

| answer | means | who says so |
|---|---|---|
| refuted | not a theorem — a perturbation breaks it | `DiagramFilter`, with no run at all |
| unproved | true in the model, past the DD core's reach | membership miss on a *complete* run |
| proved | here is the proof, and `verify()` says certificate | `Proof.of` |

`test/fixtures/perp-true-unproved.rgl` is the middle row, from a user document: right angle at B, D = mid AB, E = mid DB, the perpendicular to CA through E meeting BC at F. `perp(C,D,D,F)` holds in every perturbation and the 44-application fixpoint cannot reach it — **structurally**, since only two of the 23 rules conclude a `perp` (`para_perp_perp` needs a parallel that does not exist here, `perp_bisector` degenerates). The human proof ends in `∠CDF = ∠BDF + ∠BDC`, which *adds* two angles; `eqangle` can only say two angles are equal. That is the AR half of DDAR — **M-P3** — and this fixture is now the thing asking for it.

- [x] **What a selection can phrase** (`lib/domain/prover/questions.dart`, pure): selected objects → point pairs (a selected pair of points is one; a carrier contributes every pair of points `structurallyIncident` on it, as `hypotheses.dart` already does) → the askable questions. Two pairs give `para` / `perp`, plus `cong` when both pairs actually bound a length rather than merely witnessing a direction. Three points give `coll` and the three `midp` readings; four points give `cyclic` and the three pairings of `para` / `perp` / `cong`. `eqangle` / `eqratio` / `simtri` / `contri` are **not** selection-shaped (eight points, or a correspondence a selection cannot express) and are left out, said rather than silently missing. A carrier with fewer than two known points phrases nothing — honest, since DD could do nothing with an unnamed line either
- [x] **A question is a statement, not a spelling — and this is the part that is easy to get wrong.** `para(A,B,C,D)` and `para(A,X,C,D)` are *different facts* even with `A`, `B`, `X` collinear, because the core has no coll-propagation rules (Session 156's note, and the same gap that blocks `perp-true-unproved.rgl`). So a question asked with one witness pair would miss a proof spelled with another. A `ProverQuestion` therefore carries **every** equivalent spelling, and is proved when *any* of them is in the database: which points name a line is the prover's business, not the user's
- [x] **Early exit** (`ProverEngine.runChunked`'s `stopWhen`): with a goal there is no need for quiescence, only for one fact. Checked between chunks, so a run overshoots by less than `chunkBudget`. **Measured on `provoleas2.json`, the document that never reaches quiescence: 1 000 applications and 17.7 ms to answer, against 30 000 and 776.6 ms that still answers nothing** — asking is 44× cheaper than deriving *and* it terminates, which is the whole argument for doing this before the isolate work
- [x] **`ProverNotifier.ask(Predicate)`**: refuted straight from the filter with no run; otherwise run with the goal as the stop condition, reusing a held run when one is already complete at this revision. The answer distinguishes *unproved* from *not yet*: a budget-exhausted run has not shown anything about reachability, and saying "unprovable" there would be a lie
- [x] **Selection-driven UI** in `proof_panel.dart`: with a selection that phrases something, the panel offers those questions as chips *above* the derived list — "what follows from this figure" and "does this hold?" are both useful, and a selection does not say which one the reader wants. Chips name their points, because four selected points phrase three different perpendicularity questions and a row all reading "perpendicular?" would be a coin toss. A proved answer drops into the existing step rows with the existing on-figure emphasis: the proof of an asked question is not a different kind of object from the proof of a listed one. One back affordance, in the header — not at the foot of a proof the reader would have to scroll past
- [x] Tests: `questions.dart` over selections of points, segments, lines and mixtures (including what it refuses to phrase); the early exit reaching the same answer as a full run and costing less on the blowup fixture; the three answers on rigs that produce each — Varignon for *proved*, `perp-true-unproved.rgl` for *unproved*, a false claim for *refuted*; the panel's chips and the three renderings
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **2979** green (2950 + 29), 32 goldens byte-identical, browser gate green (6), `flutter build web --wasm --release` compiles

## Phase 149 — the panels open when the user opens them

Two UI corrections, both user requests, and they turn out to be the same correction.

- [x] **The proof button moves beside the geometry menu.** Both answer questions *about* the document rather than editing it, and the pairing is not only visual: the prover's vocabulary is Euclidean, so which geometry the globe names decides whether it can speak at all
- [x] **Style & properties is opened by its button, not by the selection.** It used to appear the moment anything was picked, which meant a tap on the canvas *resized the canvas under the pointer* mid-gesture. Now it is a docked panel behind an app-bar toggle, exactly like the object tree, and a bottom-sheet-free drawer on compact — the path that was already there for phones, promoted to being the only path
- [x] **The button is always shown, and the empty panel says what to do.** The alternative — showing it only with a selection — was rejected for the reason the object tree already records: a toolbar whose buttons come and go is a toolbar you cannot aim at, and a panel the user opened and that then vanished on a deselect reads as broken. So `AttributesInspector` no longer collapses to `SizedBox.shrink()`; with nothing selected it renders "Select an object to style it."
- [x] Tests updated where the behaviour genuinely changed rather than worked around: the inspector harness opens the panel the way the user does; the two collapse tests become "opened by its button, not by the selection" and "clearing the selection leaves the panel open and empty"; both `app_bar_layout_test` cases; the object-tree hand-off; the three shortcut tests that reach the name field
- [x] **Follow-up (user request): the style button sits between hide/delete and undo**, which puts it with the buttons that act on the selection rather than with the view chrome. It is now far enough right that the app bar scrolls to it on a phone — consistent with undo/redo, which are in the same position, and with Phase 47's decision that narrow windows scroll the bar rather than re-arrange it
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **2984** green, 32 goldens byte-identical, browser gate green (6)

## Phase 151 — M-P2d: the incidence closure (done)

The first of the two structural gaps Phase 150 found behind its three rules (PLAN §"Two closures the rule table is standing in for"). Ahead of M-P3 in value, and the cheapest of the three open prover phases.

**Split, and the split is what caught the error.** 151a landed the closure with no consumer; 151b was to move the matcher and delete the rules it subsumed. Measured separately, the matcher move turned out to be a **regression on every fixture** — had it shipped together with three rule deletions, the cause would have been unreadable. 151c then chased the fact key instead, and a third measurement closed that too: **the closure's consumer is M-P3.** So the phase ships its structure and hands the rest to Phase 152, which is now next.

### 151a — the carrier index (done)

- [x] **Lines and circles as objects carrying their point sets** (`lib/domain/prover/carriers.dart`), merged by union-find over the tuples that name them — pairs for a line, triples for a circle, which is the classical soundness argument and the only difference between the two closures. A `Fact` keeps its point-tuple form: the vocabulary and the save format do not move, and nothing here writes to a `FactDatabase`
- [x] **The invariant that makes a lookup a map read**: every arity-subset of a class's point set maps to that class, maintained by re-keying after each merge. So a merge that makes two previously unrelated names equal is found when the points are re-keyed, not later by a rule — and `lineThrough` is total, a pair no `coll` has mentioned answering the carrier of exactly those two points
- [x] **Incremental, because the engine is**: `absorb` takes one fact at a time and lands where a rebuild from the whole database lands, in any arrival order (pinned both ways)
- [x] **Measured against the rule set as it stands**, before anything moved — and read too optimistically at the time, see 151b. On `perp-true-unproved.rgl` (+ its auxiliary point) 8 `coll` facts are 2 line carriers of 4 points each, and `coll_transitive` + `perp_coll` account for 9 of the 41 facts; on `provoleas2.json` 11 `coll` facts are 2 carriers (5 and 4 points) and the two rules account for 11 of 75. **A full closure rebuild over `provoleas2`'s 75 facts costs 41 µs** — less than one average rule application (Session 158 measured 65 µs to 2.3 ms). The closure is cheap; what 151b then found is that cheapness was never the binding constraint
- [x] **`cyclic_fifth_point` fires on no fixture**, so the circle closure has no measured consumer today — recorded as `para_coll`'s status was, rather than claimed as a saving
- [x] Tests (`test/domain/prover/carriers_test.dart`, 19): the empty closure's total lookup; merge on a shared name and *no* merge on a shared point (a pencil is not a line); the merge only the re-keying finds; `coll_transitive`'s and `cyclic_fifth_point`'s content as unions; what it refuses (`para`/`perp`/`midp`, a repeated point); incremental = rebuilt, forwards and backwards; and a sweep over a real run asserting every `coll` the rule derived is already a lookup in the closure built from the facts it did *not* derive
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **3003** green (2984 + 19)

### 151b — where the closure goes, settled by measurement (done — as a negative)

The step that was going to move the matcher, and the measurement that says not to. **Nothing shipped but the table and the finding**, which is the `para_coll` precedent from Phase 150: written, rigged, measured, deleted, recorded.

- [x] **The slot-shape table** (`lineSlots` in `carriers.dart`): which argument pairs name a *line* and may therefore be resolved through the closure. `para`/`perp` both pairs, `eqangle` all four (it reads mod π); `cong`/`eqratio` **none**, because a pair there names a segment and a segment has a length its carrier does not. Pinned against `Predicate.holdsOn` over a seeded sweep, driven off the table rather than restating it — mutation-checked: listing `cong`'s pairs fails on a numeric counterexample. Kept, because it is correct and is the substrate whichever way the design goes
- [x] **The carrier-aware matcher was built, flagged and measured — and reverted.** Resolving a premise's line slots through the closure, rule table unchanged: `locus3` **162 applications and quiescent → 4 000 000 and still running** (35 s), 29 facts against 15; `apatitos-topos` 632 apps / 1.3 s → past 30 000 / 11.7 s. Removing `coll_transitive` / `perp_coll` / `cyclic_fifth_point` on top *loses* facts — 16 on `perp-true-unproved.rgl` including the `perp` the JGEX proof turns on, 32 on `provoleas2.json`
- [x] **The mechanism, which is the reusable part.** Identity matching *prunes*: a premise bound to the wrong point fails at once and its join subtree is never walked. Carrier matching removes the pruning while the conclusion side still emits point tuples, so every newly-successful join yields another *spelling*, which is stored, which pivots, which joins more loosely still. On the premise side alone the closure is a fact multiplier — "cheaper to match" is not "fewer matches"
- [x] **PLAN §"Two closures…" amended first**, since the sentence being corrected ("deletes rules, deletes the spelling search, makes every later rule cheaper") is a PLAN claim
- [x] **`coll_transitive` and `perp_coll` stay.** They are the patch, and the patch is measurably cheaper than the half-closure that was going to replace it. `questions.dart`'s all-spellings search stays with them

### 151c — the carrier-reduced fact key (closed, unbuilt: M-P3 is the consumer)

What M-P2d looked like it wanted after 151b: `perp(A,B,C,D)` and `perp(E,F,C,D)` as one database entry. Three measurements, all taken off a baseline run with no engine change, closed it instead.

- [x] **The upside is real and concentrated.** `provoleas2.json` — the fixture that never reaches quiescence — holds **53 line-shaped facts that are 8 distinct statements**; its 75 facts reduce to 30, and `perp-true-unproved.rgl` 41 → 28. The three fixtures that already converge collapse by **nothing**
- [x] **The rule table cannot take a plain reduced key.** 10 of 26 rules have a variable in both a line role and a point-or-segment role (`perp_bisector`, `inscribed_angle` and its converse, `isosceles_base` and its converse, `midline_para`, `intercept_eqratio`, `sas_simtri`, `aa_simtri`, `perp_coll`). `perp_bisector` — `cong(o,a,o,b) & cong(p,a,p,b) => perp(o,p,a,b)` — binds `a`, `b` as segment ends and then names them as a line. A design that survives it is narrower: one entry per carrier-statement *carrying its witness spellings*, enumerating only at spanning variables
- [x] **And then the third measurement made it moot.** Split the line-shaped facts by kind: `provoleas2` is para 18 / perp 11 / eqangle 24, `perp-true-unproved` 9 / 12 / 3, `apatitos-topos` 0 / 1 / 13. **Every line-shaped kind is a kind AR absorbs** — and that is the same observation from the other end, since `lineSlots` is non-empty for exactly the predicates whose arguments name *lines*, which is what θ is a variable over. After M-P3, `para`/`perp`/`eqangle` are not facts, and `lineSlots` is empty for every kind that remains. A witness-set database would deduplicate facts M-P3 deletes
- [x] **So M-P2d's consumer is M-P3, not a fact-key redesign**, and PLAN's §M-P3 bullet says so. The ordering was backwards: incidence went before AR because it looked cheaper, and it *is* cheaper — but only the half that was already free (Phase 151a). `coll_transitive`, `perp_coll` and `questions.dart`'s all-spellings search stay until AR lands

## Phase 152 — M-P3: the angle and ratio algebra (closed: every sub-phase done, 152e's deferred box closed in Phase 174)

The second structural gap, and the real M-P3 (PLAN §M-P3, rewritten). Not a second rule engine — a linear system. After Phase 151 it is also M-P2d's consumer: θ is indexed per *carrier*, so `carriers.dart` is what it is built on.

**The arithmetic was settled before any code** — PLAN §"AR is a ℤ-module, not a ℚ-vector space". θ is defined mod π, so a linear form is a statement only when its coefficients are **integers**; every equation the vocabulary produces starts at ±1, but elimination does not keep them there. Two ordinary `eqangle` facts — `∠(l₁,l₂) = ∠(l₃,l₄)` and `∠(l₁,l₂) = ∠(l₄,l₃)` — sum to `2θ₂ − 2θ₁ = 0`, which over ℚ pivots to `para(l₁,l₂)` and over ℤ says only `∠(l₁,l₂) ∈ {0, π/2}`: parallel **or** perpendicular. Rational elimination turns a disjunction into a theorem, and the numeric filter would wave it through on any diagram sitting on the parallel branch. So: integer row reduction, entailment is ℤ-span membership, a `2θ` row publishes **nothing**, and log-lengths are a separate genuinely-ℚ system.

### 152a — the arithmetic and the closure, pure (done)

- [x] **`Rational`** (`lib/domain/prover/rational.dart`) — exact, `BigInt`-backed. `int` is not the same type on every target this ships to (64-bit on the VM and under dart2wasm, a double-backed 53-bit integer under dart2js), so an int-backed coefficient would overflow *differently* per platform and the browser gate would find it late. Canonical form — positive denominator, coprime — so `==` is value equality and `hashCode` agrees. `modOne` is the ℚ/ℤ reduction the angle constants live in, and `perp` twice over coming back to `0` is `perp_perp_para` arriving as arithmetic
- [x] **The angle closure as a ℤ-module** (`angle_closure.dart`): `AngleEquation` is `Σ cᵥθᵥ ≡ k (mod π)` with **integer** coefficients and the constant in units of π; `AngleClosure` keeps rows in echelon form by *lattice* reduction — never dividing a row by its own leading coefficient, combining two rows through the extended gcd instead. `entails` is ℤ-span membership, and the divisibility test is literally where ℚ and ℤ part company
- [x] **Provenance from day one**, the `FactDatabase` precedent: every row carries the integer combination of *input* equations it is, `entails` answers with that certificate, and `recombine` multiplies it back out — so verifying an AR step is arithmetic, not search. Pinned by a sweep: whatever a tangled closure claims to prove, the certificate recombines to it
- [x] **The `2θ` row is retained and never published.** The counterexample is executable: two `eqangle`s (`∠(l₁,l₂) = ∠(l₃,l₄)` and `∠(l₁,l₂) = ∠(l₄,l₃)`) entail `2θ₂ − 2θ₁ ≡ 0` and every even multiple of it, and entail **neither** `para` nor `perp`. **Mutation-checked**: normalizing an installed row by its coefficient gcd — what a rational pivot amounts to here — fails exactly those two tests and nothing else
- [x] **Asking does not assert**: a refused question leaves the basis, the rank and the input list untouched. A target that cannot be reduced must not quietly extend the closure on its way to being refused
- [x] Pure Dart, no geometry — the predicate → equation mapping is 152b. `para`/`perp`/Chasles/`para_transitive`/`perp_perp_para`/`para_perp_perp`/`eqangle_transitive` all fall out as row arithmetic and are pinned as such
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **3049** green (3008 + 41)

### 152b — the vocabulary mapped onto it (done)

- [x] **`angle_translation.dart`**: `para` / `perp` / `eqangle` / `coll` in as rows, `para` / `perp` out as facts with certificates. `cong` / `eqratio` are the length system's and every other kind is DD's, each answering false rather than being silently skipped
- [x] **A variable is a point *pair*, not a carrier — the plan had this backwards.** Indexing θ by carrier is wrong twice: two pairs on one carrier do have equal θ, but equal θ is *parallel*, not *identical*, so the implication runs one way only; and the carrier moves when carriers merge, which is Phase 151c's mutable-key problem again. So the variable is a stable pair, and `coll` contributes the θ-equalities its own three pairs license — with exact provenance, since one `coll` states each
- [x] **Incidence does not reduce to the algebra, and the boundary is now pinned.** Given `coll(a,b,c)` and `coll(a,b,d)` the closure holds `θ_ab = θ_ac = θ_bc = θ_ad = θ_bd` and says *nothing* about `θ_cd` — not from weakness but because the pair `cd` names no variable. That `c` and `d` are each on line `ab` is an incidence statement and directions cannot express it; `CarrierIndex` is what knows it. The bridge is applied at **query** time in 152c, not eagerly — eagerly means one variable and one published fact per pair on every line, which is Phase 151b's spelling explosion at a new address
- [x] **`CarrierIndex`'s job here is sharper than indexing**: it tells a parallel from an identity, so `para` is never published about a line and itself. Pairs sharing a point are skipped for the same reason
- [x] **`eqangle` is deliberately not enumerated on output** — pairs of variables are quadratic and quadruples quartic, so publishing every entailed `eqangle` would be worse than the blowup AR exists to remove. DD *asks* instead, which is 152c
- [x] **Measured, and the headline is soundness**: across the five fixtures AR published **51 `para`/`perp` facts and not one failed the numeric screen**. Rules falling out as arithmetic with no rule: `perp_perp_para`, `para_perp_perp`, `para_transitive`, `eqangle_transitive`, Chasles — and **`perp_coll`**, the rule Phase 150 added and Phase 151 could not delete
- [x] **And the other half: AR alone is nearly useless, so the exchange is where the value is.** From hypotheses only it misses 20 of DD's 21 `para`/`perp` on `perp-true-unproved.rgl` and 26 of 29 on `provoleas2.json` — because those come from `midline_para`, `perp_bisector` and the inscribed pair, whose premises AR does not read. Fed DD's *fixpoint*, it yields **+21 beyond DD's 21 on `perp-true-unproved` in 2 ms**, and **+6 on `provoleas2` in 3 ms** — the document DD cannot finish in 30 000 applications and 778 ms. `apatitos-topos` and `no-locus` gain nothing
- [x] **AR's output is not a superset of DD's, by design**: fed the fixpoint it still declines 10 of `perp-true-unproved`'s 21, all of them a line against itself or a pair sharing a point. Recorded so the difference is not read as a miss
- [x] Tests (18) + Gates: analyze clean, suite **3067** green (3049 + 18)

### 152c — one `Prover` facade: the interleaved fixpoint (done)

**152b's measurement made this the phase, not the polish.** AR alone reaches almost nothing; fed DD's fixpoint it doubles the angle facts on `perp-true-unproved` in 2 ms. The value is entirely in the exchange, and it runs both ways — AR's new `para`/`perp` are DD pivots, which derive more, which AR reads again.

- [x] **`prover.dart` — DD and AR as peers over one `FactDatabase`.** A pass is: DD steps under the budget, AR reads everything new, AR publishes what it entails, DD takes the publication into its pivot queue. The run ends when DD is quiescent **and** the last pass published nothing, so it is a loop and not a pipeline. `ProverEngine.absorbExternal` is the one addition the engine needed — sound because its `_facts` mirror *is* the database's list, so what it has not seen is the tail
- [x] **AR's output is screened by the same filter DD screens with**, with no exception made. The algebra is believed; a conclusion that is not numerically true in the diagram means something upstream is wrong, and letting it in would hide that
- [x] **The budget counts DD applications only.** AR's cost is not in the same currency — a pass is a bounded elimination measured in single-digit milliseconds on every fixture — and putting two incomparable things behind one number would make the cap mean nothing
- [x] **An AR step is verified by re-derivation, not by re-multiplication** (`derivation_check.dart`). There is no rule to re-match, so the check builds a closure over the recorded premises *alone* and asks whether they entail the conclusion. Checking the stored certificate would be cheaper and would be checking the engine's arithmetic against itself; this is the same choice the file already makes in restating the matcher rather than sharing it. **Mutation-checked**: dropping the entailment test fails exactly the fabricated-step test
- [x] **Measured against DD alone**, cap 30 000 applications: `perp-true-unproved.rgl` **41 → 93 facts**, still quiescent, 202 → 312 ms, 21 angle steps; `locus3.json` 15 → 24, still quiescent, no slower; `provoleas2.json` 75 → 81 at the cap. `apatitos-topos.rgl` and `no-locus.rgl` unchanged and no slower. **Nothing lost on any fixture** — the exchange's fact set contains DD's
- [x] **Every proof of every angle step verifies**, across all 31 of them — and `Proof.of` walks the whole sub-DAG, so that is the DD steps underneath as well
- [x] **The cost is DD applications, and it is real**: `perp-true-unproved` goes 3 906 → 16 034, because AR's conclusions are pivots. It still reaches quiescence. On a document already at the cap (`provoleas2`) AR gets one look instead of several, which is the honest limitation of a budget shared this way
- [x] Tests (14) + Gates: analyze clean, suite **3081** green (3067 + 14)

### 152d — AR reaches the user (done)

- [x] **`prover_provider.dart` runs `Prover` instead of `ProverEngine`** — a rename at the call sites, because the facade was built to the engine's shape (`database`, `filter`, `applications`, `isComplete`, `runChunked`). So M-P3 is now what the proof panel shows, and the wasm smoke reaches it through the same import graph M-P4 opened
- [x] **Measured in the provider's own configuration** (chunk 1 000, cap 30 000): `locus3.json` 100 → 97 ms for 15 → 24 facts, `apatitos-topos.rgl` 1 595 → 1 585 ms unchanged, `perp-true-unproved.rgl` 225 → 391 ms for 41 → 93 facts, `provoleas2.json` 882 → 928 ms for 75 → 81. Thirty AR passes on the worst of them cost 46 ms, so the per-pass cadence is not a problem
- [x] **A bug the widget gate caught and no unit test could have.** `Prover.runChunked` yielded *after* spending its last budget; a yield is a timer, and inside `testWidgets`' fake async a caller that awaits without pumping waits on a timer nobody fires. `ProverEngine.runChunked` takes every exit before the yield for exactly this reason, and the facade now does too. Pinned with `fakeAsync` + `flushMicrotasks` — mutation-checked, removing the guard fails exactly that test
- [x] **And a second, found the same way**: `maxApplications` caps *this call*, not the engine's lifetime. Comparing the lifetime count made `proveMore` a no-op, so *Keep going* kept nothing going
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **3083** green (3081 + 2), `fake_async` declared
- [x] **Browser gate extended, and for a reason this phase created** (`test/web/angle_closure_web_test.dart`, 6 tests, gate 6 → 12). AR is the first thing here whose correctness rests on **exact integer arithmetic**, and integers are the one primitive that is not the same type per target — 64-bit on the VM and dart2wasm, a double-backed 53-bit integer under dart2js. `Rational` is `BigInt`-backed for that reason; this is what says the reason was honoured. The ℤ-vs-ℚ decision is pinned there too, because a target where coefficients quietly went through a double would pass every VM test and publish `para` for a figure that is only parallel-or-perpendicular
- [x] **Looked at in a real browser** on the deployed build: the proof panel opens by its button (Phase 149), the prover runs, a Varignon proof renders readably (`para transitive from [3], [6]`), and tapping a step highlights exactly its points on the figure (Phase 147). Session 159's UI is no longer unseen

### 152e — what the facade still owes (closed: one deferred box)

- [x] **DD asks AR for `eqangle` — measured, and the box had the cause wrong.** The premise was that AR entails `eqangle`s DD never stores, so a premise slot should resolve against the closure. **The first half is true and larger than expected**: AR entails 129 / 89 / 33 / 272 / **2527** `eqangle` statements beyond the database across the five fixtures — and **not one is refused by the numeric filter**, which is another soundness data point for the algebra. It is also why AR must not *publish* them: 2527 facts on `provoleas2` would drown the database
- [x] **The second half is false. Nothing consumes them.** Both readings were simulated against the real rule table. The *safe* form — an `eqangle` slot resolved only when every variable is already bound by another premise, so the ask is a **guard** and not a generator, which is where Phase 151b's blowup came from — applies to `sas_simtri` and `aa_simtri`: **0 new `simtri` on every fixture**, over 24 / 104 / 8 / 16 / 156 asks. The *generator* form — every entailed `eqangle` offered to the single-premise converses — gives `inscribed_converse` and `isosceles_converse` **0 new `cyclic` and 0 new `cong`, on every fixture**, from all 3 050 entailed statements
- [x] **So the ask is not what `apatitos-topos.rgl` is missing.** It is not that AR cannot state that document's angle equalities — it entails 89 beyond the 13 stored. It is that `eqangle`'s only consumers are `eqangle_transitive` (whose conclusions AR already subsumes), the two converses and the two `simtri` rules, and none of them finds a new instance. **The gap is in the rule table's consumers of `eqangle`, not in the exchange**, and a fixture whose theorem *is* an inscribed-angle chase would be the thing that shows it — the corpus does not contain one
- [x] Enumerating the entailed set costs 121 / 765 / 7 / 98 / 1 329 ms per pass, which is the quartic the box warned about. Worth recording as measured rather than feared, and irrelevant while the payoff is zero
- [x] **The incidence bridge — closed as a negative, measured before building.** The plan: when a query names a pair no fact mentions, `CarrierIndex` says which carrier it is on and the θ-equality is added for that pair alone. The upside was measured first, as the **eager** version — every pair on every named carrier registered and equated, which is the upper bound on anything a query-time bridge could unlock. Across all five fixtures it publishes **zero** `para`/`perp` the exchange does not already hold (`locus3` 3 bridge equations, `apatitos-topos` 3, `no-locus` 2, `perp-true-unproved` 4, `provoleas2` 9 — new facts 0, 0, 0, 0, 0). That covers the query side too, since the enumeration ranges over the bridged variables: there is no question the bridge would let AR answer that it cannot answer today
- [x] **Why nothing — the mechanism, which is the reusable part.** `hypotheses()` already emits every witness pair, and `coll_transitive` / `perp_coll` are precisely the patch for this hole on the DD side. The bridge is a *second* patch for the same hole, so it can only pay where the first one is removed — which the next box says it is not. Recorded the way Phase 151b's finding was: written, rigged, measured, deleted
- [x] **What an AR step *reads* as — done (`angle_chase.dart`).** The DD steps always read well (`[6] para(E, D, A, D)  midline_para from [3], [5]`); it was the AR line that was a black box: `[9] perp(E, A, F, C)  angle_arithmetic from [1], [2], [4], [6], [8]` names its premises and explains nothing. A step *is* a sum of relations, so the sum is what is shown — one line per equation, in `θ` language, under the step and indented, in the printed proof and in the panel alike:

      [9] perp(E, A, F, C)  angle_arithmetic from [1], [2], [4], [6], [8]
            θ(AB) = θ(CB) + π/2  [1]
            θ(CB) = θ(FC)        [2]
            θ(AD) = θ(AB)        [4]
            θ(ED) = θ(AD)        [6]
            θ(EA) = θ(ED)        [8]
            ⟹ θ(EA) = θ(FC) + π/2

- [x] **Per input, not per premise fact — the box's own warning, honoured.** A multiple beside each cited fact cannot be written down: a `coll` contributes two equations and a certificate may weight them differently. A certificate indexes *equations* and each equation has exactly one source fact, so a chase line is one equation citing one step, and two lines citing the same step is the honest rendering of a `coll` rather than a duplicate
- [x] **Re-derived, never carried.** The chase is rebuilt from the recorded premises the way `derivation_check.dart` rebuilds the entailment — nothing is stored on the `Derivation`, the save format does not move, and a chase that cannot be re-derived is null rather than a rendering claiming more than it can support
- [x] **Three decisions that are not formatting.** Lines read out in *citation* order, not certificate order, because a proof's citations point upwards and the eye should not jump back (ties keep certificate order, so a `coll`'s two relations come out in the order it states them). The `θ(AB)`/`θ(A,B)` separator is decided once per chase — with single-letter points the geometric spelling is what a reader expects, and with an id like `aux` in play it would run two names into a third. A doubled row renders as `2θ(AB) = 2θ(CD)`, so the ℤ-module decision is visible in the reading rather than silently halved
- [x] Tests (24) + Gates: analyze clean, suite **3107** green (3083 + 24), browser gate 12. Mutation-checked both ways: dropping the certificate scaling fails `isSound`, dropping the citation sort fails the ordering test
- [x] **Browser gate 12 → 13, and looked at live.** The chase is the one thing in a proof written in characters the rest of the app never uses — `θ`, `π`, `⟹`, which live in three different Unicode blocks and reach the page as string literals through whichever web backend is compiling. That is a claim about the target, so it is pinned in `test/web/`. On the deployed build a Varignon proof renders its seven steps cleanly with **no chase lines on any DD step** — the "a DD step is left alone" property, live. Varignon derives no angle steps at all (measured in session 161), so seeing a chase in a browser needs the JGEX document loaded through the file picker, which browser automation cannot drive; that is why the gate carries it instead
- [x] **The length system — deferred on a measurement, re-measured in Phase 155e, still deferred — and the deferral ended in session 174 (the ratio box below; Phase 165 is the build).** The premise below was "a fact about the corpus", and 155 changed the corpus without changing the answer: `entailed == cong` on all six fixtures, so the stored facts are already the pairwise closure and the union-find has nothing to add; and the ratio half is blocked structurally (the direct/reflected split), not by the corpus. Guarded by `length_closure_upside_test.dart`. Original note follows.
- [x] **(152e's original note, superseded by the measurement below.)** `cong` / `eqratio` over log-lengths, genuinely ℚ, no modulus, no branch problem; `Rational` is already what it needs. Fed only `cong`, the closure *is* a union-find over segments, so its whole upside is cheap to simulate without writing it — and it is **0 new `cong` on all five fixtures**. The genuine ℚ content is ratio chains, and the corpus holds **one** `eqratio` fact in total (`perp-true-unproved.rgl`), so that half has essentially no input to work on. Build it when a document needs it; building it now would be building to a corpus that cannot exercise it. **Phase 155 is what re-opens this measurement**: tangent-length equality manufactures `cong` and power-of-a-point manufactures ratio chains, so the deferral's premise is a fact about the corpus and the tangent hypothesis changes the corpus
- [x] **The rule table re-examined — and `coll_transitive` / `perp_coll` stay, again.** `perp_coll` is derivable as arithmetic (pinned in 152b), so the deletion looked safe; it is not. With AR running, dropping both from the table **loses facts on three of five fixtures**: `apatitos-topos` 30 → 28, `perp-true-unproved` 50 → 39, `provoleas2` 81 → 57, nothing gained anywhere. Adding the eager bridge on top recovers **12 of `provoleas2`'s 24 and none of `perp-true-unproved`'s 11**. So the two mechanisms are partial substitutes for each other rather than a replacement, and neither combination reaches what the rule table reaches
- [x] **And the losses split in a way that names the real blocker.** They are `coll` (2 / 2 / 4) and `para`+`perp` (0 / 9 / 20). **AR cannot publish `coll` at all** — collinearity is an incidence statement, not a statement about directions, so no amount of angle algebra replaces `coll_transitive`. `CarrierIndex` is what knows those facts; publishing them is a *carrier publisher*, which is a different piece of work from AR and is what a future deletion of `coll_transitive` would rest on. The measurement is the finding; the rules stay
- [x] **Deferred with them — and measured in Phase 163, one at a time**: `para_transitive`, `perp_perp_para`, `para_perp_perp` and `eqangle_transitive` are all row sums. `eqangle_transitive` is **deleted** — it lost no non-`eqangle` fact anywhere, and its 128 × 128 orbit join was the whole of `provoleas2`'s non-convergence and most of `apatitos-topos`'s and `tangent-chase`'s run time. `para_transitive` loses nothing either (recorded, not deleted — its own decision). `perp_perp_para` and `para_perp_perp` lose stored `para`/`perp` facts the publisher does not reach, so they stay
- [x] **The ratio half has input now (Phase 163).** With `provoleas2.json` reaching quiescence it holds five `eqratio`s sharing a segment — `|MA|/|AO| = |AO|/|AB|` beside `|AO|/|AN| = |ON|/|AO|` — so the "one `eqratio` in the corpus" premise below no longer holds. Re-measure the ratio closure's upside on that document the way `length_closure_upside_test` measures the `cong` half, before building
- [x] **Re-measured (session 174), and the deferral's premise is gone.** `length_closure_upside_test.dart` now simulates the deferred system itself — a ℚ row reduction over segment log-lengths, fed `cong`, `eqratio` and `midp` — and reads off every `cong` and `eqratio` it entails beyond the database.
