On `provoleas2.json` it entails **one new `cong`, `|AB| = |LO|`**: an integer combination of the intercept fact `|MO|/|ON| = |OB|/|LO|` and the similar-triangle fact `|MA|/|AO| = |AO|/|AB|` with the midpoint `cong`s, carrying a coefficient 2 on `l_AO` that a union-find cannot form; and **43 `eqratio`s** chained through it (2 more on `perp-true-unproved.rgl`, none elsewhere). Not one is refused by the filter — the same soundness data point 152e's `eqangle` measurement made. Two things the measurement settled about the build's shape: **the midpoint constant is not needed** (the same counts without the `ln 2` row, so DDAR's constant column can wait), and the payoff is a `cong`, which is a *published* fact with DD consumers (`cong` feeds `isosceles`, `tangent_lengths`' converse, `sas_simtri`, `contri`), not an ask-only `eqangle`. Pinned per fixture; the build is **Phase 165**

**Not in scope until the algebra exists**: Pythagoras (squared lengths), the `2·∠`/`3·∠` family and the 180° angle sum (a coefficient ring). Named here so they are not mistaken for rule-table work — and note the `2·∠` family is the same coefficient the ℤ-module decision is about, so it arrives as vocabulary, not as a division.

## Phase 153 — auxiliary construction (done)

What JGEX calls A2, and the thing that decides whether the app can answer a user's question unaided. Phase 150's regression needs the midpoint of BC drawn *by hand*; JGEX constructs it and says so in a dialog.

- [x] **The candidate list, in `domain/prover/` rather than written twice** (`auxiliary_points.dart`). Three families in the checklist's order — midpoints of named pairs, feet of perpendiculars, intersections of curves — enumerated deterministically over the objects' own order, so index *i* names the same point in every copy of a document, which is what lets a caller try candidates one at a time on fresh constructions and what lets a measurement cite one. **The dedup is numeric and that is the decision**: some coincidences are structural (a document already naming the midpoint of `AB`) and some are theorems (the foot of a centre on a chord *is* that chord's midpoint), and no structural test sees the second kind. It is the `DiagramFilter` boundary rather than a breach of it — which points are worth *proposing* is a search heuristic, where reading the diagram is sanctioned, and nothing there asserts a predicate. A structural incidence skip was written first and deleted: it failed no test the dedup was not already failing. Mutation-checked, six tests
- [x] **The upside, measured before the search — 2 unlocks of 1 fact from 276 candidates over seven documents.** Every candidate added one at a time to a fresh copy, the exchange run to quiescence, what it reaches beyond the baseline read off (`auxiliary_upside_test.dart`, `benchmark/auxiliary_search_bench.dart`). Both unlocks are on `perp-true-unproved.rgl`, both are midpoints, and both reach **`perp(C,D,D,F)`** — the midpoint of `BC`, which is the point JGEX's dialog names, and the midpoint of `BF` on the same line. The proof verifies and cites the invented point. **So A2 is exactly the mechanism that document needs, and nothing else in the corpus needs it** — the Phase 152e finding a fifth time, not an exception to it
- [x] **The metric is entailment, not the fact set, and the correction is most of the answer.** A first pass compared fact sets and reported unlocks on six of seven fixtures, every one a re-spelling: adding *any* point to `no-locus.rgl` "unlocks" `para(F,G,F,B)`, which is the hypothesis `coll(F,G,B)` in `para` language — the angle closure entails it and declines to *publish* it, a `para` sharing a point being a degeneracy. The screen is `Prover.resolve`, the question a user's *Ask* already goes through. Pinned as a test, because reading the fact set would have made every number in the phase wrong by an order of magnitude
- [x] **The order is settled and the budget is the problem.** Both halves of the box below, measured rather than chosen. *Order*: the 87 feet and 45 intersections unlock nothing on any document, so midpoints-first is now a measurement. *Budget*: a candidate costs a full re-run — probe, hypotheses, exchange — so one pass of an exhaustive search is (candidates) × (baseline run): 78 × 643 ms = **50 s on `provoleas2.json`** against its own 368 ms baseline, worst single candidate **4.4 s**. The probe is not the cost (2 ms of the 50 s); the exchange is. **No ordering makes an exhaustive sweep cheap** — what an ordering buys is finding the point *early*, which is a different claim, and it is the one a search has to be built on
- [x] **When the fixpoint stalls without the goal, propose points and re-run** (`auxiliary_search.dart`). Goal-directed with early exit, and **resumable by cursor** on `ProverEngine`'s Phase 140 precedent, because an attempt is a whole document run: `step()` tries a bounded number of candidates and returns, so a caller drives it in slices and Stop is noticed inside one attempt. It takes the question's *spellings* and reports which it reached — they are one statement, and the proof has to be read off the spelling the run derived. Nothing is mutated: each attempt runs against the document's objects plus one **detached** point, so the goal keeps naming the user's real points and accepting the proposal stays the caller's decision. `reached` is `Prover.resolve`, not membership, since the closures answer `eqratio`/`eqangle` on ask and never publish them. Mutation-checked three ways
- [x] **And the ordering claim, measured on the one document that has an answer**: JGEX's midpoint of `BC` is candidate **4 of 26**, so early exit costs five runs rather than twenty-six. That is what an order buys — not a cheaper sweep, an earlier answer, which is the distinction the budget box turns on
- [x] **`searchForPoint` on the provider, guarded on exactly one verdict.** *Refuted* is settled, *proved* needs nothing, and **undecided has shown nothing about reachability** — `askMore` is what that wants, and searching there would spend a hundred runs to learn what one more budget would. Only a finished run that could not get there is evidence the rules are the obstacle. **User-initiated, never automatic**, on `askMore`'s precedent: an answer that costs nothing today would start costing a minute. One candidate per event-loop turn, progress counted in *candidates* — a search's unit is a whole document run, and adding its applications to the total would report a number that leaps by thousands and means something else. The run behind an invented point is **not published**: it holds facts about a point the figure does not have, and pointing `askMore` and *Show everything derived* at it would be showing the user a document they do not have
- [x] **The proof stays readable when the prover invented a point.** The point carries the document's own next automatic name (`nextNameFrom`), so a step reads `perp(F, D, D, G)` rather than naming a thing with no name — and accepting it is an `AddObjectCommand` and nothing else, since the auto-namer skips an object that already has one. The panel says what was built *above* the proof, where the name it introduces sits next to the steps that use it, with the kind and parents underneath (`Midpoint of B, C`), and **Add it** is an ordinary reversible command. JGEX puts the same sentence in a modal
- [x] **The offer, and its withdrawal.** A row under an unproved verdict as *Keep going* sits under an undecided one. Once a search has tried every candidate the row goes and the message changes — "neither these rules nor any one extra point the prover could construct reaches it" is a **stronger** statement than the rule set's limit, and `searchExhausted` is false after a search the user *stopped*, which has shown only that they stopped it
- [x] **`perp-true-unproved.rgl` does *not* stop being the *unproved* rig, and the box's premise is what was wrong.** It assumed the search would run as part of asking; it does not, for the cost reason above, so `ask` answers `unproved` on that document exactly as Phase 148 shipped it and the three-verdict tests are untouched. The fixture is now the rig for *both* halves — the honest middle verdict, and the point that settles it — which is better than needing a second document. **What the box got right is that its stopgap is spent**: Phase 155's rule box landed, and `tangent-chase.rgl`'s `cong(P,S,P,T)` is *stored* in the baseline run now, exactly as the box predicted. A replacement, if one is ever wanted, is what the sweep finds for free: a statement no single auxiliary point unlocks
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **3369** green (3342 at the phase's start), browser gate **15**

**Not built, and named so it is not mistaken for an oversight**: the search tries **one point at a time**. A theorem needing two auxiliary points is invisible to it and comes back exhausted, and JGEX constructs more than one on harder documents. Nothing in the corpus needs two, so there is no rig for it — which is the same sentence the last five measurements ended on.

## Phase 155 — the tangent hypothesis (done)

`TangentLine` contributes **nothing** to the prover, and it is not in `hypotheses()`'s documented list of kinds that contribute nothing deliberately — which is how the gap should be read. Tangency is JGEX's *Tangent* and it needs no new predicate: a tangent to a circle with a structural centre `O` touching at a named `T` is `perp(O, T, T, P)` for every other named `P` on the line, and the radii `cong` is already emitted. See PLAN §"JGEX's question list, compared".

- [x] **Emitted when the touch point is named** — `perp(O, T, T, P)` for every other named `P` on the line, radii `cong` already covered by the circle case. Two things it needed beyond the obvious: a **named centre**, which a `ThreePointCircle` does not have, so a `CircleCenter` the user drew on it is looked up as the fallback (without it the most natural tangency figure emits nothing); and a **guard on the parent being a circle by construction**, since the tangent to a general conic at `T` is not perpendicular to the join of `T` with the centre — mutation-checked, dropping the guard fails the conic test. The two conditional silences (no named touch point; a conic parent) are written into the doc comment beside the per-kind list
- [x] **Two rules, measured alone against all six fixtures — one kept, one dropped.** **`tangent_lengths`** (`perp(o,s,s,p) & perp(o,t,t,p) & cong(o,s,o,t) => cong(p,s,p,t)`) reads as *Pythagoras*, not as a circle theorem, which is why its premises name no circle. **0 new facts on all five older fixtures**, exactly three on `tangent-chase.rgl`: the theorem, the chord of contact `perp(s,t,o,p)` that `perp_bisector` reaches from it, and one `isosceles_base` angle. A rule with a consumer — the bar `para_coll` failed. **`tangent_chord`** is a genuine theorem (mod-π spelling checked against the filter; the swapped reading is false) and it fires — and it is **dropped**: on the tangency rig it reaches the *same* two substantive facts by the longer road through `isosceles_converse` plus 13 eqangles at ~2× the run time (its `cong × cong` premise pair is the expensive shape); on `apatitos-topos.rgl` it adds 3 facts, **every one an eqangle**; on the other four, nothing. Sixteen eqangles corpus-wide with no consumer — 152e restated. Written, rigged, measured, deleted, numbers left in the table beside the rule that stayed
- [x] **Audited — both contribute, so neither joins the list.** `PolarLine` twice: perpendicular to the centre→pole join, and **La Hire**, which reaches the tangency fact with no `TangentLine` in the figure at all — a named point on both the polar and the circle is a point of contact, so the tangent there passes through the pole. That one has a trap worth stating because getting it wrong emits a *falsehood* rather than nothing: the pair's other point must be the **pole**, not another point of the polar (the tangent at `T` is `T→pole`; the polar is the chord of contact). Checked rather than asserted — with both contact points drawn the filter accepts `perp(O, T0, T0, pole)` and rejects `perp(O, T0, T0, T1)`. `RadicalAxisLine` once: perpendicular to the line of centres; its defining property, equal power, is not in the vocabulary. **Not** emitted: that a point on both circles is on the axis — an incidence, so its place is `incidence.dart`, where it would reach line clipping and hit testing too
- [x] **Re-run, and the deferral stands — with a better reason than it had.** Tangency *does* change the corpus (`tangent_lengths` manufactures a `cong` that was not there), and the closure still buys nothing: **`entailed == cong` on all six fixtures**, so the stored facts already *are* the pairwise closure of their classes and a union-find has no work left. That is stronger than "the corpus does not exercise it". The ratio half stands structurally rather than by corpus: still **one** `eqratio` in the whole corpus, and the tangency document adds none — not for want of a rule but because power of the point needs the direct/reflected split M-P1 defers. **Power of the point was not what tangency was missing**: `simtri(p,s,a,p,b,s)` is *true* in the figure, but the two triangles are oppositely oriented so `aa_simtri`'s shared-angle premise is **false as a mod-π eqangle** (checked, not assumed). Kept as a tripwire in `length_closure_upside_test.dart` — the day `newCong` goes non-zero, that failure is the signal to build it
- [x] **A fixture whose theorem is a tangency chase** — `test/fixtures/tangent-chase.rgl`, v1, 11 objects: two tangents from an external `P` with both contacts drawn (`S`, `T`), plus a secant meeting the circle at `A` and `B`. It carries both of the rule box's theorems — `cong(P, S, P, T)` and power of the point as an `eqratio`. Both hold in the figure; the exchange reaches **43 facts from 18 hypotheses** (2 of them the new tangency `perp`s) and **neither goal is among them**, so the rule box is now a measurement rather than a description. The sharper half: **0 `eqratio`** on a document built around a ratio theorem — the shortage 152e measured is not that the algebra cannot hold ratios but that no rule manufactures one. Joins `v1_migration_corpus_test`'s sweep for free, which pins the round-trip and the v1 stamp

## Phase 156 — the run the user can stop, and the run that does not restart (done)

Reported in session 166, and both halves were defects on a surface that already ships. Both hooks were already in the code and unused — and the measurement box overturned its own premise, which is the phase's real finding (see the last box).

- [x] **A cancel flag, read where `stopWhen` already is.** `ProverNotifier.stop()` sets it (guarded on `ProverRunning`, and reset at the top of every run method so a stop belongs to the run it interrupted); `prove` / `ask` / `proveMore` / `askMore` pass `stopWhen: () => _cancelled || <the existing goal test>` into `Prover.runChunked`. **A stop publishes `ProverReady(reachedFixpoint: false)`** — the identical state a spent budget publishes (an interrupted ask publishes *undecided*) — so *Keep going* resumes it with no second mechanism and no new arm on `ProverState`. Cancellation is **not** supersession: `_generation` drops a superseded run's result, a stopped run publishes one. The header swaps the disabled spinner-button for a Stop button while running
- [x] **`prove()` resumes instead of rebuilding.** The guard is `ask()`'s, generalized: `_heldRevision` reads the revision off `ProverReady` *and* `ProverAnswered` (the two states `_engine` is only ever assigned beside), answers null for `ProverRunning` — reusing the engine an in-flight run may itself be driving would interleave two `runChunked` loops. Pinned by counting applications across two ▶ taps (cumulative 6 on a reuse, back to 3 on a rebuild) and by database identity, plus the third shape: ▶ after an undecided ask continues the ask's engine to the fixpoint
- [x] **The chunk measured — and the premise of the box is wrong, which is the finding** (`benchmark/prover_chunk_bench.dart`, kept). The worst pass is **flat across every candidate budget on `apatitos-topos` (1.4 s) and `tangent-chase` (2.5 s)**, because their cost is the join enumeration that yields no candidate, which is advanced without being charged as an application — no chunk budget bounds it, and a frame-sized worst chunk is unreachable on this corpus. Where applications *are* the cost the budget binds: `provoleas2`'s worst pass is 53 / 36 / 24 / 18 ms at chunk 1000 / 500 / 250 / 125. **`proverChunkBudget` 1000 → 250, the knee**: a dropped frame instead of a freeze on the blowup document, for +17 % wall on the capped run and +7 % applications on `perp-true-unproved` (same fixpoint); 125 buys 6 ms more for another +20 % wall. Full table in the constant's doc comment. The unbounded pass is its own phase — **Phase 161**
- [x] **The unyielded prologue measured: 47–430 µs across all six fixtures** — a floor Stop cannot lower, and one that needs no lowering. Median of five per fixture, in the same bench; recorded beside the constant
- [x] **Progress while running.** `Prover.runChunked` gained `onPass` beside `stopWhen` — once per pass, the final one included, never when the entry checks return (pinned in `prover_test.dart`). `ProverRunning` carries the applications spent so far, republished per pass through a generation-guarded report so a superseded run's progress is dropped with its result; the body renders 'Deriving… N steps so far.' in `_continueTile`'s own unit. A resumed run's first publish starts from the held count, not zero
- [x] **`clear()` got its caller**: File ▸ New and File ▸ Open both drop the held engine with the document (`main.dart`), pinned in `file_menu_test.dart` both ways
- [x] Tests (+13: 6 provider, 2 domain, 2 file-menu, 1 panel, 2 reworked): a stopped run publishes a resumable prefix and, resumed, lands where an uninterrupted domain-side run lands; a stopped ask is *undecided* and `askMore` settles it; a stale stop does not bleed into the next run; the two ▶ reuse pins plus the after-answer one; `onPass` both ways; the Stop button exists only while running (the widget test holds the run mid-flight on its yield timer — the fixture must outlive one chunk, Varignon never does); `clear()` on New/Open. Mutation-checked both ways: a no-op `stop()` fails exactly the three stop tests, dropping `prove()`'s guard fails exactly the two resume tests. Gates: analyze clean, suite **3140** green (3128 + 12, one re-shaped), browser gate 13

## Phase 158 — the derived list, grouped like the object tree (done)

The other half of session 166's readability report: the derived list is a flat column in database order, on the same screen as an object tree that groups by kind under foldable headers. **The pattern is already written** (`object_tree_panel.dart`) — a fixed-order `Map<String, List<T>>` seeded with every bucket, a `switch` over kinds, empty buckets dropped, a header with a chevron and a count-while-folded, and `Set<String> _expanded` as view state.

- [x] **`groupedGoals(...)` as a pure function**, mirroring that shape. Pure and context-free because it is this panel's stated convention — *what it says* should not need a widget tree to read, which is why `provableGoals` and `stepReason` are already top-level. Grouping happens **after** `provableGoals`' selection filter, exactly as the tree filters before bucketing, so a header's count is the filtered count
- [x] **`predicateKindLabel` — the display-name table the vocabulary does not have.** "Collinear points", "Parallel lines", "Perpendicular lines", "Equal lengths", "Concyclic points", "Equal angles", "Equal ratios", "Midpoints", "Similar triangles", "Congruent triangles", in `PredicateKind`'s own declaration order, so header order is fixed rather than data-dependent. Same exhaustiveness argument as Phase 157's last box: a closed enum the compiler checks, not a string that drifts
- [x] **The header mirrors the tree's, it does not share it.** `_GroupHeader` carries select-by-kind — tap replaces the selection with the group, long-press adds — and a *fact* group has no such action. Sharing the widget would drag those semantics along. So: chevron, label, count-while-folded, folding as the only gesture — and since a tap has nothing else to mean here, the whole header row is the fold target rather than the chevron alone. No tooltip on it either: it lives in the lazy list whose hover tooltips Phase 157 had to hold back, and a header has no raw spelling to offer
- [x] **Groups start folded, and a kind with nothing in it has no header — the tree's behaviour in both halves, deliberately not re-litigated.** Folded first means a finished run opens as a per-kind overview of *what sort* of thing it found, and expanding is opting into the list; the count on a folded header is what keeps it from hiding the answer, which is the same job it does in the tree. Folding is view state (`Set<PredicateKind> _expanded` — keyed by the enum rather than the label, since the label is the display decision and the kind is the fact's own), gone when the panel closes, like the tree's. And an empty bucket is dropped before rendering rather than shown as a zero: a header reading "Similar triangles 0" is a claim about the run that the absence of the header already makes better
- [x] **The empty-bucket rule composes with the selection filter, which is where it earns its keep.** `provableGoals` narrows to statements mentioning a selected object, so with a selection live most kinds *are* empty — and the surviving headers are then a direct answer to "what does the prover know about this?". Dropping happens after filtering for that reason, and a selection that leaves every bucket empty must still reach `_goals`' existing 'Nothing derived about the selection' note rather than an empty column
- [x] **The proof step list is *not* grouped, and that is an explicit non-goal.** A proof is a numbered list whose citations point upwards; that ordering is `FactDatabase`'s premises-strictly-older invariant surfaced, and it is what `verify()` certifies. Regrouping it would be saying something different from the certificate — the same reason the panel already renders from `Proof.steps` and never from `Proof.render()`
- [x] **`_continueTile` stays outside the groups**, at the foot of the list: it is about the run, not about a kind
- [x] **A search field is not in this phase.** The tree has one because it lists everything; this list is already narrowed by the selection. Revisit only if a measured run's grouped list is still unreadable — `perp-true-unproved.rgl` reaches **93 facts** under the exchange, so the number to check against exists
- [x] Tests (+10: 2 domain, 8 panel): `groupedGoals` per kind and its fixed order; empty buckets absent; grouping composed with the selection filter; groups folded on first build and the count showing while folded, rows in database order once opened; a kind with no facts having no header at all, with and without a selection; the all-empty selection reaching the existing note; `_continueTile` and the convention note outside every group. Two mutations caught separately: groups born expanded, and empty buckets kept. Gates: analyze clean, suite **3177** (3167 + 10), browser gate 13, wasm release build compiles
- [x] **A rig finding worth keeping:** Varignon cannot exercise "a selection empties a kind" — measured, every one of its eight points touches all five derived kinds — so those tests run on `perp-true-unproved.rgl`, whose point C (a `PointOnObject`) narrows six kinds to `para`/`perp`. And every existing test that reached a row now opens its group first through one helper, `expandGroup`, which scrolls-until-visible *before* it `ensureVisible`s: a header below the lazy list's fold is not built yet, and `ensureVisible` needs an element (session 168's lesson, now on headers too)

## Phase 158b — the row points, the arrow opens; the trivial group (done)

Two UX points the user raised on the grouped list (session 169). The first is the step rows' own gesture, missing from the goal rows. The second is a measurement: on `perp-true-unproved.rgl` **20 of the 45 derived facts relate a line to itself** — `midline_para` where the midline lies on the base, copied 16 times by `perp_perp_para` — and on Varignon 4 of 22 (`isosceles_base` on a flat triangle: angles BAM and MBA "are equal", both zero). True, and content-free. Decided: **group, don't hide.**

- [x] **A tap on a goal row lights its points on the figure; the trailing arrow opens the proof.** The same toggle the step rows have (`_readStep`): tap again to clear, selected styling while lit, and opening a proof clears it. The arrow is an `IconButton` inside a waited tooltip ("Show proof") — `IconButton.tooltip` shows on hover immediately, which is the Phase 157 storm; every tooltip in this list waits
- [x] **`CarrierIndex.isTrivial(Fact)` — "a relation between a line and itself", answered by the closure that knows same-line.** `para`/`perp` whose two lines coincide; `eqangle` whose both sides are a zero angle, or whose two sides are one angle under two spellings. Every other kind is never trivial: `cong` on collinear segments is a real fact, and `coll` names a line rather than relating one. Domain, with a test per arm
- [x] **`ProverReady.carriers`** — the run's own incidence closure, `CarrierIndex.over(database.facts)`, computed once on first use. Not the engine's `incidence`: that one is the angle side's and may trail the database after a stop
- [x] **A `Trivial` group, last, folded, cutting across kinds.** `groupedGoals` takes the carriers and answers `Map<GoalGroup, List<Fact>>` — `KindGroup` per kind in declaration order, then `TrivialGroup` — with trivial facts *removed* from their kind's bucket; without carriers nothing can be called trivial. Born folded it costs one line (`Trivial 20`), which stops the pollution as completely as hiding would while staying honest: proofs cite these facts, the count explains the run's total, and a chip answered *proved* for "AB ∥ AC" on collinear points has a place to be found. When opened, one italic line says what the group is, because "ED is parallel to AB" reads as a real fact unless you know the four are collinear
- [x] **Why not at the source, recorded in PLAN** (§"Nondegeneracy the numeric screen cannot carry"): `rule.dart`'s argument that the DD sources' `ncoll` conditions are carried by the numeric screen holds only when the degenerate conclusion is *false*. `0 = 0` and `AB ∥ AB` pass every check. And they are load-bearing today — 10 non-trivial facts in `perp-true-unproved.rgl` cite a trivial `para` directly, as the rule table's way of saying "same line" — so refusing them is M-P3's work (the carrier index taking that role, which Phase 151b measured as a regression when done alone), not a panel's
- [x] Tests (+8: 4 domain, 4 panel): `isTrivial` per arm and per refusal; `groupedGoals` with carriers putting the trivial group last and out of its kind, and absent when nothing is trivial; the `Trivial` header on the fixture with the measured count, below every kind header, folded; the row tap lighting the figure without opening the proof, the second tap clearing, the arrow opening; `ProverReady.carriers` knowing the run's lines. Two mutations caught separately: `isTrivial` answering nothing, and the row opening the proof. Gates: analyze clean, suite **3185** (3177 + 8), browser gate 13, wasm release build compiles
- [x] **Test finding:** on `perp-true-unproved.rgl` *every* derived parallel is trivial, so the `Parallel lines` header vanishes entirely — a test that assumed a real-parallel bucket there was wrong, not the panel. And a widget test that groups without the carriers the panel uses looks for headers that do not exist; every widget-side `groupedGoals` now passes `ready.carriers`

## Phase 159 — the questions a selection cannot ask (done)

M-P4e. What the panel will *offer*, which is a strictly smaller thing than what the prover can decide — six of the vocabulary's ten, from four selection shapes. Session 163's report: four selected segments produced no chips and no explanation.

- [x] **The empty state says why** (`proof_panel.dart:229`). *Done, session 170 — `ProofPanel.unaskableSelectionHint`, rendered only for a non-empty selection that phrases nothing.* Today `if (questions.isNotEmpty)` renders the section and otherwise renders nothing at all, so a selection that phrases nothing is indistinguishable from a broken panel. One line naming what *would* phrase a question. No dependencies; the cheapest item in M-P4e and the one that turns a limitation back into a limitation
- [x] **`eqangle` from four carriers**, and the reason this is not a reversal of Phase 148. *Done, session 170 — with one correction: the three readings are the three equal-sum partitions of the four line angles, not the three side-pairings, which the transpose symmetry collapses to two (see STATUS).* That box excluded `eqangle` as "eight points, or a correspondence a selection cannot express", which is true of *point* selections and false of carriers: four segments are exactly `∠(s1,s2) = ∠(s3,s4)`, and the residual ambiguity is the three pairings — the same ambiguity already accepted when four points offer three pairings of `para`/`perp`/`cong`. Chips name their segments, as the four-point chips already name their points. Phase 160's builder subsumes the general case; this box is the *fast path* for the selection that already says it unambiguously, and the two are meant to coexist the way §"The question builder" describes
- [x] **Answer it against AR's entailment as well as the database.** *Done, session 170 — `Prover.resolve`; and it is the first positive, not the fifth negative: 54 true four-carrier questions across the corpus, DD proves 8, the closure 15 more, under 6 ms per fixture (STATUS).* 152e measured AR entailing 129 / 89 / 33 / 272 / 2 527 `eqangle` statements beyond the database with **none** refused by the filter, and every *consumer* of that set at zero. Asking is the one direction not measured, and it is a single membership query rather than the 121–1 329 ms quartic enumeration those consumer measurements priced. If it comes back zero as well, record it as the fifth negative and delete the path
- [x] **Concurrency as sugar** *(done, session 170 — `ProverQuestion.reading` carries the sentence, the `coll` stays the fact)*: three selected carriers with an existing `IntersectionPoint` between two of them, desugared to `coll(P, X, Y)` over every pair naming the third, with the all-spellings treatment `ProverQuestion` already does. When no such point exists the honest answer is a chip that is not offered — and the general fix is Phase 153, not a special case here
- [x] **A `concurrent` predicate is *not* in this phase, and the split is deliberate.** *(Scope note, not work — stands.)* A 3×3 determinant over line coordinates would buy refutation with no named point at all, which sugar cannot; it is also a vocabulary addition, so it needs its own symmetry group pinned against its evaluator in `FactDatabase` before a line of it is written. PLAN §"JGEX's question list, compared" records the case; open it as its own phase if a document wants it
- [x] **The tangency ask** *(done, session 170 — a line and a circle; `circleCentre` / `isCircleByConstruction` now public so the ask and the hypothesis share one definition)* — two carriers, or a carrier and a circle: "is this tangent?". Depends on Phase 155, since without the hypothesis the answer is *unproved* for every tangent in every document, which is worse than no chip
- [x] Tests: the new selection shapes and what they still refuse to phrase; the empty-state hint; the `eqangle` ask reaching the same verdict as a full run *(session 170: 21 new tests across `questions_test`, `angle_translation_test`, `prover_test`, `hypotheses_test`, `prover_provider_test`, `proof_panel_test`; every guard mutation-checked)*

## Phase 160 — the question builder (done)

JGEX's *Similar Triangle*, *Congruent Triangle*, *Equilateral Triangle* and *Bisect* — and, once this exists, every other question too. The first draft of this phase called it "blocked on ordered selection" and offered two ways out; **both were wrong**, and JGEX's own *Equal Angle* dialog is the correction: a relation dropdown, eight point slots grouped *First Set* / *Second Set*, a live count, *OK* disabled until full. It does not order a selection — it does not read the selection at all. **The slot carries the order.** See PLAN §"The question builder".

So `selectionProvider` stays a `Set<String>` and no consumer of it changes. This adds a second way to phrase a question beside the chips, not instead of them.

- [x] **`QuestionTemplate`, pure, in `lib/domain/prover/`**: a predicate kind, an ordered list of typed slots, and the labels grouping them. `eqangle` is eight point slots in two groups of four; `simtri`/`contri` are six in two rows of three — **the rows are the correspondence** a set could not state; tangency is a line slot plus a circle slot; `concurrent` is three line slots. Templates for the six already-askable relations too, so any question can be asked with no selection at all. **Landed as an enum** (`question_template.dart`), exhaustive over the vocabulary by test: every `PredicateKind` has a template, plus `concurrent` and `tangent` lowered as the chips lower them. **One shape differs from the box**: a *line* slot is one carrier **or two points**, so `eqangle` is four line slots — JGEX's eight points when filled by points, every witness pair when filled by carriers — and a *segment* slot is a line slot that must bound a length (`cong`, `eqratio`, `midp`'s segment)
- [x] **A filled template produces a `ProverQuestion`** — the existing type, unchanged. That is the test that this is a producer in front of the prover and not a change to it. A slot filled with a *carrier* expands to every witness pair exactly as `questions.dart` already does, which is strictly more than JGEX's dialog can express: it makes the user name two points on a line the app already knows. `QuestionDraft` (immutable; `tap` / `put` / `clear` / `question(objects)`) spells through `question_spellings.dart`, **extracted from `questions.dart` first** so the chips and the builder spell one statement one way. The extraction found `cong` reading through witness pairs — `|AB| = |CD|` on a segment carrying `X` also asked `cong(A,X,C,D)`, a different statement — fixed and pinned
- [x] **Tap-to-fill is the primary gesture, not a dropdown.** Eight dropdowns is workable with six named points, bad at thirty, and unusable on a phone at any size. Next empty slot highlighted; a tap on the figure fills it and advances. A named-object dropdown stays as the fallback for a hard-to-hit point and as the way to correct one slot without clearing the rest. This is also where the ordering physically comes from — clicks arrive ordered even though the selection recording them does not keep an order. `questionDraftProvider` holds the open draft; `_handleTap` in select mode hands the hit to it and leaves the selection alone. Every slot chip is also a menu of what fits it (the dropdown fallback), with *Clear* on a filled one
- [x] **Refute before *OK***. The verdict is three-way and `DiagramFilter` answers *refuted* with no prover run, so a template whose slots are full shows that in the builder. Asking is what costs; refuting is free. A user who fills eight slots into a statement false in the figure should learn it there. `draftCheckProvider`: a complete draft is probed by `DiagramFilter` alone; the bar says *Not true in this figure* in the error colour and *Ask* stays disabled, the prover never runs (pinned in the widget test)
- [x] **Seed from the selection when it fits.** If two segments are selected when an `eqangle` template opens, they land in the first group. Convenience only — never a silent reordering, and every seeded slot must be as visibly editable as an empty one. `QuestionDraft.seeded` taps the selection in construction order, skipping what does not fit; the bar's relation dropdown reseeds on a switch
- [x] **The compound questions, and the one decision inside them.** Equilateral is two `cong`s; Bisect is a `midp` or an `eqangle`. Either `ProverQuestion` grows a conjunction or these are several chips and the compound is never named. **The part that needs deciding is the mixed verdict** — one conjunct refuted, another unproved — not the plumbing. Decide it before writing the type **Decided: no conjunction, and no compound named.** A verdict is about one statement and the answer is three-way by design (M-P4); a compound with one conjunct refuted and another unproved would need a fourth answer that is about the *question*, not the figure. Equilateral is two `cong` asks; Bisect is the `midp` or the `eqangle` template — both exist. The template list stays the vocabulary's, which is what keeps "every kind has a template" a pinnable property. See PLAN §"The builder as built"
- [x] **`simtri`'s eqangle conclusion stays absent** whatever this phase does. `simtri` is orientation-free (M-P1) and a reflected pair negates every mod-π angle; the direct/reflected split is what would change that, and it is not here. Nothing here touched the rule table
- [x] **Mobile is a first-class target for this surface, not an afterthought** — it is the case that kills the dropdown design, and Phase 154's sheet is where it has to live. A template with eight slots must be fillable at a phone's sheet height. **The builder is not in the panel at all**: on a phone the proof panel is a modal sheet that covers the figure, and the figure is what fills the slots. `QuestionBuilderBar` sits along the canvas's bottom on every layout, its slot row scrolls sideways, the sheet closes when the builder opens from it and reopens on *Ask* — pinned on a 400×800 window
- [x] Tests: templates → `ProverQuestion` for every kind, including carrier-filled line slots expanding to all spellings; the refute-before-OK path taking no run; the correspondence actually reaching `simtri` on a rig where the wrong correspondence is false. `question_spellings_test` (13), `question_template_test` (20, the correspondence on a scalene triangle and its medial triangle: the rows' reading holds under the filter, the swapped one is refuted with no run), `question_draft_provider_test` (5), `question_builder_bar_test` (6). Gates: analyze clean, suite **3274**, browser gate 13, wasm release build

## Phase 161 — the uncharged pass (closed in Phase 188: measured, and the shape was a lookup)

Phase 156's measurement, promoted: `proverChunkBudget` bounds only the *charged* half of a DD step, and on `apatitos-topos.rgl` (worst pass 1.4 s) and `tangent-chase.rgl` (2.5 s) the uncharged half — the join enumeration that yields no candidate, advanced without being counted as an application — is the whole freeze, flat across every candidate budget. No chunk budget makes those passes frame-sized, Stop cannot be noticed sooner than one of them ends, and the same mechanism is why `tangent-chase` runs **5.7 s to quiescence** in the provider's configuration. `benchmark/prover_chunk_bench.dart` is the rig; the Phase 145 note ("a join enumeration that yields no candidate is advanced without being charged") named the mechanism without pricing it.

**Repriced in Phase 163.** The uncharged enumeration was almost entirely one rule's — `eqangle_transitive`, now deleted. At the provider's chunk of 250 the worst pass is **72 ms on `apatitos-topos` (was 1.4 s) and 297 ms on `tangent-chase` (was 2.5 s; 0.4 s to quiescence, was 5.7 s)**; `provoleas2` converges with a 59 ms worst pass. What remains over a frame is `tangent-chase`'s one pass, and it is the remaining `eqangle × eqangle` joins (`aa_simtri`, `sas_simtri`) over the twelve tangent–chord facts. The box below stands, smaller.

- [x] **Decide the unit before the fix** — taken up as **Phase 188**, and the measurement decided it: neither option. The dominant shape was not a join but a fully bound premise scanned as one (99 % of `tangent-chase`'s worst pass, zero applications); looked up instead, the pass fell to 14–19 ms and what remains is the charged half at 50–100 µs per application. The unit stands as it is

A user brought a JGEX screenshot proving `perp(C,D,D,F)` on `test/fixtures/perp-true-unproved.rgl` — the theorem Phase 148 had shipped as the *unproved* rig. Two sessions' worth of claims were wrong, and the screenshot said why: **JGEX constructed an auxiliary point** ("H is midpoint of DA" — the midpoint of our BC) before reasoning. Translating its trace step by step showed where ours could not follow.

- [x] **The gap is one Session 156 named and only half-fixed.** The vocabulary is point-tuples, so a *line* is named by a pair of points on it and one line has many names. `hypotheses` emits every witness pair for that reason; a fact a **rule** derives gets no such help, so the run stalls holding the statement it needs under the wrong name. `questions.dart`'s all-spellings search (Phase 148) was the same hole seen from the consumer side
- [x] **Three rules** (`rule.dart`): `coll_transitive` (`coll(a,b,c) & coll(a,b,d) => coll(a,c,d)`), `perp_coll` (`perp(a,b,c,d) & coll(a,b,e) & coll(a,b,f) => perp(e,f,c,d)`), and `orthocentre` (`perp(a,b,c,d) & perp(a,c,b,d) => perp(a,d,b,c)`) — the last stated on four points because that is what it is, an orthocentric system in which each point is the orthocentre of the other three. It is what JGEX reached with full angles, available here without them
- [x] **`para_coll` was written, rigged, measured and dropped.** The exact analogue for parallels derives *nothing new* on any fixture, including the one that motivated the family. The hole it closes is real in principle and it is one line the day a document needs it; a rule with no consumer pays its cost on every pivot for nothing. Recorded at the site so the next reader does not re-derive it
- [x] **Both halves are necessary, and the regression pins both directions** (`rule_engine_test.dart`): the rules alone leave the document at 23 facts and no goal; the auxiliary point alone leaves it at 22. Together the fixpoint reaches it, `Proof.verify()` comes back empty — a certificate, not a transcript — and the proof cites `midline_para`, `perp_coll`, `coll_transitive`, `orthocentre` and `para_perp_perp`, mirroring JGEX's own structure
- [x] **Cost measured before adding, not after**: `locus3` unchanged, `apatitos-topos` +2 facts, the motivating fixture 13 → 23, `provoleas2` 49 → 75 facts for the same 30 000 applications, and no wall-clock regression on any of them
- [x] **What this corrects, said plainly.** Session 158's claim that no auxiliary construction could help was an over-generalization from a fact-seeding experiment, and the claim that the theorem needs M-P3's angle arithmetic was wrong too — it needs neither. The structural half of the argument survives: with the core *as it was*, and the objects *as they were*, it was unreachable
- [x] Tests: a numeric rig per new rule (the per-rule convention — a rule that is not a theorem would let the filter's screen mask an unsound step); the document regression above; `rule_test`'s table count 23 → 26. Mutation-checked: dropping any of the three fails exactly its own rig plus the document regression; dropping `para_coll` failed only its own rig, which is why it is gone
- [x] **One test changed character, and that is the phase working**: the provider's "any spelling counts" pin used to rest on `coll(A,E,B)` being *unreachable*. It is now derivable, so the test moved to a budget too small to reach it — the scan past the first spelling is still what is pinned
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **2984** green, 32 goldens byte-identical, browser gate green (6)

## Phase 162 — a question reads as spelled (done)

A user's report on `test/fixtures/tangent-chord.rgl` (session 170): selecting the four segments of the tangent–chord figure offered "angles ECD and CBD are equal" — false as magnitudes — and two chips beginning "the angle from EC to DB…" truncated past reading. All three were the *right* statements: the chip label read `readFact(Fact.of(canonical))`, and `eqangle`'s transpose symmetry canonicalized `∠(BC,CE) = ∠(BD,DC)` (the theorem, 47.4° = 47.4°) into its equivalent `∠(EC,DC) = ∠(BC,DB)`, whose three-point prose names different angles. A fact reads canonical because it *is* its orbit; a question is a spelling the user chose, and must read as spelled.

- [x] **`readPredicate`** beside `readFact`, one implementation; `questionLabel` reads the question's own spelling, the asked proof's goal row too
- [x] **The four-carrier orientation is chosen, not inherited from construction order**: of a statement's eight spellings prefer one that reads three-point on both sides, and among those one whose *magnitude* reading is true in the figure (positions, as the filter reads them); deterministic on ties
- [x] **Chip labels wrap** — a phone's sheet is full-width and a docked panel is 300 px; a draggable panel would help neither, wrapping helps both. *Landed as `QuestionChip` (Material `Chip` cannot wrap a label — see STATUS) with the chip block capped at 40 % of the panel and scrolling inside the cap*
- [x] Tests: `readPredicate` keeps the spelling `readFact` canonicalizes away; the fixture's four segments read "angles BCE and BDC are equal"; a long label wraps in a narrow panel
- [x] **Not here, and the reason the user's actual goal still came back *unproved*:** the tangent–chord theorem needs the `tangent_chord` rule Phase 155 wrote, measured and dropped — its conclusions were reachable another way on *that* fixture, not on this one (7 facts, complete, no route). *Reinstated in Phase 163 — and the "cheaper premise shape" this box asked for did not exist, because the shape was never the cost*

## Phase 163 — the tangent–chord rule, and the rule it cost (done)

Opened from Phase 162's last box. `test/fixtures/tangent-chord.rgl` is a user's figure whose theorem is the alternate-segment theorem and nothing else reaches it (the inscribed-angle road needs a fourth point on the circle). Phase 155 had written `tangent_chord`, measured it on `tangent-chase.rgl` and dropped it — ~2× the run for thirteen `eqangle`s with no consumer, blamed on its `cong × cong` premise pair. Two things had changed since: the consumer arrived (Phase 159's four-carrier ask *is* an `eqangle` question), and there was now a document that needs the rule. The box asked for a cheaper premise shape. The measurement said the shape was irrelevant.

- [x] **Premise order is not the cost.** `perp`-first and `cong`-first are identical to the millisecond on all seven fixtures. The engine enumerates every fact of a premise's kind through its whole orbit regardless of what is bound, so a three-premise rule's join is the same work in any order; the price of `tangent_chord` was what its *conclusions* did downstream
- [x] **The downstream cost was one rule, and it was the table's most expensive line.** `eqangle_transitive` has no shared variable between its two premises, so every one of a pivot's 128 orbit forms joins every one of every other `eqangle`'s 128 forms — 16 384 bind attempts per pair, none charged as an application. PLAN §"Two closures" named this shape as the reason `provoleas2.json` never converged; this is the measurement. Twelve new `eqangle`s on `tangent-chase` pushed it from quiescence at 16 882 applications to *not finishing* at the 30 000 cap
- [x] **`eqangle_transitive` deleted, on 152e's own terms — one rule at a time, everything else unchanged.** It is a row sum, which is what the angle closure *is*: every fact it could store is entailed, and `Prover.resolve` answers an ask against the closure. Dropping it loses **no fact of any other kind on any fixture**; the only facts lost anywhere are 15 `eqangle`s on `provoleas2`, every one closure-resolved. Gains: `apatitos-topos` 1 272 → 49 ms to quiescence, `tangent-chase` 5 261 → 186 ms, `locus3` 72 → 8 ms, and **`provoleas2` completes** — 25 826 applications, 115 facts where the capped run held 82 (+27 `para`, +4 `perp`, +3 `coll`, +5 `eqratio`, a `midp`, a `simtri`, a `contri` the transitive join had been starving of pivots). Pinned: on `tangent-chase` every composite of two stored `eqangle`s sharing a side resolves through the closure
- [x] **`tangent_chord` in**, at its price now that the transitive rule is gone: `apatitos-topos` 49 → 71 ms (+3 `eqangle`), `tangent-chase` 186 → 388 ms (+12 `eqangle`, quiescent at 1 594 applications), the user's document 3 → 5 ms and **proved** — one `tangent_chord` step over the tangency and two radii, `verify()` empty, and pinned to have no route without the rule
- [x] **The other three row sums, measured the same way and recorded, not acted on**: `para_transitive` loses nothing on any fixture and cuts applications 30–45 % on three — deletable by the same argument, left for its own decision; `perp_perp_para` loses 17 / 44 stored `para` on two fixtures and `para_perp_perp` one `perp` on `locus3`, all closure-entailed but unreached by the publisher, so both stay
- [x] **The length-closure tripwire fired, on the ratio half.** `provoleas2` at quiescence holds five `eqratio`s (three spellings of one `simtri_eqratio`, two `intercept_eqratio`s) and they share segment `AO` — a chain the deferred ratio closure would close. Re-pinned at 6; the re-measurement is recorded in 152e as the next thing owed there
- [x] Tests: the per-rule rig (the swapped mod-π reading refuted), the document regression, the closure-composite property, the panel chip proved end to end; the provider's cap comment corrected. Gates: analyze clean, suite **3219** green (3215 + 4), browser gate 13, wasm release build compiles. Phase 161 repriced below


## Phase 164 — two tangents that are one line (done)

Session 172: the tangent–chord theorem proved on the VM, in `flutter run`, and — driven through the extension with the fixture loaded — on the deployed wasm build, yet the user's own clicks on the site got "Nothing to ask about this selection". The build was never the difference. `tangent-chord.rgl` holds **two coincident `TangentLine`s at C** (`c` = branch 0, `d` = branch 1): the tangent tool emits both branches unconditionally, and from a point *on* the circle both are the tangent at that point. `E` is on `d`. A canvas click on the line goes to whichever of the two is numerically nearer by a last-bit margin — the hit-tester breaks only *exact* ties by insertion order — so it lands on `c` or `d` depending on the click position and the platform's arithmetic. `c` carries only `C`, so no witness pair, so no angle question. Which object names a line is the prover's business, not the click's.

- [x] **`coincidentCarriers(a, b)` beside `structurallyIncident` in `incidence.dart`** — structural, no numerics: two `TangentLine`s on one point and one circle with the point on the circle (or on the same branch anywhere); two-point kinds (`LineThroughTwoPoints` / `Segment` / `Ray`) over the same two points in either order — a ray and its reverse are one carrier; `RelativeLine`s of one kind through one point whose references *coincide* (recursive, so a perpendicular to segment `AB` and one to the line `AB` are one line); `AngleBisectorLine`s of one vertex over the same arm pair. Symmetric; a kind not listed coincides with nothing but itself
- [x] **`askableQuestions` gathers a carrier's points across its coincident twins** — through `pointsOnCarrier(objects, carrier)`, so `c` selected reads exactly as `d` selected. A segment's length still comes from its own two endpoints. **`hypotheses()` reads through the same helper**, so a `coll` whose three points sit on two copies of one line is emitted; on the seven fixtures this changes no fact count (coincident copies are rare, and the one pair the corpus has carries two points)
- [x] **The tangent tool emits one line when the point is on the circle** — `structurallyIncident(circle, point)` decides it, branch 0; from an external point the pair stays. Existing documents keep their duplicate and are answered by the box above
- [x] The hit-tester is not touched: a near-tie between two objects that are the same line is not the hit-tester's to resolve, and once the question builder reads both the same the tie stops mattering
- [x] Tests (10): `coincidentCarriers` per kind (the on-circle tangent pair true, the external pair false, a reversed ray true, symmetric both ways); `pointsOnCarrier` order; the fixture with `c` selected offers the same three questions as with `d` and the first is proved by `tangent_chord` either way; a segment beside the line through its ends borrows the line's glued point; a `coll` across two copies of a line; the tool on an on-circle point adds one `TangentLine`, on an external point two
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **3229** green (3219 + 10), browser gate 13
- [x] Looked at on the deployed build after the merge (`9d6464b`, `main.dart.wasm` 2 727 931 bytes), with the fixture loaded through the extension: the same four clicks that gave "Nothing to ask" on the previous build offer the three chips, and "angles BCE and BDC are equal" proves by `tangent chord` from the tangency and two radii

## Phase 165 — the length system (done)

M-P3's other half, un-deferred by session 174's measurement (152e's last box): a ℚ-linear closure over segment log-lengths entails `|AB| = |LO|` on `provoleas2.json` — a `cong` the rule table cannot reach, with a coefficient 2 the union-find cannot form — and 43 `eqratio`s with it. The angle system is the template (`angle_closure.dart` / `angle_translation.dart` / `angle_chase.dart`), with one deliberate difference: lengths have no modulus, so this elimination **is** over ℚ (PLAN §"AR is a ℤ-module" — the length paragraph), and `Rational` already is what it needs. What it must not do is share code with the angle side that would let a ℚ pivot leak into θ.

**Shipped in four commits.** Gates at the end: analyze clean, suite **3342** green (3275 at the phase's start), browser gate **15** (was 13). The yield is one fact on one document — and that is the point rather than a disappointment: the closure exists to reach a `cong` the rule table cannot, and volume was never the claim.

- [x] **`LengthClosure`, pure** (`length_closure.dart`): homogeneous rows over segment variables, `cong` → `l₁ − l₂ = 0`, `eqratio` → `l₁ − l₂ − l₃ + l₄ = 0`, `midp` → `l_ma − l_mb = 0`, Gaussian elimination over ℚ with **provenance** on every row from the first line — the certificate `Proof.verify()` re-multiplies, exactly as the angle side does. 34 tests, including the property the angle side cannot run (below). Three things the build decided:
  - **No constant term, so no inconsistency.** The vocabulary is equalities and ratios, both scale-invariant, so there is nothing to carry and the all-zero assignment satisfies any set of rows — `AngleAddOutcome.contradiction` has no counterpart. The `ln 2` of a midpoint enters as one more *variable* the day a document wants the 1:2, which is how session 174's rig carried it and costs the closure nothing
  - **The basis is kept fully reduced, not merely echelon** — a new pivot is back-substituted into every row that mentions it. That is what makes `residual` a *canonical* coset representative, without which the n² `eqratio` enumeration (152e's trick) buckets by an arbitrary partial reduction and misses pairs. Mutation-checked: disabling back-substitution fails exactly the two tests that pin it
  - **The ℚ payoff is pinned in variables**: `provoleas2`'s intercept and similar-triangle facts entail `|AB| = |LO|` through a coefficient 2 on `l_AO`, and the `cong` facts alone do not — the union-find's ceiling, executable
  - **Property-tested against an evaluator**, and it is stronger than the angle side's sweep: over ℚ with one degree of freedom left the row span *is* the set of relations vanishing on the solution ray, so `proves` and a numeric assignment must agree in **both** directions (three seeds). A ℤ-module's span is a proper subset of what vanishes, which is the whole `2θ` story
  - **Not here, and deliberately**: the `GeoPoint` pair → variable keying (`carriers.dart`'s spelling) lands with `length_translation.dart` in the next box, where `angle_translation.lineVariable` lives — the closure stays pure Dart over `String` variables, so no ℚ pivot can reach θ
- [x] **What it publishes and what it answers** (`length_translation.dart`, wired into `Prover` beside `AngleTranslation`; `checkDerivation` re-derives a `length_arithmetic` step). Publishes `cong`, answers `eqratio` on *ask* through `Prover.resolve` — the opposite split from the angle side, by the same rule: publish what has a consumer and what the enumeration can afford
  - **Measured on all seven fixtures against the same run without the publisher**, and the delta is one fact in the whole corpus: `provoleas2.json` 118 → 119 facts, `cong` 10 → 11, the new one `|AB| = |LO|` under `length_arithmetic`, plus one more pass to notice it. Applications identical everywhere (13 268 on `provoleas2`); the other six byte-identical by kind
  - **Publisher cost per pass**, enumerating over the quiescent database: `no-locus` 1 conclusion in 10 µs, `perp-true-unproved` 2 in 76 µs, `tangent-chase` 11 in 165 µs, `apatitos-topos` 10 in 376 µs, `provoleas2` 11 in 384 µs. Three orders off the 1.3 s the quartic cost, as the pairs-of-variables shape predicted
  - **Two asymmetries with the angle side, both deliberate**: `midp` may be *cited* and may not be *concluded* (its row is a consequence of it, not equivalent — equal distances do not put a point on a segment; `coll`'s treatment next door); and there is no shared-point refusal, because `|AB| = |AC|` is an isoceles triangle where `para` between two lines through a point is a degeneracy
  - **`length_closure_upside_test.dart` changed job rather than moving**: its `cong` column is 0 everywhere now because the system whose upside it measured is switched on, and a `cong` reappearing there is a *defect*. Its `eqratio` column fell 43 → 23 on `provoleas2` — the published `cong` brought 20 within reach of the stored `cong`s alone — and stays, since `eqratio` is never published
  - Tests: 15 in `length_translation_test.dart` (the vocabulary, the conclusion/absorption asymmetry, the publisher's enumeration, provenance across the boundary), 6 added to `derivation_check_test.dart` including the ℚ step a union-find cannot form
- [x] **The step reads as a chase** (`length_chase.dart`): `length_arithmetic from [..]` with one line per equation under it, re-derived at render time and never stored, `angle_chase.dart`'s three decisions carried over (citation order, separator decided once, a scaled row shown already scaled)
  - **The spelling is products, not ratios, and that was the open decision.** Rows are over log-lengths, so a reader shown `log|AM| + log|AB| = 2·log|AO|` is being shown the implementation; exponentiating gives `|AM|·|AB| = |AO|^2`, the same statement in the quantity a geometer has — the counterpart of the angle side rendering in θ rather than in rows. Ratios read better for a stored `eqratio` and *only* for that shape: a chase line is an arbitrary ℚ-combination, and `l_AM − 2·l_AO + l_AB = 0` (a real line from `provoleas2`) has no canonical split into two ratios. Ratio-when-possible is two notations in one chase, which is what the separator decision exists to forbid
  - **So there is no fraction slash** — the character set is `|`, `=`, `^`, ASCII digits, `/` only inside an exponent, plus `·` and the `⟹` the angle chase already uses. Pinned on the browser gate (13 → 15) beside the angle chase's glyphs, together with a ℚ pivot of `1/3` a double would round
  - **`ProofStep.chase` is now the `ArithmeticChase` interface** — an interface and not a base class, because the two chases share a shape and no arithmetic, and a common superclass is one refactor away from a common closure. `angle_chase_test` casts where it reaches `lines`, which is the claim it is making
  - The chase `provoleas2` produces, pinned whole: `|OA|^2 = |OB|^2` [1], `|OB| = |ON|` [2], `|MO| = |MA|` [3], `|ON|·|OB| = |MO|·|LO|` [14], `|MA|·|AB| = |OA|^2` [21], `⟹ |AB| = |LO|`. The squares are the coefficient 2; the two `eqratio`s are the intercept and similar-triangle facts cross-multiplied
  - **The browser caught a guess.** The first web expectation pinned the other sound reading of one line (the elimination doubles the `cong` row rather than halving the `eqratio` row); VM and target agree, and both now pin the row the elimination actually chose
- [x] **The regression is the measurement**: `provoleas2.json` derives `cong(A, B, L, O)` at quiescence, `verify()` empty, the chase names the intercept and similar-triangle facts; `length_closure_upside_test.dart`'s novel `cong` counts are **0 everywhere** and the tripwire now fires in the other direction — a `cong` reappearing there is a document the publisher is *missing*
  - **A third test, for the half those two cannot state.** "Nothing is outstanding" is equally true of a publisher that never ran, so it pins what was *published*: one `cong` in the whole corpus, of kind `cong` and no other, screened by the same filter DD screens with, each verifying as a certificate. One fact is the phase's honest yield — the closure's value is not volume, it is reaching a `cong` the table cannot
  - That file's fixture list goes **6 → 7**: `tangent-chord.rgl` was missing from it for no recorded reason and is in the corpus everywhere else
- [x] **A question the builder can now answer**: the *Equal lengths* template, four taps into two two-point segment slots on `provoleas2.json`, asked through the provider — `|AB| = |LO|` comes back **proved** under `length_arithmetic`, with a chase reading `|AB| = |LO|` and a proof that verifies. Before the phase the same question answered *unproved*: the run finishing and not getting there

## Phase 166 — `para_transitive` deleted (done)

Phase 163's recorded-not-acted-on row sum, acted on — its own decision, one rule at a time, everything else unchanged (PLAN §"A row sum is not a rule").

- [x] **Measured before deleting, on all seven fixtures**: identical fact counts by kind with and without the rule everywhere; applications to quiescence 682 → 386 on `locus3` (−43 %), 4 896 → 3 400 on `perp-true-unproved` (−31 %), 22 996 → 13 268 on `provoleas2` (−42 %); `apatitos-topos`, `no-locus`, `tangent-chase`, `tangent-chord` untouched to the application. Wall time follows on the three (44 → 15 ms, 55 → 42 ms, 445 → 392 ms)
- [x] **Deleted**, with the note at its place in `rule.dart` and the `eqangle_transitive` note beside `cong_transitive` updated; the two rules that stay (`perp_perp_para`, `para_perp_perp`) do so because the publisher does not reach what they store — that finding is unchanged
- [x] **The Varignon rigs keep the rule locally.** `rule_engine_test` and `proof_test` run a two-rule table with no AR, where the theorem *needs* a join; that is an engine test, not a table test, so `para_transitive` lives there as a test-local `Rule.parse` and `verify()` is handed the table plus it — with the unknown-rule refusal pinned beside, as a check on the check. The per-rule soundness rig is gone with the rule; the rule's mechanics are still exercised by Varignon
- [x] **Pinned** (`para_transitive_test.dart`): the table lacks it, and on `provoleas2` at quiescence every composite of two stored `para`s sharing a side is stored without it (over a hundred composites, 46 `para`s). Table count 27 → 26
- [x] **Two boundary rigs followed the table**: the provider test's DD-only control run became the exchange (the theorem's last step is the publisher's now), and the panel test pinning a bare DD step opens a `midline_para` fact instead of the theorem, whose proof now carries a chase
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **3275** green, browser gate 13

## Phase 167 — the corpus, and the first honest baseline (done)

`docs/PLAN.md` ends five separate measurements with the same sentence — "the corpus is the limiting factor, not the algebra". This phase stops that being an assertion. The user asked for JGEX's and Newclid's provers to be reverse-engineered and worthwhile features ported; both passes were done first (PLAN §"What JGEX and Newclid actually hold"), and they say the same thing the corpus finding does: **the benchmark is the thing to port first**, because without it no later port can be justified or falsified. No `lib/` change.

- [x] **The parser** (`test/corpus/newclid_problem.dart`): Newclid's one-line construction DSL, its grammar read off the corpus rather than assumed — all 928 goals fit it. Three decisions: several goals after one `?` are **several problems** sharing a construction (PLAN §"No compound question" — a compound whose conjuncts split would need a fourth verdict about the question's shape); a `|` opens the **auxiliary section**, parsed, kept, and deliberately **not built**, since handing the prover the construction it was supposed to find would measure the corpus's authors; and a malformed body costs **that body**, not the file it is in — one bad line used to cost two whole files, 116 problems, and the report said nothing about it
- [x] **The translator** (`test/corpus/newclid_translation.dart`): 28 constructions onto object kinds that already exist. The mapping is read off `jgex/constructions/*.py`, where each `JGEXDefinition` states the predicates it asserts, **not inferred from macro names** — which is how two of them turned out simpler than they look: `lc_tangent x a o` is only `perp a x a o`, so it needs no `TangentLine`, and both circle intersections name a point already on both curves. One point on one curve is a `PointOnObject`; on two it is an `IntersectionPoint`; the rest are named kinds. Nothing is written as a `.rgl` — `test/fixtures/` is v1's permanent save-format corpus
- [x] **A new point never lands on a point the figure already names**, and that is worth **86 of the 466 problems that build**. Only `intersection_lc` and `intersection_cc` say so explicitly, and reading only the explicit form got the wrong point: `m = on_circle m o b, on_line m a b` says exactly the same thing silently. Mutation-checked — and the first mutation check *passed*, which meant the test was too weak, not the rule redundant; `test_get_two_intersections` (two crossings of one pair of circles, so branch 0 twice builds one point) is the shape that pins it
- [x] **A degenerate figure, or one the goal is false in, is resampled** — the reference implementation's own policy and not a way of choosing the answer: Newclid rebuilds a problem up to **100** times until its goals hold numerically (`problem_builder.py`). `d = on_pline d a b c, eqdistance d c a b` meets its curves at the isosceles trapezoid *and* at the parallelogram, and a point-tuple vocabulary without `sameside` cannot say which. Goal-false fell 30 → 10
- [x] **The benchmark** (`benchmark/corpus_bench.dart`, `benchmark/run_corpus.sh`): the provider's own numbers — 30 000 applications, chunk 250, `stopWhen` on the goal — and `prover_provider`'s own verdict rules, so it measures the engine the app runs. Every proof is read back and `verify()`d. Informational and non-failing, like every benchmark here. The corpus is **not vendored**: `--corpus` points at a checkout
- [x] **"Refuted" turned out to be a claim the run had not earned, and separating it is the phase's sharpest correction.** Four problems came back refuted; all four are figures that *came apart* — a second, independently seeded perturbation carries a point across a branch, the intersection the construction names swaps, and the problem's own `coll` hypothesis stops holding. They are reported as `unstableFigure`. **The baseline has zero refutations**, where the first draft of it would have published four
- [x] **The baseline** (PLAN §"The prover against a real benchmark"): 928 goals, **466 built, 46 proved, 401 unproved, 19 undecided, 0 refuted**, 119 s, and **0 unsound proofs**. Two gaps are sharper than the total: `cyclic` **0 of 43** and `coll` **1 of 59**, which is PLAN's own prediction — "collinearity is an incidence statement, and no angle algebra can publish it" — arriving with a number attached for the first time
- [x] Tests (31, `test/corpus/newclid_problem_test.dart`), fifteen problems **inlined verbatim** so the gate needs no external checkout. The one that earns its keep asserts **every hypothesis of every translated problem holds under `DiagramFilter`** — a real assertion and not a tautology, since `hypotheses()` reads *parent ties*, so a statement it emits must be true in any figure those ties produce, and one that fails means the construction is not what the clause said. It caught a hand-written problem of mine that was simply false
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **3400** green (3369 + 31), browser gate 15

## Phase 168 — the carrier publisher (closed: measured negative)

The corpus benchmark's own ordering (PLAN §"The prover against a real benchmark") ranked this first: `coll` proved on 1 of 59 built goals, and `CarrierIndex` (Phase 151a) already knows the incidence closure that `coll_transitive`/`perp_coll`/`cyclic_fifth_point` compute one rule application at a time. Measured before writing a publisher, on the corpus's own 466 built problems, the way every recent prover phase measures first.

- [x] **The coverage question, answered directly: 0 of 52.** For every corpus `coll` goal that finished *quiescent* and unproved, a fresh `CarrierIndex` built over the run's own final fact set was asked the goal — the strongest form of the question, since it hands the closure everything DD/AR derived. Not one comes back yes that the stored facts do not already answer. `coll_transitive` merges on a shared *pair* of points (`coll(a,b,c) & coll(a,b,d) => coll(a,c,d)`), which is the same soundness trigger `CarrierIndex.absorb` merges on — run to a fixpoint, the two are the same closure by construction, and the corpus is the first evidence at a scale that shows it rather than assumes it
- [x] **The cost question, checked next since PLAN's actual claim was "what a future deletion of `coll_transitive` rests on", not "more coverage".** `coll_transitive`/`perp_coll`/`cyclic_fifth_point` together derive **5.6% of all facts corpus-wide** (1 503 of 26 610 across the 466 built problems). Of the corpus's 19 undecided (budget-exhausted) problems, 12 hold some fact from these three rules — active, not idle — but none shows the dominant share `eqangle_transitive` did before Phase 163 measured and deleted it. No single problem's budget reads as spent on these three
- [x] **So: no new `coll` goal, no measured budget win — the `para_coll` precedent (Phase 150), applied to the item the benchmark itself ranked first.** `coll_transitive` and `perp_coll` stay in the rule table, unchanged; `cyclic_fifth_point` stays too, its own status ("fires on no fixture", Phase 151a) now read against a corpus that still finds nothing for it to do beyond what the rule already reaches. Written up in PLAN, no `lib/` change
- [x] **`cyclic`'s own gap (0 of 43) is not this phase's business, and the measurement above does not touch it.** PLAN already read it as a rule-table gap — Newclid reaches `cyclic` through R01 (`cong` ×3 from a centre), R04 (the inscribed converse with an `ncoll` guard) and R49/R50 (recognising a centre), none of which this table has — not a closure gap, before this phase started. That is the next item in the benchmark's ordering

## Phase 169 — the rule Newclid's cyclic goals actually needed (done)

The benchmark's ordering, next item after Phase 168's negative: the ~18 in-vocabulary rules Newclid has that this table lacks, `cyclic` first (0 of 43 proved). Newclid's own list — R01 (`cong` ×3 from a centre), R04 (the inscribed converse, already `inscribed_converse`), R49/R50 (recognising a centre) — narrowed to what needs no new predicate and no `sameclock`/`sameside` guard (the direct/reflected split is its own later item), measured one rule at a time on the corpus, the way every rule addition since Phase 150 has been.

- [x] **`equidistant_cyclic`** (Newclid's R01): `cong(o,a,o,b) & cong(o,b,o,c) & cong(o,c,o,d) => cyclic(a,b,c,d)` — the definition of concyclic, stated as a rule because it is not otherwise reachable: a structural circle's centre already feeds `hypotheses` a `cong` chain and every witness pair of `cyclic` directly, so this rule earns its keep only where four points are shown equidistant from a common point by *other* reasoning, which is exactly what an olympiad "prove ABCD cyclic" problem states. Numeric rig in `rule_engine_test.dart`, table count 26 → 27
- [x] **Zero effect on all seven fixtures — none puts four points equidistant from a point that is not their constructed centre — and measured on the corpus instead, per Phase 167's whole point.** `benchmark/corpus_bench.dart` before/after: **46 → 62 proved** of the corpus's 466 built goals. One direct `cyclic` proof and fifteen indirect: `inscribed_angle` reads the new `cyclic` facts into `eqangle` (13 → 20 proved), which the angle closure then reads into `para` (10 → 18 proved). `coll` (1/59) and `perp` (11/58) untouched, confirming the rule is exactly as narrow as its premises say. 0 unsound, 0 refuted, **undecided count unchanged at 19** — the new rule does not push any additional problem past budget
- [x] **Newclid's R50 measured alongside it and dropped.** `cyclic(a,b,c,d) & cong(o,a,o,b) & cong(o,c,o,d) => cong(o,a,o,c)` — partial-evidence circumcentre recognition. +1 proved for **+3 undecided**, a worse trade than R01's own: `cyclic`'s large orbit makes it an expensive premise to add a second time, and the marginal proof it buys does not cover the budget it costs elsewhere. Written, rigged, measured, not kept — the `para_coll` precedent again, this time on a rule that *does* have a small positive effect and is dropped anyway because the effect is negative on net
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **3401** green (3400 + 1)

**Next.** `cyclic`'s remaining 42 unproved goals and the rest of the ~18-rule estimate — R49 in cong-only form was subsumed by R50's measurement above rather than tried separately, so it is still open if a future session wants it (expect the same orbit cost). Then the direct/reflected `simtri` split (`sameclock`), then constants, per the benchmark's ordering.

## Phase 170 — cyclic_trapezoid: fires, and still measures to zero (closed: measured negative)

The rescan of Newclid's ~92 rules that Phase 169's "Next" pointed at, run before picking the next candidate rather than guessing: all 92 cross-referenced against regula2's closed 10-predicate vocabulary and 27-rule table, ranked by promise on `cyclic`/`coll`. R21 came back the cheapest, most targeted first pick — two premises, no guard, nothing in the table already states it.

- [x] **`cyclic_trapezoid`** (Newclid's R21): `cyclic(a,b,c,d) & para(a,b,c,d) => eqangle(a,d,c,d,c,d,c,b)` — a cyclic trapezoid is isosceles. Numeric rig: a half-turn through a circle's centre maps any chord to a parallel one and keeps the image on the same circle, so building `c`, `d` as the `CentralReflectionPoint`s of `a`, `b` through the centre makes both premises structural with no second circle needed
- [x] **Measured on the corpus: 62 → 62 proved, unchanged.** `cyclic` stays at 1 of 43, no undecided/refuted count moves
- [x] **Checked directly whether the rule ever fires, rather than assumed idle from the proved-count parity — it does.** A standalone instrumented run (rule added, every fact its derivation credits to `cyclic_trapezoid` logged) finds **68 new `eqangle` facts across 15 corpus problems**, every one absent from the database before the rule ran. The facts are real; nothing downstream consumes them — the same shape Phase 152e's `eqangle`-ask measurement found on the seven fixtures, now confirmed on the corpus too
- [x] **Dropped on the `para_coll`/R50 precedent**: a rule with real output and no consumer still pays `cyclic`'s large orbit on every pivot for nothing. Written up in PLAN, no `lib/` change beyond the comment recording the measurement
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite unchanged at **3401**

**Next.** `cyclic`'s remaining unproved goals are a rule-table gap in `eqangle`'s *consumer* set now, not in what can state new angle equalities — R21 joins R01's own indirect route (through `inscribed_angle`/AR) as evidence the table can express what's needed and nothing reads it forward from there. R49 (cong-only centre recognition, still untried per Phase 169) is the next rule-shaped candidate; also worth revisiting whether a widened converse set (an `eqangle`-consuming rule beyond `eqangle_transitive`/`simtri`/the two single-premise converses) is a general lever rather than a per-rule one. Then the direct/reflected `simtri` split (`sameclock`), then constants, per the benchmark's ordering.

## Phase 171 — the `eqangle` consumer gap (done)

Phase 170's finding, taken as a general question rather than as one more per-rule coin flip. Two rules in a row said the same thing: `equidistant_cyclic` (R01) reached the corpus's `cyclic` goals only *indirectly*, and `cyclic_trapezoid` (R21) produced 68 genuinely new `eqangle` facts that **nothing read**. `eqangle`'s consumer set was `eqangle_transitive` (deleted in Phase 163, subsumed by AR), the two `simtri` criteria and the two single-premise converses — and that was the whole of it. So: is *widening the consumer set* a general lever? Measured before anything was written, in the Phase 168/170 shape.

- [x] **The candidate list, read off Newclid's `all_rules.py` rather than guessed.** All 88 rules parsed, the 19 with `eqangle` in the premises kept, narrowed to the **seven** this vocabulary states without a new predicate and without the deferred `sameclock` guard: R02 `parallel_from_inclination`, R30 `perp_from_inclination`, R12 `bisector_eqratio`, R74 `bisector_cyclic`, R46 `incentre_third_bisector`, R16 `tangent_converse`, R05 `same_arc_same_chord` (R04 and R14 are already the table's two converses; R09 is angle transitivity, which AR subsumes)
- [x] **The answer to the phase's own question is a negative: 2 goals of 385.** Every corpus goal that finished *quiescent and unproved* was re-run with the base table plus one candidate — quiescence is what makes that both cheap and exact, since forward chaining is confluent, so the re-run reaches the fixpoint a from-scratch wide run would. The corpus's 19 budget-exhausted goals are excluded rather than guessed at. Only **R30** moved anything real. R74's and R46's single unlocks are `testing_minimal_rules.txt:r74` and `:r46` — Newclid's one-problem-per-rule unit suite, each rule proving its own test case, which is not a corpus win and is not counted as one
- [x] **`bisector_eqratio` is the recurring shape for the third time: 30 facts on 26 problems, real output, zero goals moved.** Phase 152e's `eqangle`-ask measurement and Phase 170's R21 said this about `eqangle`; it is now said about `eqratio` too. This table's difficulty is not that it cannot state things
- [x] **`perp_from_inclination`** (R30): `eqangle(a,b,p,q,c,d,u,v) & perp(p,q,u,v) => perp(a,b,c,d)` — kept. `benchmark/corpus_bench.dart` before/after: **62 → 64 proved** of 466 built, **undecided unchanged at 19**, 0 unsound, 0 refuted. It reaches `examples.txt:two_goals_cong_aconst` and `large_examples.txt:regular_square`, both `cong` goals. Per-rule rig in `rule_engine_test.dart` (two quarter-turns, so both premises are structural rather than coordinate luck), table count 27 → 28
