- [x] **What R30 actually closes is a hole in the angle closure, and the more general fix measured worse — which is the phase's real finding.** All **24** of the facts it derives across 15 corpus problems are right angles at a *shared vertex*; none has four distinct points. `AngleTranslation.conclusions` skips every variable pair sharing a point, which is right for `para` (two lines through a point are the same line or they meet) and wrong for `perp`. Narrowing that guard to `para` was written, pinned, mutation-checked and measured as the alternative: **63 proved / 20 undecided**, against the rule's 64 / 19, *losing* `complete_001_6_GDD_FULL_01-20_16.gex` its existing proof to budget. Publishing every entailed shared-vertex `perp` is supply-side and floods any figure holding a pencil of lines through one point; the rule is demand-side. **Phase 165's rule — publish what has a consumer — deciding between a closure and a rule for the first time.** Reverted; the finding lives as a comment in `angle_translation.dart` and in the rule's own doc, per the `para_coll`/R50 disposal convention
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **3402** (3401 + the per-rule rig), browser gate **15**. Fixture suite unchanged, which confirms rather than assumes the zero-fixtures-effect claim

**Next.** The seven-candidate sweep is the last cheap rule-shaped lever on `eqangle`; what remains of the ~18-rule estimate needs either `sameclock` (the direct/reflected `simtri` split — R34–R37, R62–R67, eight of the nineteen `eqangle` consumers alone) or angle/length constants. Those two are now the benchmark's ordering, and the split is the larger and better-evidenced of them. `cyclic` stands at 1 of 43 and `coll` at 1 of 59 — both untouched by this phase, both consistent with its finding that the gap is not in what the vocabulary can state. `imo_102_requires_aux.txt` (44 built, 0 proved) is still the rig Phase 153's auxiliary search never had.

## Phase 172 — the direct/reflected split (closed: measured negative)

The benchmark's ordering, next item after Phase 171: the `simtri` split with `sameclock` — `rule.dart` names it as the blocker for `simtri`'s *angle* conclusions ("deriving eqangle from simtri would be unsound until the direct/reflected split exists"), PLAN names it as the larger of the two remaining levers, and eight of the nineteen `eqangle` consumers in Newclid are the direct/reflected pairs alone. Measured before the predicate, in the Phase 168/170/171 shape.

- [x] **The guard needs no predicate to measure, and that is the phase's cheap step.** Newclid's `sameclock` is a `NumericalPredicate` — checked against the diagram, never derived (`predicates/sameclock.py`) — and `rule.dart`'s own header already says a rule's non-degeneracy side conditions are carried by the numeric screen rather than by the pattern. So both arms of R52/R53 (three `eqangle` each, direct and reflected) were handed to the engine and the `DiagramFilter` left to keep whichever holds. That *is* the split, modulo the proof having to cite the guard — so reachability is measured exactly, not bounded
- [x] **The answer: 0 goals of 383.** Every corpus goal that finished *quiescent and unproved* was re-run from its own stored fixpoint with the six arms added (confluence again, the 19 budget-exhausted excluded). Nothing moved, and **no fact was credited to any of the six** — the wide re-run reached quiescence without adding one
- [x] **Checked whether the rules ever fire rather than reading idleness off the flat count** (Phase 170's correction). They fire on every one of the **40** `simtri`/`contri` facts those fixpoints hold. Of the 2 880 conclusion-instances that produces over the full 12-element orbit, **1 440 are already in the database and 1 440 are refused by the screen** — an exact halving, and the structural reason is the finding: the angle set a similarity would republish is the set `aa_simtri` matched in order to derive it. The reflected arm is the numerically false half, every time
- [x] **The control arm, pre-registered, also 0.** R52's remaining orientation-free `eqratio` (`simtri(a,b,c,d,e,f) => eqratio(b,c,a,c,e,f,d,f)`), which survives reflection and needs no guard, moves no goal and derives no new fact either. So `simtri`'s output set is saturated here, and what is missing is not orientation
- [x] **The supply is the real number: `simtri`/`contri` appear at all in 11 of those 383 problems.** The premise side cannot be the fix either — regula2's `aa_simtri`/`sas_simtri`/`sss_simtri` are orientation-*free*, so they fire strictly more often than Newclid's guarded R34/R36/R60 pairs; splitting them can only lower the count
- [x] **What the split would buy on the goal side is 5 goals of 928, and they are not proofs.** `simtrir` (3) and `contrir` (2) are the corpus goals this vocabulary cannot *state*; `sameclock` is 15 more, and since Newclid never derives one, answering a `sameclock` goal is the numeric screen replying, not the prover. All 20 are `unsupportedGoal` today, so none is in the 466 built
- [x] **Dropped on the `para_coll` / R50 / R21 precedent.** No `lib/` change beyond the measurement recorded in `rule.dart` next to the comment that had been carrying the split as a to-do since M-P1; the probe (`benchmark/_scratch_sameclock_probe.dart`) was the answer, not a deliverable, and is deleted
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite unchanged at **3402**

**Next.** Constants — angle (`aconst`, 12 corpus goals) and length (`lconst` 6, `rconst` 9) — are what is left of the benchmark's ordering, and Phase 165's length system already carries the note that `ln 2` enters as a variable the day a document wants a 1:2. Three of the four measured levers since Phase 167 have come back zero, and the two that moved anything (R01, R30) moved 18 goals between them; `cong` is 259 of 928 corpus goals and `coll` 131, so the honest next question may be what the 383 quiescent-unproved runs are *missing* rather than which rule to add next. `imo_102_requires_aux.txt` (44 built, 0 proved) is still the rig Phase 153's auxiliary search never had.

## Phase 173 — is the prover told what the problem says? (done)

Four rule-shaped levers measured since Phase 167, three of them zero. Every one asked *which rule is missing*. This asks the input-side question none of them did, and it is the first half of the "shape of the search" item Phase 172's Next named: **`hypotheses()` reads parent ties off a `Construction`; Newclid's `JGEXDefinition.clauses` states, per macro, the predicates that macro asserts.** Two independent routes to the same set, so the diff is a measurement of whether the runs were ever given what their problems state.

- [x] **The diff, over all 466 built problems: 4 739 predicates stated, 4 069 seeded, 549 not seeded but derived anyway, 0 refused by the screen, and **121 absent at the fixpoint on 45 problems** — 41 of which end *quiescent and unproved*.** So the input side was 97.4% right, and the 2.6% was not spread thin: three macros account for all of it, `incenter2` (64), `parallelogram` (56) and `circle` (1)
- [x] **Both real gaps are in `hypotheses()`, not in the corpus translation, and both are facts the construction genuinely guarantees** — which is that function's stated contract, so this is a defect and not a missing feature. A user drawing either configuration in the app had the same hole
  - **`TranslatedPoint` emitted one pair of the parallelogram's sides and not the other.** `t − point == vectorTo − vectorFrom` rearranges to `vectorFrom − point == vectorTo − t`; the four points are a parallelogram, and only the displacement pair was being stated. Plainly an oversight rather than a decision
  - **An `Incenter` said nothing about its touch points.** The incentre is equidistant from all three sides, so the feet of its perpendiculars are equidistant from *it* — the incircle, stated without the incircle being drawn. It reads the construction rather than `i`'s own parents, because a foot is a separate object per side; that is the cross-object shape `ReflectedPoint` already uses. Emitted as the chain `cong(i,f₀,i,f₁)`, `cong(i,f₁,i,f₂)`, which is the chain Newclid's `incenter2` states — the third pair follows
  - The `circle` 1 is a probe artifact, not a gap: `Circumcenter` emits a *spanning* pair of radii and leaves the third to follow, and the probe asked for the third on one problem where the run had not got to it. It is a problem that proves anyway
- [x] **Measured on the corpus: 64 → 64 proved, and the proved set is the same 64 problems** — diffed by name from two `--verbose` runs rather than inferred from the count, which is the check Phase 171 made a point of. `coll` 1/59, `cyclic` 1/43, `cong` 8/154 all unchanged
- [x] **The cost is 2 problems (4 goals) moving *unproved → undecided*, both `incenter2` problems** (`imo.txt:2024_p4`, `imo_sl.txt:imo_sl_2005_g6`): the new congruences give the length closure more to chew on and the budget runs out. Neither state is a proof and none was lost, so this is a budget question — Phase 161's open box — and not a coverage regression
- [x] **Kept, and the disposal rule is deliberately not the `para_coll`/R50/R21 one.** A rule is optional machinery and is dropped when it has no consumer. A hypothesis is *what the document says*: `hypotheses()`'s contract is every predicate the construction guarantees, the two emissions are guaranteed, and a prover not told its own figure's parallelogram is wrong independently of what the corpus scores
- [x] **The gap re-measured after the fix: 121 → 1**, i.e. 4 738 of 4 739 stated predicates now present. So the input side is closed as an explanation
- [x] Tests: three in `hypotheses_test.dart` — the parallelogram's both pairs, the incentre's touch-point chain, and a foot on a line that is *not* a side of the triangle saying nothing. Mutation-checked: dropping either emission fails exactly its own test, and widening the side guard from two vertices to one fails the negative. `extractAndPin` screens every emission through `DiagramFilter`, so soundness is asserted rather than assumed
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **3405** (3402 + 3), fixture suite unchanged

**Next — and this is now a much narrower question.** Both cheap explanations for the 379 quiescent-unproved runs are closed: it is not the rule table (four levers, three zero) and it is not the hypotheses (4 738 of 4 739 present). What is left is that the deductive closure of the stated hypotheses under this table genuinely does not contain those goals — which points at **auxiliary construction**, the one piece of machinery this repo has built (Phase 153) and never run at corpus scale. `imo_102_requires_aux.txt` is 44 built / 0 proved and is literally the rig; the cost model is the blocker, since Phase 153 measured a candidate as costing a *full re-run* (50 s on one document). That is its own phase, and it needs a budget before it needs code.

## Phase 174 — the ceiling for auxiliary construction (closed: measured negative)

Phase 173 closed both cheap explanations for the corpus's 379 quiescent-unproved runs — not the rule table, not the hypotheses — and named auxiliary construction as what was left. Before spending a phase on the cost model Phase 153's search needs, this asks the prior question: **would a perfect auxiliary search help?** The corpus states its own auxiliary points after a `|`, which is an answer key, and Phase 167 parses them and refuses to build them. Lifting that refusal is the ceiling measurement.

- [x] **Correcting Phase 173's own "Next": `imo_102_requires_aux.txt` states no auxiliary sections at all.** Its 44 problems *need* auxiliary points; the file does not say which, so it is not the rig that entry called it. Corpus-wide only **17** problems state one, 16 in `imo.txt` and 1 in `imo_sl.txt`
- [x] **`withAuxiliary` on `translateNewclidProblem`, off by default** — Phase 167's refusal stands as the benchmark's contract, and the flag exists for the one question where handing over the answer key is the point. Pinned in `newclid_problem_test.dart`: the point appears only with the flag, and it brings hypotheses with it
- [x] **The result: 0 of 17, and 0 of the 8 that build.** Nine of the seventeen do not build for translation reasons (4 `unknownMacro`, 3 `unsupportedGoal`, 1 `degenerate`, and those refusals are identical in both arms). Every one of the remaining eight is `unproved` before and `unproved` after
- [x] **Not because nothing happened — the mechanism fires, and that is what makes the zero mean something** (Phase 170's correction, applied a third time). Every one of the eight gains facts: `imo_sl_2015_g5_constr` **139 → 354**, `2019_p6` 119 → 180, `2009_p2` 38 → 70, the smallest gain 12. The answer key adds real deductive content — up to 2.5× the fixpoint — and closes nothing
- [x] **And it is not the budget.** Re-run at 10× (300 000 applications) all eight reach *quiescence*, including `2008_p1a`, which was the one undecided at the provider's own budget. A quiescent unproved run has exhausted its closure, so no budget rescues it
- [x] **So the upper bound is zero, and that is the useful shape of the finding.** A search that has to *find* the points is strictly weaker than being handed them, so whatever Phase 153's machinery would score on these eight, it is ≤ 0. The cost model is not worth solving for this
- [x] **Stated with its n.** Eight problems, all IMO, which is the corpus's hard tail rather than a sample of the 379. This is evidence that points are not what *these* runs lack; it is not evidence about the whole unproved set, and it cannot be, because the corpus does not record the answer key anywhere else
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **3406** (3405 + 1)

**Next.** Three explanations survive for the 379, and one measurement separates all three: **run the reference implementation over the same corpus and diff the proved sets.** If Newclid also fails on these, this prover is at parity and the limit is DDAR's method — which would make constants (`aconst` 12 goals, `rconst` 9, `lconst` 6, the last item in the benchmark's ordering) the only remaining lever worth its cost. If Newclid proves a large share, there is a concrete target list and the gap is in this implementation. That run needs a Python environment Newclid can import — its deps are not installed in the checkout — so it is an install decision to make before it is a measurement.

## Phase 175 — the reference comparator, set up and found unusable (closed: could not be measured)

Phase 174 named the one measurement that separates the three surviving explanations for the corpus's 379 quiescent-unproved goals: **run Newclid over the same corpus and diff the proved sets.** The environment is now built and the harness is in `benchmark/comparator/`. The comparison itself cannot be made, and why is the phase's result.

- [x] **The environment.** Newclid's deps are not installed in the checkout, and its `pyproject.toml` wants Python ≥ 3.11 while this machine's default is 3.14 — too new for the `scipy`/`symengine`/`matplotlib` wheels it pins. A 3.12 venv at `~/Code/var/Newclid/.venv` (already gitignored there) with `pip install ./newclid` works. **Nothing in that checkout was modified**
- [x] **The harness** (`benchmark/comparator/run_newclid.py` + `README.md`): one goal per problem, matching regula2's parser, so the proved sets are comparable goal by goal; auxiliary sections not built, matching both defaults; each problem in a **forked child with a hard timeout**, because Newclid blocks in C and an in-process `SIGALRM` does not bite — one problem overran a 60 s alarm by 280 s before that was changed. `corpus_bench.dart`'s verbose lines are now source-qualified, since problem names repeat across files and a bare name cannot be diffed
- [x] **Newclid 3.0.1 — the latest release, and the checkout's own version — does not run its own corpus.** Five distinct crash classes. **Three are unambiguous bugs and were fixed** in the venv's installed copy (never the checkout), each with the fix the authors plainly intended: `expected_lconsts.copy()` iterated as pairs when it is a `dict` read with `.items()` everywhere else; `Diff` constructed as a hardcoded pair when `Diff.points` is already `tuple[Point, ...]` with a pairwise check, and their own R34–R37 pass `diff A B C`; and a rule conclusion's "variables" including literals like R51's `1/2`, looked up in a mapping of point variables
- [x] **Two are not one-liners and were left alone**: `NotImplementedError: LengthEquationPredicate is not implemented`, its AR deductor producing a predicate its own handler lacks, and a pydantic `ValidationError` on `simtri c b d c b d`, its matcher generating a degenerate triangle pair its own validator rejects. Repairing those means rewriting the reference's reasoning, at which point it is not the reference
- [x] **The rate, on a 150-key random sample of the 466 built (seeded, so it is a sample and not the alphabetical prefix an earlier partial run was): 146 goals ran, and 16 gave a verdict — 11%.** 117 errors (55 `NotImplementedError`, 50 `ValidationError`, 10 `TypeError`, 2 `ValueError`) and 13 timeouts at 30 s. **The two dominant crash classes are exactly the machinery the comparison was for**, so the surviving 11% is biased toward what Newclid finds easy and a favourable result read off it would be inflated
- [x] **What the 16 say, with its n stated.** 6 proved by both, 2 unproved by both, 1 proved only by regula2 (`examples.txt:tangents_to_circle`), 7 proved only by Newclid. **Six of those seven are Newclid's own rule-unit problems** — five in `testing_minimal_rules.txt` and `examples.txt:rule_r29_not_found_explicit` — which is the exclusion Phase 171 already made for that file, and `r46` is literally the same problem it excluded then. **One genuine benchmark problem each way.** So on the surviving sample the two are at rough parity, and there is no evidence the reference reaches goals this prover cannot
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite unchanged at **3406**

**Next.** The decisive measurement is off the table without repairing someone else's prover, so the benchmark's own ordering resumes at its last item: **constants** — `aconst` 12 corpus goals, `rconst` 9, `lconst` 6, and Phase 165's length system already carries the note that `ln 2` enters as a *variable* the day a document wants a 1:2. It is the last untried lever, and after four measured levers and two closed explanations it should be measured the same way: what does it move, before it is built. Worth noting against it that 27 of the corpus's constant goals is a small prize, and that `cong` (259 goals) and `coll` (131) remain where the mass is.

## Phase 177 — constants, measured before built (closed: +3 from a respelling; the rest priced and declined)

The benchmark's ordering ends here: `aconst` / `rconst` / `lconst`, the last untried lever, measured the way the four levers before it were. Two facts framed the measurement before it ran. Newclid's own rule table touches constants in exactly **one** rule (R51, midpoint ⇒ `rconst 1/2`) — its constants come from construction macros (`s_angle`, `lconst`, `rconst`, `rconst2`, `l2const`, `triangle12`) and from AR, not from DD — and this repo's translator supports **none of those macros**. And the two AR systems are not symmetric: `AngleEquation` already carries a rational constant in units of π (it is how `perp` enters as ½), while `length_closure.dart` deliberately has no constant term and says so at its head.

- [x] **Static classification of every constant goal in the corpus** (two throwaway probes, Phase 168 pattern, deleted after): **31 constant goals** — `aconst` 16, `rconst` 9, `lconst` 6 (STATUS's remembered "27" was an undercount). The decisive split, from running the real translator on each: **9 `unsupportedGoal`** (figure builds, goal is the sole blocker; 8 outside `testing_minimal_rules.txt`) and **22 `unknownMacro`** — gated by `s_angle` (13), `lconst` (6) and kin as *constructions*, so for two-thirds of the prize the goal vocabulary is not the frontier. Every `lconst` goal is in the gated set: zero sole-blockers
- [x] **Supply side**: the constant-stating macros alone gate **44 of the corpus's 928 problems**, 23 of which have goals already in the ten-predicate vocabulary. The input side is the bigger half of this lever, and it echoes Phase 173: constants in this corpus are mostly something the *figure states*, not something DD derives — Newclid agrees, having only R51
- [x] **Reachability, measured at the fixpoint rather than sketched.** Of the 8 real sole-blockers: **three respell as existing predicates** — `aconst … 1pi/2` *is* `perp`, `rconst … 1/1` *is* `cong` — and all three **prove today** when respelled. Two more (`angles_eq_triangle`, `angles_double_eq_triangle`) are pinned by the angle closure **up to a threefold root choice**: `3·(θ_cd − θ_ab) ≡ 3k` is entailed at the fixpoint while the unscaled equation is not — mod-1 arithmetic cannot divide by 3; an `aconst` reader would need the division plus a numeric screen to pick the root, the same shape as the existing eqangle read. `regular_hexagon#2` is out of reach for non-constant reasons (its `cong` sibling goal is also unproved). The two genuine `rconst` goals are **inexpressible**: no constant term in the length system (Phase 165's `ln 2` note, confirmed at the head of `length_closure.dart`)
- [x] **The respelling, built** — the one honest cheap piece, and it is translation fidelity rather than machinery: refusing a goal the vocabulary already states misreports the corpus. `_respelled` in `newclid_translation.dart` (π/2 in `Npi/D` or degree form → `perp`; ratio 1 → `cong`; genuinely constant values still refuse), four tests in `newclid_problem_test.dart`. Corpus: **466 → 469 built, 64 → 67 proved** — the three named problems exactly, 0 unsound, unproved/undecided unchanged at 379/23
- [x] **Verdict on the rest, priced and declined.** An `aconst` vocabulary (value-carrying predicate, question template, root-screening reader) buys **+2** corpus goals; the length-constant dimension buys **+2** (`rconst 1/2`, `2/1`); the construction macros unlock 44 problems but with unknown proof yield and require the vocabulary work first to state their hypotheses. Against `cong` 259 / `coll` 131 unproved, none of these is the next phase. The benchmark's ordering is now **exhausted**: every lever it named has been measured, and the standing answer is that the table and inputs are right — the 379 quiescent-unproved runs are beyond this DD+AR closure's reach, which is the same wall Phase 174 measured from the auxiliary side and Phase 175 could not compare against
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **3410** (3406 + 4)

Every lever in the benchmark's ordering is measured, and the 379 quiescent-unproved are beyond the closure's reach. The **23 undecided were the one pocket that claim did not cover**: they ran out of budget, not out of closure, so "more of the same" was still an untested explanation there — Phase 174 had converted exactly one undecided to quiescent at 10× (and it went quiescent-*unproved*). One probe pass, Phase 168 pattern, probe deleted.

- [x] **The shape of the zero is the finding**: 20 of 23 quiesce within 33–41 k applications — barely past the 30 k line, not deep in an explosion; only `complete_016…M021-64.gex` (210 k) and `1995_p1` (133 k, both copies identically) needed real headroom. Duplicated problems across files land on identical apps/facts counts, which is the determinism behaving
- [x] **So the claim extends corpus-wide with no budget caveat**: every buildable, unproved corpus problem exhausts its deductive closure under this table — 402 of 402 measured quiescent. Budget is not a lever on this corpus, which also answers the "raise the budget" arm of Phase 161's decision box for the corpus's part: charging or yielding remains a *latency* question, not a proving-power one

## Phase 179 — R49 cong-only: the rule reverted, and the kernel bug it flushed out fixed (closed: +2 proved)

Phase 169's "Next" left one loose end the fixpoint-composition argument does not cover: **R49 in cong-only form was dismissed as subsumed by R50's negative result rather than measured on its own** — and R50 was dropped on *cost* (+1 proved, +3 undecided), not on a fixpoint zero, so nothing proved R49 shared its fate. The form: `cong(o,a,o,b) & cong(o,b,o,c) & cyclic(a,b,c,d) => cong(o,a,o,d)` — full-evidence centre recognition, R01's converse direction; no `ncoll` guard needed since `cyclic` already means a genuine circle.

- [x] **The first bench run flagged an UNSOUND proof, and chasing it found a real kernel defect — in the verifier's favourite blind spot.** `regular_hexagon`'s proof carried an `angle_arithmetic` step whose recorded premises "did not entail" its conclusion. Dissection showed the opposite: the certificate was *valid* (`recombine == query`, hand-checked — it scales one perp by 4 and another by 2, wiping their halves mod 1), and the false no came from `AngleClosure.add`'s Bézout branch, which continued elimination with a determinant-|y| combination of the two rows it merged. **Non-unimodular, so the surviving basis generates a sublattice**: later `entails` calls miss combinations that exist over the inputs — the verifier reports sound steps unsound, and the live engine under-proves. Present since the closure was written; R49 merely produced the first proof whose verification walked the lossy path
- [x] **The fix is the classic complement** — continue with `(theirs/g)·current − (mine/g)·old`, determinant −1 — plus a regression test distilled to the exact seven equations in citation order, confirmed failing before the fix and passing after. The greedy triangular membership test is exact once the basis is lattice-faithful (unique pivots force the combination), so this is completeness restored, not a heuristic patched
- [x] **The fix alone: 67 → 69 proved** (`regular_hexagon` — the goal Phase 177 measured as out of reach, reachable all along — and a JGEX book problem), no losses, undecided unchanged at 23, 0 unsound, full name-diff. Suite **3411** (3410 + 1), no pinned fixture moved
- [x] **R49 on the fixed engine, measured clean: R50's trade restated.** +1 genuine proved (`point_on_circle_eqdistant_from_center`, the rule's own statement posed as a problem; the unit-file `r49` excluded per standing policy) for **+3 undecided** (`imo_sl_2022_g7` twice, `imo_sl_2010_g2b_constr`) — the same expensive `cyclic`-premise join R50 paid. **Reverted on the R50 protocol**, measurement recorded in the rule table's doc comment beside R50's and R21's. Its one other apparent gain on the broken engine, `regular_hexagon`, belonged to the closure fix
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite 3411, corpus baseline now **469 built / 69 proved / 377 unproved / 23 undecided / 0 unsound**

## Phase 180 — the constants stack, designed before built (closed: PLAN §, decomposition)

The user chose the constants stack as the prover's next direction (session 180). PLAN §"The constants stack — vocabulary as payload, algebra as reader" records the design: value-carrying fact kinds no rule matches; the ℤ-module line held against root-screening (eq_triangle's goals stay named incompletenesses); the supply theorem that forces macros-before-ask on the angle side; the prime-log constant column reversing Phase 165's omission at the cost it predicted; macros as real construction objects per the `on_aline` line. Phases 181–183 decomposed there.

- [x] PLAN section written, argued against the pinned sections it touches (ℤ-module, Phase 165's length decisions, the benchmark's `on_aline` ruling, §"A shape is not an angle")

## Phase 181 — the length system's constant column (closed: +4 proved, and a translator defect flushed)

First implementation phase of the constants stack (PLAN §"The constants stack"). Corpus baseline moved **469/69/377/23/0 → 483/73/387/23/0**.

- [x] **`LengthEquation` gained its constant column** as a formal ℚ-vector over `{ln p}` (`Map<BigInt, Rational>`, prime → exponent) — ℚ-row-scaling halves `ln 2` into `{2: ½}` exactly, where a rational number has nowhere to put `ln √2`. `isContradictory` (unique factorization makes `{ln p}` ℚ-independent, so `0 = k` is genuine), `LengthAddOutcome.contradiction`, the latched `isInconsistent` flag; entailment stays conservative when inconsistent, `AngleClosure`'s arrangement. The "cannot be inconsistent" and `ln 2` sentences are out of the headers; `renderLengthEquation` exponentiates the constant to a leading number per side (`|AB| = 2·|MA|`). Evaluator sweep extended componentwise over formal assignments
- [x] **The `rconst(a,b,c,d; q)` and `lconst(a,b; q)` fact kinds**, value part of the canonical identity — `rconst`'s pair swap inverts the ratio (same-segment ties broken by the lesser value); `lconst` sorts its pair. Matched by no rule; `orbitArguments` lists the value-preserving forms only, documented. Absorption and `equationOf`/`entailmentOf` in `LengthTranslation`; `ratioIs`/`lengthIs` numeric checks (`lconst` pinned as the vocabulary's one non-scale-invariant statement); the builder's every-kind-has-a-template invariant narrowed to point-pure kinds with the reason stated
- [x] **`Midpoint`, the exact-half `SegmentRatioPoint` and `CentralReflectionPoint` emit `rconst(p₁,m,p₁,p₂; ½)`** beside their `midp` — one half suffices, the other follows from the cong in the closure. The corpus's `midpoint` macro builds a real `Midpoint`, so the emission reaches it with no translator change
- [x] **Goals phrase instead of refusing**: `rconst`/`lconst` goals parse their value and become one value-carrying spelling over the corpus's own points, resolved through the length closure like any ask. `_respelled` keeps its precedence — a value that collapses into `cong` keeps the plainer spelling
- [x] **Measured, with the name diff**: +4 proved, no losses — `midpoint_splits_in_two`, `r51`, and `r29` in both spellings (Newclid's R29 midpoint-is-an-eqratio falls out of two rconst-½ rows meeting in the closure: a rule bought by AR, not DD). `obm_1fase_2006_p15` flushed a translator defect instead: its goal holds only when the two eqdistance apexes take consistent orientations, and the fixed branch-0 policy made the goal-true instance unreachable — **`_cross` now draws one surviving branch with the attempt's generator, and `goalFalseInFigure` dropped 11 → 0** (all eleven were fixed-branch artifacts; all build now, unproved). The proved set is bitwise identical with and without the branch change; two marginal figures flap to `unstableFigure` on the shifted sample stream
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **3446**, corpus baseline **483 built / 73 proved / 387 unproved / 23 undecided / 0 unsound**, fixtures untouched, every proof verified

## Phase 182 — the constant-stating macros + `aconst`, landed together (closed: +42 built, +19 proved, 0 lost)

Second implementation phase of the constants stack (PLAN §"The constants stack — vocabulary as payload, algebra as reader"). Supply landed with demand, per the supply theorem. Corpus baseline moved **483/73/387/23/0 → 525/92/409/24/0**.

- [x] **The `aconst(l₁,l₂; r)` fact kind and ask** — value a residue mod 1 in units of π (Newclid's convention `θ_cd − θ_ab ≡ r`, checked against its `constant_angle.py`); canonicalization value-coupled like `rconst`'s (`_canonicalValueCoupled`: the pair swap negates mod 1, ties broken by the lesser value, 0 and ½ its fixed points); absorption is one row into `AngleTranslation` whose constant is the stated value, and the existing `resolve()` ask needed **no change** — `equationOf` learning the kind was the whole hookup. Unscaled entailment only; the eq_triangle threefold-root goals stay named incompletenesses (ℤ-module line). The half *is* `perp` inside the algebra (pinned), and the translator respells ½ → `perp`, 0 → `para`, keeping `_respelled` precedence
- [x] **`Rational` moved to `lib/domain/math/`** — construction objects carry exact stated values, and construction must not depend on the prover built on it. Pure move, zero imports of its own
- [x] **Four carrier kinds cover the seven macros**: `FixedAngleLine` (a stated rational turn of π from a reference through a point — `s_angle` and the `aconst` macro; extends `RelativeLine`, with a `coincidentCarriers` case ahead of the generic one so different turns never merge), `StatedRadiusCircle` (`lconst`/`l2const` — separate from `FixedRadiusCircle`, whose float parameter cannot honestly *state* a value), `ScaledCompassCircle` (`rconst`, `triangle12`), `RatioApolloniusCircle` (`rconst2`; ratio 1 refused — that locus is the perpendicular bisector). All Euclidean-only (the `SegmentRatioPoint` arrangement). Codec params ride as canonical `n/d` strings (a JSON number would round the value the kind exists to state); additive kinds, so still v1 documents — a v1 reader refuses an unknown type outright, the `FivePointConic` precedent, so no version bump
- [x] **`hypotheses()` states the constants**: `FixedAngleLine` emits its `aconst` through witness pairs on both carriers (the `ParallelLine` shape with the value carried; `extractAndPin` doubles as the sign pin), the circles their `lconst`/`rconst` per named point; the two centred kinds join `_structuralCenter` so rim pairs still become `cong`
- [x] **The seven macros translate**, constraint-shaped so they compose with any other constraint as an intersection. Value-1 collapses route to the plainer carriers (`rconst 1/1` → compass circle, `rconst2 1/1` → perpendicular bisector, sharing those macros' dedup keys); `l2const` takes an exact rational square root and refuses an irrational one (the corpus's one use is `l2const b a 4`); `triangle12` is the `iso_triangle` shape with the third point at twice the base
- [x] **Measured, with the name diff**: 45 problems left `unknownMacro` (209 → 164; the Phase 177 static count said 44). **42 build** — 19 prove, 23 quiesce unproved — and **3 are `goalFalseInFigure`**: `obm_1fase_2001_p2`/`p9`/`2008_p16` state `aconst` goals that are **numerically false under Newclid's own implemented semantics** (verified by hand-computing its `angle_between_4_points` on `p16`'s branch-free figure: 130° against a stated 50° — a chirality transcription defect in the corpus, since its own `rotatea` is counterclockwise like ours). The 19 include `worlds_hardest_easy_geometry_problem1_with_construction` (proved at 0 apps — pure AR entailment of the stated angles) and `1995_p1#2`. **0 lost, 0 unsound, unstable set identical (5)**; `obm_1fase_2006_p15` (the named re-check) stays quiescent-unproved as predicted — its 60° lives in an `eq_triangle`, which states congs, and the threefold root stays refused. `regular_hexagon#2` drifted unproved → undecided (scheduling perturbation from the enum growing; it crossed the 30 000 line without quiescing)

Phase 180's decomposition ends here: the measurement is in (Phase 182: **+45 problems out of `unknownMacro`, 42 built, +19 proved, 0 lost, 3 exposed as corpus defects**), and this phase decides what it means for what comes next. Candidates on the table from the session-180 report, updated by the number: the app-facing `aconst` reader/tools for the four new carrier kinds (the prover can now *state* a fixed angle but no user can draw one); the Phase 176 trigger (check `pip index versions newclid` each session — still 3.0.1 as of 2026-09-02); or the corpus number is a plateau at 92/525 and the kernel/UI track resumes.

## Phase 184 — the constant tools: the app draws what the prover reads (closed)

The drawing half of the verdict (PLAN §"The constants stack's verdict"). Four tools, exact-rational dialogs, chords, kind labels; no prover change.

- [x] **`Rational.tryParse`** — integers, decimals (exact over a power of ten) and integer fractions; everything else null. The dialogs' "garbage reads as cancel" convention, with `sqrt(2)` and `pi` deliberately on the garbage side: what comes out is stated in a hypothesis
- [x] **Four tools**: `FixedAngleLineTool` (a `PointAndLineTool` subclass, refusing non-Euclidean input in `onInput` since the base class has no `availableUnder`), `StatedRadiusCircleTool`, `ScaledCompassCircleTool` (span, span, centre — the compass order; factor 1 builds `CompassCircle`), `RatioApolloniusCircleTool` (A then B; ratio 1 builds `PerpendicularBisectorLine`). All check their value's contract when picked, not on the tap
- [x] **Dialogs and chords**: `_RationalDialog` behind four asks — stated angle in degrees stored as the residue (`(deg/180).modOne()`), radius/scale/ratio positive. `G ⇧ D`, `G ⇧ R`, `G ⇧ O`, `G ⇧ Q`; rows in the Lines and Circles flyouts beside their float twins; `main.dart` dispatch through one `_activateAfterAsking`
- [x] **Euclidean-only rows** — `euclideanOnlyConstants` beside `euclideanOnlyMacros`, passed to the Lines and Circles groups, cross-checked against the tools' refusals in `toolbar_test`
- [x] **Kind labels carry the exact value**: `Line at 60°` / `Line at 45/2°`, `Circle of radius 5/2`, `Compass circle × 3/2`, `Apollonius circle, ratio 2`
- [x] Gates: analyze clean, suite **3517** (3489 + 28), corpus untouched, fixtures untouched

The other half of the verdict (PLAN §"The reader: a value is read off the closure and then proved"). Phase 184 made the four carrier kinds drawable; this phase made their statements askable and readable.

- [x] **The reader on both closures** — `AngleClosure.constantOf` and `LengthClosure.constantOf`, the `entails` reduction returning the residual constant instead of testing it (angle side negated, length side as it stands: the two rows keep their constant on different sides); `LengthEquation.antilog` back to a rational only where every exponent is whole. The ℤ-module line held: `2Δθ ≡ 0` reads no `Δθ`. `readAngle`/`readRatio`/`readLength` on the translations register nothing; `Prover.readConstant` dispatches by kind
- [x] **Value-carrying templates**: `aconst` (from line, to line), `rconst` (two segments), `lconst` (one segment) in `QuestionTemplate`; the value on `QuestionDraft.value` beside the slots (`withValue`, `needsValue`, `constantSpellings`); the template test's exclusion flipped to its positive. Out-of-range values are "no question", not a throw
- [x] **The reader wired as a fill**: `ProverNotifier.read` runs to the fixpoint (reusing a held run), tries the spellings in order, leaves the run held; the bar's value field takes degrees for an angle and hands the draft the residue, *Read* fills it, an undetermined read says so, and the ordinary *Ask* proves the value with a certificate
